| 13–14 | 3.5 | depcheck and the dependency report (ranking, Go vendored check, typed count bound into `taint.clear`); the purge inside `taint.clear`; the attach dependency gate. A-G16–A-G18. `taint.clear` doesn't exist before these weeks, so the purge never lags it; until then attach is gated by control paths only, as in v5.2. |
| 15–16 | (benchmark) | A-G19. |
| **Total** | **~11** | |

### 3.7 Residual risks
1. **No ask change.** 182.7 → 182.7 from the cache alone (A-G19). Go tasks taint on the cache exactly as on the CONNECT. A clean path for lockfile-pinned files is a separate, later proposal: it needs workspace stickiness fixed first, plus fills tied to the caller's allowlist, one status for hit and filled, an epoch-fenced INTENT, a 3-day release-age cooldown and the dependency report.
2. **Go only, downloads only.** Compile time stays (`GOCACHE` is per task; a shared build cache needs a trusted builder). npm, PyPI and crates stay cold; npm and Python trees already persist in the workspace, and Cargo is not in the image.
3. **More code and state.** The proxy gains an HTTP GET path, a TLS client, zip hashing, a checksum-database client, a SQLite index and a writable store; it still holds no secrets. taskrun gains an HTTP server and a TLS client. depcheck parses attacker-written files, sandboxed with no network. A compromised proxy can deny or slow Go fetches, fill each store to its limit, and see which public module versions each agent fetches.
4. **Turning checks off.** A task that sets `GOSUMDB=off` or `GONOSUMDB`, or skips the filter, trusts the proxy's bytes for files not in go.sum. With the filter kept, the gate record taints it; skipping the filter already needs a prior injection. Accepted as a bounded residual (R41; section 3.4, row 11).
5. **Deputy hosts.** An agent with `proxy.golang.org` or `sum.golang.org` allowlisted can still send data out in module paths, tainted or not, as in v5.2. v5.3 makes that a human-only, flagged decision. v2: a private module proxy as the upstream.
6. **Presence channel.** Within one agent, tainted tasks can learn which public versions the store holds, one bit per probe, until the next clear. The store's size shows in the agent's disk usage as a number. Neither carries text or code.
7. **Purge cost.** The first Go task after each clear is cold, and the proxy's checksum consistency anchor resets at each clear (go itself keeps no anchor across tasks either).
8. **Per-agent copies.** No cross-agent dedupe: each agent pays its own first download and uses up to 8 GiB of its `1/N`.
9. **`GOTOOLCHAIN=local`.** A repo whose `go.mod` needs a newer Go fails until the image's mise pin moves, or until the agent sets `GOTOOLCHAIN=auto`, and that download taints.
10. **Dependency review limits.** npm and Python installed code can't be checked against an upstream hash, so every changed `node_modules/` or `.venv/` package is high-risk at a clear, and so is vendored code of a changed Go pin. JS-heavy owners may pass `--ack-dependency-changes` by habit; it is separate from `--ack-control-changes`, so the control-path signal survives that habit. v2: offline checks of stored checksum-database proofs for changed Go pins.
11. **Live-check cost and one assumption.** About 0.5–2 s per task for 60 modules. It assumes a withdrawn version answers 404 or 410 on `.info`; A-G3 checks it, and the cache ships off if it fails.
12. **GOPRIVATE** is only as good as the admin's patterns. An unlisted private path still reaches sum.golang.org on lookups, as with go's default.

## 4. Proposal B: workspace-bound memory notes

### 4.1 Problem
- Nearly every task is tainted in steady state (section 2.1). In v5.2 every tainted `memory.write` is quarantined: not indexed, and unreadable by any task until a human releases it with `memory review` (§8). At 0.3 notes per task that is about 30 reviews per 100 tasks, and until a review the note is useless. So agents end up with no working memory across tasks.
- Two v5.2 spec bugs sit in the same area:
  1. §5.2 says epochs move only on clean → tainted and on clear. §5.3 says `workspace.restore` sets the workspace taint "as a new epoch". The two disagree.
  2. §5.3 acks a snapshot's INTENT if the workspace epoch is unchanged, and that check runs before the btrfs call. A holder tainted between the ACK and the snapshot leaves tainted bytes in a snapshot recorded clean.

### 4.2 Design
**Rule.** What a tainted task may do without a human depends only on where it writes, never on what it read. Registry, docs and web reads taint exactly as §6.2. There is one exemption: while the writer's workspace subject W is tainted at epoch e, own-scope `memory.write` and `memory.forget` by an automated task run without a human, as **workspace-bound** facts tied to (W, e).

| Piece | Rule |
|---|---|
| Who | Only an automated task (`writers.holder` is `task:<id>`). An `agent.attach` session (`session:<id>`) never gets it: its tainted own-scope writes stay quarantined, because session-home holds subscription tokens. The holder kind comes from auditd, never from the caller. |
| Where | Only own scope `agent/<self>`. Shared scopes and `procedural` are unchanged. |
| When | Only while W is tainted at e, while fewer than `memory.bound_max_per_epoch` (50) active plus forgotten facts are bound to (W, e), and while `now − taint.since(W)` is at most `memory.bound_stale_ms` (7 days). Otherwise: quarantine, as in v5.2. |
| Write | INTENT{writer, taintEpochs {W: e}, title and body hashes}. auditd ACKs only if W is still tainted at e and `writers` maps the task to W. The fact commits `active`, `taint=1`, `ws_subject=W`, `ws_epoch=e`, indexed only in `memory_fts_ws`. Reply: `{id, state: active, bound: true}`. |
| Read | Only to a reader that holds W while W is tainted at `ws_epoch`. That reader is already tainted, so no READ-INTENT is needed. Bound facts are matched in `memory_fts_ws`, newest first, never bm25-ranked and never mixed into `memory_fts`. A clean reader skips rows that would need a READ-INTENT unless it passes `raise: true`. |
| Forget | A task executor may forget its own current-epoch bound fact without a signature. The row stays (`state='forgotten'`, `ws_*` kept to the epoch's end); its `memory_fts_ws` row goes; `taint review` and `memory review --bound` still list it by hash. |
| End | Any epoch move ends the binding: `taint.clear`, or a signed `workspace.restore`. The facts fail the read predicate at once, and agentboxd quarantines them. Epochs never repeat. Release is a separate, optional, paginated `memory review` later; there is no bulk accept, and a released fact keeps `taint=1`. |
| Visibility | `agentbox status` shows each tainted workspace's bound-fact count and epoch age. Every 6 h, if there are new bound facts, one ntfy digest per agent lists them by count and short hashes only: no body, title or label. `agentbox memory review --bound` lists the current epoch any time. |
| Agent view | `ask.status` (section 6) returns `taint = {tainted, workspaceBound}`, the one agent-facing taint shape (R10). There is no separate taint op (R11). The reply never carries causes, `since` or source classes, so it can't reveal that a human attached or what a sibling read. |
| Cedar | `Ctx` gains `workspaceBound`, set by agentboxd only. `tainted-ask` gets `&& !context.workspaceBound`. A new forbid makes the flag inert on every other action. rootd's floor never reads it. |
| Spec fixes | A signed `workspace.restore` moves W's epoch, so §5.2 agrees with §5.3, and it quarantines earlier-epoch facts. Snapshot taint is raised at RESULT, from the workspace row read after the btrfs call. rootd runs `workspace.snapshot`, `workspace.restore` and `taint.clear` one at a time per workspace. |

**Why this opens no new path.**
- A bound fact moves no byte off the box and is never exported to instructions.
- Its only readers are tasks of the same agent that hold W at e, which are already tainted, plus humans. Every exfil-capable op from those tasks still needs a signature (B-G8).
- It is never stronger than an auto-loading `CLAUDE.md`, `AGENTS.md` or build script that a tainted task can already plant in the same workspace.
- Attach sessions are excluded, so no credential path opens (§0.5 holds).
- **§0.6:** a bound fact has the same readers, the same taint on every reader and the same end as a file in the same tainted workspace, and it also ends on a signed restore. §0.1 and §0.7 hold: code computes the flag, and no taint is lowered.

**Not adopted.** Two judged designs gave registry and docs reads a lower taint level. Both let attacker-controlled text commit to own memory with no review and survive `taint.clear`, a new injection-to-persistence path (judge safety scores 2 and 3 of 10). From them v5.3 keeps only parts that lower no taint: the clean-reader `raise: true` filter, the snapshot-taint-at-RESULT fix, and a golden diff over every op. A GET-only package broker stays a v2 path for the egress residual.

### 4.3 Spec changes (ready to paste)
Shared spots: M1 (§0.6), M5 (§5.4), M8 (§7), M10 (§9), M11 (§10), M12 (§11), M15 (App. D), M16 (App. E).

1. **§4.2 Interactive sessions.** Add a bullet: "A `memory.write` or `memory.forget` from an `agent.attach` session is never workspace-bound. It follows the v5.2 quarantine path (§8), because session-home holds subscription credentials." Replace the post-attach scan sentence "After attach ends, and before the next task may start, rootd scans the workspace diff since attach start for the session-home OAuth access and refresh token strings." with "After attach ends, and before the next task may start, rootd scans the workspace diff since attach start, and the bodies of facts the session wrote to `agent/<self>` during the attach (agentboxd returns them over a read-only call), for the session-home OAuth access and refresh token strings."
2. **§4.3 Credentials**, Subscription OAuth row, append: "A session can't persist this token into memory as a readable fact: session `memory.write` is quarantined (§8), so no later task reads it, and a human sees it at review."
3. **§5.1 Operation registry.** After the `taintsOutput` flag, add "the **`workspaceBindable` flag** (§8)". Add:
   ```markdown
   - **Workspace-bindable check.** An op may set `workspaceBindable` only if its rollback coverage is `logical`, it has no remote effect, agentboxd executes it, its target is read only by the executor's own tasks and humans, its effect can be bound to W's epoch, and the writer is an automated task executor (a `task:<id>` holder, never a `session:<id>`). A golden test pins the set to exactly {`memory.write`, `memory.forget`} on `agent/<executor>`. agentboxd and rootd refuse to start on a mismatch.
   ```
   Golden set, append: "A tainted own-scope `memory.write` by a child task with W tainted gets Run (bound); the same write by an attach session gets quarantine (0 bound rows). With `tainted=true`, every op's floor and Cedar outcome is the same for `workspaceBound` true and false, except own-scope `memory.write` and `memory.forget` by a task executor."
4. **§5.2 Taint fence.** Replace "**Epochs move only on clean → tainted, and on clear:**" with "**Epochs move on clean → tainted, on clear, and on a signed `workspace.restore` (§5.3):**". After the sentence that ends "and records `cleared_by`.", add: "A signed `workspace.restore` sets the workspace to max(current, snapshot) and adds 1 to its epoch. auditd applies that move only when it arrives on rootd's socket as the INTENT of a signed restore, the same rule as a clear (§9). Taint rows are never deleted, so a subject's epochs never repeat, and an ended binding never matches again." Compromise bound, append to "It also controls …": "It also computes `workspaceBound` and the §8 read predicate; this adds nothing it doesn't already control."
5. **§5.3 Ops.**
   - `memory.read` row, Floor, append: "a clean reader skips rows that need a READ-INTENT unless it passes `raise: true`; the reply never says whether rows were skipped; workspace-bound facts follow §8".
   - Replace the `memory.write` row with:
     ```markdown
     | `memory.write` | agentboxd | Own scope `agent/<self>`, untainted: trusted write (§8). Own scope while W is tainted, by an automated task executor, under the per-epoch cap and the staleness bound: Run as a workspace-bound write (`active`, `taint=1`, bound to W's epoch, §8). Attach sessions, shared scopes, a tainted own-scope write while W is clean, over the cap, or stale: Cedar Ask carried out as quarantine, released by `memory review` (§8). | body hash | logical |
     ```
   - `memory.forget` row, Floor, replace with: "Run on own `agent/<self>` when untainted, or when the fact is bound to W's current epoch and the caller is a task executor (the row is kept as `forgotten` and listed in `taint review`, §7, §8); otherwise Ask; Cedar Ask on shared scopes; never `procedural`. `factId` resolves only to facts the caller could read now (§5.2)."
   - Taint rule, append: "Workspace-bound ops are exempt (§5.1): own-scope `memory.write` and `memory.forget` by an automated task executor while W is tainted. Their effect is bound to W's epoch and ends at the next epoch move unless a human releases it (§8). An attach session never gets this exemption. It depends only on where the op writes; reads taint exactly as §6.2. rootd never reads the flag, and no rootd op is bindable."
   - Snapshots carry taint: after "The snapshot's INTENT is ACKed only if the workspace epoch is unchanged.", add: "After the btrfs call returns, rootd raises `snap:<id>` to the workspace taint read at that moment, in the RESULT. That is an upper bound: taint only rises while a holder is live, `taint.clear` refuses while one runs, and rootd runs `workspace.snapshot`, `workspace.restore` and `taint.clear` one at a time per workspace." After the restore bullets, add: "A signed `workspace.restore` sets W to a new epoch (§5.2). Every memory fact bound to an earlier epoch then fails the §8 read predicate, and agentboxd quarantines it as a step of the restore, even though `memory.db` is not inside the restored subvolume."
6. **§6.2:** the taint-classes sentence in A's item 12 ("There is no lower taint tier …") covers B.
7. **§8 Shared memory.** Replace the **Write**, **Index**, **Read** and **Deletion** bullets with the text below, and add **End of binding** and **Visibility** after **Read**. **Taint and review are separate fields**, **Exports** (plus M9) and **Embeddings** stay.
   ````markdown
   - **Write:**
     - A fact's taint is the max of its sources' taint.
     - Trusted writes to `agent/<self>` commit directly only if auditd ACKs INTENT{writer, epoch} with the taint epoch agentboxd evaluated still unchanged. Otherwise the write is quarantined.
     - **Workspace-bound writes.** A tainted own-scope write commits `active` and bound only if: the writer is an automated task (`writers.holder` is `task:<id>`); W is tainted at epoch e; fewer than `memory.bound_max_per_epoch` (50) active plus forgotten facts are bound to (W, e); and `now − taint.since(W)` is at most `memory.bound_stale_ms` (7 days). agentboxd sends INTENT{writer, taintEpochs {W: e}, title and body hashes}. auditd ACKs only if W is still tainted at e and `writers` maps the task to W. On ACK the fact commits with `taint=1`, `ws_subject=W` and `ws_epoch=e`, is indexed only in `memory_fts_ws`, and the reply is `{id, state: active, bound: true}`.
     - Every other tainted write is quarantined: attach sessions, shared scopes, a tainted own-scope write while W is clean (ancestor-only taint), over the cap, past the staleness bound, or with no ACK. Shared-scope writes are quarantined even when clean. Cross-agent summaries are shared-scope writes, so they are quarantined too. decide labels are shown only as untrusted hints (§5.5).
     - Cedar returns Ask for these writes (`tainted-ask`, `risk-ask`, `budget-ask`, or no own-scope permit). agentboxd never turns this Ask into a live prompt. It stores a quarantined row and returns `MEMORY_QUARANTINED` (§5.6). The row is not indexed, and no task can read it.
     - A human releases a quarantined row with `agentbox memory review`, the CLI front end for `memory.review` (signed over fact ID and body hash; `memory.write` is `reversible`, so a phone approval works too, §5.4). At release, Cedar runs again with the writing agent as principal and `approved=true`, so `memory-procedural-never` still denies procedural writes. Release sets `ws_*` to NULL, keeps `taint=1`, and indexes the fact in `memory_fts`.
     - Quarantined writes are reviews, not asks.
     - Rate limit: 20 per task. Per-agent caps on memory rows and body bytes, bus messages and artifacts (`control/budgets.toml`). Over a cap, the write fails with `CAP_REACHED{what, limit}` (§5.6).
   - **Index:** `memory_fts` holds active facts without a binding, and released facts. `memory_fts_ws` holds active workspace-bound facts only. Quarantined facts are visible only through `memory review`.
   - **Read:** checked by Cedar per scope, returned as JSON with provenance. Every read joins `facts` with `f.state='active' AND (f.expires_at IS NULL OR f.expires_at > :now_ms) AND (f.ws_epoch IS NULL OR (f.ws_subject = :ws AND :ws_tainted = 1 AND f.ws_epoch = :ws_epoch))`, where `:ws`, `:ws_tainted` and `:ws_epoch` come from auditd's `taint.get` for the reader (§9). Unbound facts are ranked by FTS5 bm25 in `memory_fts`. Bound facts are matched in `memory_fts_ws`, own scope only, newest first, and never bm25-ranked. Episodic summaries and tainted unbound facts apply read taint to the reader before delivery (§9). A clean reader skips them unless it passes `raise: true`, and the reply never says whether rows were skipped. Bound facts need no READ-INTENT, because their only readers already hold the tainted W. This filter protects only against facts known to be tainted: it doesn't cover a fact that a compromised broker laundered to `taint=0` by omitting a READ-INTENT (a v5.2 residual, §10). Results are rendered as a quoted `untrusted-memory` block as defense in depth. Memory is never mounted into tasks.
   - **End of binding.** Any epoch move of W (`taint.clear`, or a signed `workspace.restore`) makes that epoch's bound facts fail the read predicate at once. agentboxd then sets them `quarantined` and deletes their `memory_fts_ws` rows, before its next memory op for that agent and in the nightly sweep. Epochs never repeat, so a binding never matches again. The clear doesn't offer their release; a human releases any later, one at a time, with `memory review`.
   - **Visibility.** `agentbox status` and the health view show, for each tainted workspace, the bound-fact count and the epoch age. Every `memory.digest_interval_ms` (6 h), if there are new bound facts, agentboxd sends one ntfy digest per agent from a fixed template: the count, and for each fact the first 8 hex digits of its body SHA-256 and its length. No body, title or label. It is a notification, never an ask. `agentbox memory review --bound` lists the current epoch's bound facts any time, through the `memory review` renderer; releasing one is a separate signed `memory.review`.
   - **Deletion:**
     - `memory.forget{factId}` is logical. It resolves only facts the caller could read now (§5.2). Own scope Run, shared scopes Ask, procedural never. For an unbound fact it sets `forgotten` and deletes the `memory_fts` row in one transaction. For a fact bound to W's current epoch, forgotten by a task executor, it sets `forgotten`, deletes the `memory_fts_ws` row and keeps `ws_*` until the epoch ends; `taint review` and `memory review --bound` list it by {sha256, len, title-sha256}. The nightly sweep deletes the FTS rows of expired facts.
     - `memory.purge{factId}` is an admin op (humans via `rootd-admin.sock`, CLI only, signed). rootd forwards it to agentboxd. It deletes the rows, runs FTS `optimize`, checkpoints WAL and VACUUMs. This is best-effort, not forensic; the disk is LUKS-encrypted. Audit records hold only hashes of the title and body (§9), so purge needs no audit rewrite.
   - **Episodic layer.** Summaries reference bound facts by id only, never by body. auditd's query call never returns the records listed in §9 Access.
   ````
8. **§9 Audit:** M10 (the `taint.get` call, the title's audit class, read taint).
9. **§17 Review resolution:** the Round 6 entry (section 10.5).
10. **Appendix D:** M15. **Appendix E:** M16 (facts columns, `memory_fts_ws`, budget keys).
11. **Appendix F.** After `taint: tainted`, add `ws_subject: null` and `ws_epoch: null`. Below the block, add: "A workspace-bound fact sets `ws_subject` to `workspace:<agent>` and `ws_epoch` to the epoch integer. The audited title is {sha256, len}, like the body."

### 4.4 Threats and mitigations

| # | Red-team attack (severity) | How v5.3 closes it |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Attach-to-task OAuth laundering: an injected attach session reads the subscription token from session-home and writes it to own memory as a bound fact, which a later credential-free task reads and sends out; the post-attach scan looks only at the workspace diff (high, new in the first draft) | The exemption is for task executors only. A session's tainted own-scope write is quarantined: unindexed, unreadable by any task, and shown to a human at review. The post-attach token scan also covers the facts the session wrote. §0.5 holds. |
| 2 | Exfiltration over a write-capable allowlisted host (high) | Pre-existing v5.2 residual (§0.5, §6.2, §10). A bound note adds no egress and is weaker than an auto-loading `CLAUDE.md` in the same workspace, so B doesn't widen it. v2: the GET-only package broker. Until then: never allowlist a write-capable host for an agent that reads untrusted content. |
| 3 | GET exfiltration through proxy.golang.org module paths (high) | Pre-existing residual. v5.3 makes the deputy hosts human-only and flagged (A, R4); `GOPROXY=off` for agents without them. |
| 4 | A bound fact survives `workspace.restore`, because `memory.db` isn't in the restored subvolume and §5.2 said a restore doesn't move the epoch (medium) | A signed restore moves W's epoch (§5.2 now matches §5.3). Earlier-epoch facts fail the read predicate and are quarantined as a step of the restore. Epochs never repeat. The first draft's "safe either way" claim is removed. |
| 5 | Install-time code with no model plants a bound fact that steers later tasks for the whole time between clears; the early warning that per-write quarantine gave is lost (medium) | A per-epoch cap (50) and a staleness bound (7 days) send writes back to quarantine on a neglected box. `agentbox status` shows the count, a 6-hourly ntfy digest lists new bound facts by hash, and `memory review --bound` works any time. Every later reader is already tainted, so any off-box op still needs a signature (B-G8). |
| 6 | Write a directive, let a sibling act on it, then forget it so it never shows at clear time (medium) | Forget still runs free, but the row stays (`forgotten`, `ws_*` kept) and is listed by hash in `taint review` and `memory review --bound`. The digest records it before any forget. |
| 7 | Clear-time release fatigue: the owner, in key-touching mode, rubber-stamps a poisoned fact in a forced post-clear signing run (low) | Release is decoupled from the clear. The ended epoch's facts are quarantined and released later through a separate, paginated `memory review`, with no bulk accept. The cap bounds the backlog. |
| 8 | `taint.status` as a recon and human-presence oracle: `causes` reveals an attach, and `since` its timing (low) | There is no `taint.status`; the agent-facing taint shape is `{tainted, workspaceBound}` only (R10, R11). No causes, no `since`, no source classes. Human views keep full detail. |
| 9 | A compromised proxy omits READ-INTENT, so a web-read task stays clean and commits an attacker "procedure" as a trusted `taint=0` fact (high) | Pre-existing v5.2 residual, not widened. The clean-reader filter is documented as not covering it (§8). v2: a second taint check at the task gate on broker response classes. |
| 10 | A bound note carries the attacker's token task to task, and a later tainted task pushes with it to a write-capable allowlisted host (high) | Pre-existing egress residual (row 2). The note is no stronger than a planted `CLAUDE.md`, which auto-loads. |
| 11 | Latent steering: injected bound notes ("always deploy to host Y") steer later tasks of the epoch with no early warning (medium) | Detection is restored by the status count, the digest and `memory review --bound`; the cap and staleness bound limit it; one stricter Cedar forbid brings back per-fact quarantine. Ceiling: every later reader is already tainted, so this weakens detection only, not a §10 control. |

Other threats:
- **A forged or mis-set `workspaceBound`.** agentboxd computes it from the registry flag, own scope, auditd's row for W and the holder kind, never from the caller. The registry pins the bindable set; `workspace-bound-memory-only` and `memory-own` make the flag inert on any other action or scope; rootd's floor never reads it. A compromised agentboxd already controls memory writes and quarantine, so the compromise bound doesn't grow.
- **Snapshot-laundering race.** `snap:<id>` is raised to the workspace taint read after the btrfs call (RESULT); per-workspace serialization keeps clear and restore out of the window.
- **Judge finding folded in:** the binding assumes one workspace subject per agent; R27 makes that a precondition.

### 4.5 Gates
- **B-G1 (weeks 9–10, Cedar).** A `Ctx` without `workspaceBound` gives Deny. While tainted: own-scope `memory.write` with `workspaceBound=true` gives Run; with `false`, Ask (quarantine); with `true` and Risk ≥ 4, Ask. `workspaceBound=true` on a project-scope write, another agent's scope, `file.write`, `repo.push`, `workspace.restore` or `app.deploy` gives Ask, never Run. App. D passes strict validation.
- **B-G2 (weeks 9–10, registry).** The bindable set is exactly {`memory.write`, `memory.forget`}; flagging any other op refuses startup. A golden diff over every op: with `tainted=true`, floor and Cedar outcomes are the same for `workspaceBound` true and false, except own-scope `memory.write` and `memory.forget` by a task executor.
- **B-G3 (weeks 11–12, snapshot and restore).** A test hook pauses between a snapshot's INTENT ACK and the btrfs call while a sibling task makes one CONNECT and writes a file: the snapshot is recorded tainted. A signed `workspace.restore` of a tainted workspace sets a new epoch. A phone approval of a tainted restore is refused. A `taint.clear` submitted during a `workspace.snapshot` on the same workspace waits for it.
- **B-G4 (weeks 13–14, bound write).** Task T1 writes 3 own-scope facts while W is tainted at e: 3 active rows with `ws_subject=W`, `ws_epoch=e`; 0 quarantine rows, 0 asks, 0 ntfy pushes, 0 new `memory_fts` rows. Audit holds the title and body only as {sha256, len}.
- **B-G5 (weeks 13–14, read and spread).** T2 of the same agent in epoch e gets the 3 facts with 0 READ-INTENTs. A task of agent B reading `agent/A` is denied. B's clean query returns byte-identical rows and bm25 scores before and after T1 writes 20 bound facts that share B's terms. A reader whose `writers` row names a different subject gets 0 bound facts. After a restore (B-G3), a later task reads 0 earlier-epoch facts.
- **B-G6 (weeks 13–14, clear and decoupled release).** `taint review` lists the 3 facts. `taint.clear` doesn't prompt for any release. After the clear, a clean task of the agent reads 0 of them and stays clean. Killing agentboxd between the clear's RESULT and the quarantine update gives the same result after restart. After W is tainted again (e+2), no task sees the epoch-e facts. A fact released later by a separate `memory review` is skipped by a clean reader without `raise: true`.
- **B-G7 (weeks 13–14, attach exclusion).** A tainted own-scope `memory.write` from an `agent.attach` session gives 1 quarantine row and 0 bound rows. A planted OAuth token written to `agent/<self>` during attach is caught by the post-attach scan, which blocks tasks and alerts; no later task reads it.
- **B-G8 (weeks 13–14, no model in the loop).** A test npm postinstall calls the gate socket directly and submits `file.write` under `/srv/<own-app>/`, `repo.push` of a local OID under a push policy, `gh.pr.create`, then `net.allow_domain`: 0 Run and 0 effects (three pending, the fourth refused with `ASK_CAP`). Its own-scope `memory.write` gives 1 bound row and nothing else.
- **B-G9 (weeks 13–14, forget keeps evidence).** A bound fact forgotten in the current epoch loses its `memory_fts_ws` row (no task can read it), but `taint review` and `memory review --bound` list it by {sha256, len}, and the digest recorded it before the forget.
- **B-G10 (weeks 13–14, cap, staleness, digest).** With the defaults (50 per epoch, 7 days), the 51st bound write in one epoch is quarantined, and so is a write once the epoch is older than 7 days. `agentbox status` shows the per-workspace count. The digest carries only {count, short sha256, len} and no body.
- **B-G11 (weeks 13–14, taint view).** `ask.status` from a clean task returns `taint = {tainted: false, workspaceBound: false}`; after one CONNECT, `{true, true}`; from an attach session after one CONNECT, `{true, false}`. No reply holds causes, `since` or a source class, or reveals an attach. The call sends no READ-INTENT.
- **B-G12 (weeks 13–14, laundering honesty).** A test broker that omits READ-INTENT lets a `taint=0` fact commit, and a clean reader reads it without `raise`. The test records this as the known v5.2 residual (§10), not a v5.3 regression.
- **B-G13 (weeks 15–16, benchmark).** Run the 30 tasks with the exemption and with the stricter no-workspace-bound forbid. Asks per 100 are equal. Memory reviews per 100, and bound facts written and forgotten per 100, are reported separately. Both runs have 0 exfiltrations. In the 10 poisoning tasks, after a clear or a signed restore, `memory.read` returns 0 injected notes to any task.

### 4.6 PLAN weeks

| Weeks (PLAN row) | Days | What lands |
|---|---|---|
| 3 | 0.25 | auditd never deletes taint rows; `taint.get` returns the holder kind. |
| 4 | 0.25 | The `memory.write` title gets audit class `hash`. |
| 9–10 | 1.0 | `workspaceBindable` and its pin; `workspaceBound` in `Ctx`; the two policies; `taint.get` as the source of `tainted` and `workspaceBound`. B-G1, B-G2. |
| 11–12 | 0.75 | Snapshot taint at RESULT; restore moves the epoch and quarantines earlier-epoch facts; per-workspace serialization. B-G3. |
| 13–14 | 5.0 | Bound write, read, forget and end; `memory_fts_ws`; the clean-reader filter; decoupled release; cap and staleness bound; status count; digest; attach exclusion and the extended post-attach scan (memory exists only from these weeks). B-G4–B-G12. |
| 15–16 | 0.25 | B-G13. |
| **Total** | **~7.5** | No new service, socket, uid or nft rule. |

### 4.7 Residual risks
1. **Asks don't go down.** 182.7 → 182.7 from B alone. Every op that drives asks keeps its floor.
2. **Notes end at each clear and each signed restore**, unless a human releases them one at a time. The cap and the staleness bound trade some benefit for bounded exposure on a neglected box.
3. **Steering inside one tainted epoch.** An injected bound note can steer the same agent's later tasks, and search can surface it more often than a planted file. Its reach is what a tainted task already has without a signature (local edits, Safe ops, local pushes to `agent/<name>/*`, proxy egress). The digest and `memory review --bound` give the early warning back. One stricter Cedar forbid (`forbid … when { context.workspaceBound }` on `memory.write`) restores per-fact review entirely.
4. **Write-capable allowlisted hosts and deputy GET paths stay open** (red-team 2, 3, 10). Pre-existing; not widened. v2: the GET-only package broker.
5. **Proxy laundering into trusted `taint=0` memory stays open** (red-team 9). Pre-existing. v2: a gate-side second taint check. Memory soundness still rests on agentboxd and the brokers.
6. **Defaults are model-based.** 50 facts per epoch and 7 days come from 0.3 notes per task and a weekly clear; weeks 15–16 report the real rates (B-G13), and `budget.set` can change them.

## 5. Proposal C: structured denials

### 5.1 Problem
- v5.2 refusals are "a typed error" that no section specifies, a bare 403, "a typed 429", or silence:
  - nft drops are silent by design, and a tool that does its own DNS hangs on a resolver timeout;
  - curl, git and Chromium show only the CONNECT status line, never a body;
  - Cedar and floor outcomes have no agent-facing shape at all.
- Agents can't tell "change the request" from "wait for a human" from "stop". They retry, or they give up early.
- v5.2 gives an agent no way to learn an ask's outcome. Re-submitting after approval makes a second ask, or a second PR. A human "no" can be re-asked by rewording the reason.
- The spec contradicts itself on asks at task end: §5.4 says "Open asks expire when their task ends", but §4.2 step 6 denies them only when the end reason isn't `completed`, and the §5.2 execute re-check accepts `end_reason='completed'`.
- Appendix C sets no `HTTPS_PROXY`, so tools can't find the egress forwarder unless someone configures them by hand.

### 5.2 Design
1. **One rule (§0.8).** A denial holds only the caller's own grammar-checked request fields, constants from the signed release, and facts about the caller's own task and resources. Code decides first; the explanation is built afterwards and can't change the decision. No enforcement point reads a denial. A remedy grants nothing: a remedy op goes through §5.2 like any other op.
2. **One constructor, checked twice.** `ops.Deny(code, what, slots)` builds every denial against a closed generated schema (printable ASCII, at most 1 KiB, no extra fields). The rootd-mounted, read-only in-task `agentbox` binary checks every gate reply again and re-renders the text from its own compiled registry. So a compromised agentboxd can choose a code, an id or a number, never text.
3. **36 append-only codes**, each with a `next` move: `fix`, `wait`, `ask`, `retry`, `human` or `stop`. The four designs' codes are merged into one table (R23).
4. **No existence bit.** Owner-only kinds are looked up only in the caller's namespace. App names become per owner. Shared ids (bus tasks) become random ULIDs, and a miss runs the same Cedar pass on a synthetic entity. Every `NOT_FOUND_OR_NOT_OWNED` leaves at receipt + 200 ms, and every `NEEDS_SIGNATURE`, quote and batch reply at receipt + 2 s (R16).
5. **Native surfaces.** MCP `isError` results; gate JSON-RPC error -32010; a CONNECT reason phrase `agentbox <CODE> (run: agentbox why)`; the vendor's own error envelope from keys; `remote:` lines from git.
6. **Asks you can finish.** `NEEDS_SIGNATURE` names the pending id, the channel, the expiry and the fixed human command. `ask.status` (D) returns the ask's state and agentbox-generated results; `agentbox ask wait <id>` blocks on it. One match key per op makes a human "no" final and returns an executed push or PR instead of asking again (M5).
7. **No silent hangs.** taskrun exports `HTTPS_PROXY`; tasks run with `--dns=none` and a resolver that fails in under 1 s; brokers dial within one 10 s budget; forwarders retry a missing broker for 5 s, then answer 503.
8. **In-task help.** A 64-entry denial log in the tmpfs HOME, `agentbox why [<CODE> | --errno <E>]`, an ask-cost line at the top of every generated MCP tool description, and eight fixed lines in the generated `CLAUDE.md` and `AGENTS.md`.
9. **Witness.** A per-task FIFO that only rootd reads records every denial the task saw. At task end rootd flags forged floor codes, denials it never saw, and a flooded witness. A clean task also gets one fixed ntfy alert. The witness proves what a task saw only while the task itself doesn't collude.
10. **Audit.** Best-effort, coalesced DENY records for humans. auditd's query call never returns them, so their detail never reaches an agent (M10).

No §0 principle is narrowed. Denials are outputs only (§0.1, §0.4 hold). No network path is added (§0.3). The in-task log dies with the tmpfs HOME, the witness FIFO is removed at task end, and no task ever reads it (§0.6). Denial text never feeds taint or policy (§0.7).

### 5.3 Spec changes (ready to paste)
Shared spots: M1 (§0.8), M2 (§4.2 step 2), M3 (§5.2), M4 (`net.allow_domain`), M5 (§5.4), M6 (§5.5), M7 (§6.2), M9 (§8 exports), M10 (§9), M11 (§10), M12 (§11), M13 (§13), M14 (App. C), M15 (App. D), M16 (App. E).

M, app uids and ports never appear in agent-visible output (§5.6)." In §12, `app-<name>` (71000+M) becomes `app-<M>` (71000+M). In §5.4 origin isolation item 2, `--service=svc:<app>` becomes `--service=svc:<agent>-<app>`.

   - New bullets after Limits:

     - **No DNS, fast failure.** Tasks run with `--dns=none`. The image's `/etc/resolv.conf` is `nameserver 127.0.0.1` with `options timeout:1 attempts:1`, and nothing listens on port 53, so a direct lookup fails in under 1 s. A task's own `connect()` gets `ENETUNREACH` at once (`Network=none`). taskrun exports `HTTPS_PROXY` and `https_proxy` = `http://127.0.0.1:8080`, and `NO_PROXY` and `no_proxy` = `127.0.0.1,localhost,::1`. `HTTP_PROXY` and `ALL_PROXY` are never set.

Each code has: `next` per `what`, enforcers, HTTP status, a message template, agent-visible slots with grammars, and a static remedy. Generated from it: the Go constants (`ops.Code` is the only type `ops.Deny` accepts), `gen/denial.v1.json`, the `agentbox why` text, the docs table and the DENY audit schema. The in-task binary compiles the same tables. CI diffs `gen/codes.golden`: codes are append-only and never renamed.
   - Each op declares the codes it can return, its `matchKey` fields (§5.4), its resolution kind (owner-scoped or shared, §5.2), whether it reaches rootd, and for each output whether agentbox or the remote generated it (`ask.status` returns only agentbox-generated outputs, §5.3). Each generated MCP tool description starts with one ask-cost line: `Never asks a human.`, `Asks (1 of your task's ask budget) when this task is tainted or policy requires; CLI or phone.`, or `Always asks (1 of your task's ask budget); laptop CLI only.` `repo.push` reads: `Asks unless a standing push policy covers this private remote and the task is clean; CLI only. Send it with its PR as one ask.batch.`
