A denial is not a read: its only non-constant bytes are the caller's own grammar-checked fields, returned to the same task, so it taints nothing."
7. **§5.4:** M5.
8. **§5.5:** M6.
9. **New §5.6 Denials** (after §5.5):
   ````markdown
   ### 5.6 Denials
   Every refusal, ask, cap and broker failure that a task can hit returns one denial. A denial explains. It never authorizes. Code decides first; the denial is built afterwards from the registry and can't change the decision. No enforcement point reads a denial. A remedy op goes through §5.2 like any other op.

   **Shape.** JSON per `gen/denial.v1.json` (`additionalProperties: false`), printable ASCII (0x20–0x7E) only, at most 1 KiB. `ops.Deny(code, what, slots)` is the only constructor. It drops a slot that fails its grammar and keeps the code. Fields:
   - `v` (1); `id` (`dn_<ULID>`, or the pending id for an ask); `code`; `by` (gate, cedar, floor, proxy, keys, git, forwarder); `rule` (a static name); `op`.
   - `next`: `fix` (change the request); `wait` (a human is deciding: run `agentbox ask wait <id>`); `ask` (you may submit the remedy op; a human signs it); `retry` (after `afterMs`); `human` (only a human can unblock: report the code and id, and go on without it); `stop` (never retry).
   - `subject`: the caller's own structural fields that pass their grammar: host (A-label), port, ref, OID, remote, app or snapshot id, JSON pointer, package, unit or model name.
   - `what`: from the code's closed list. `reason` and `taint` (asks only). `limit`; `resetsAt` (own scope only, rounded up to 15 min); `asksLeft` (equal to `asks.left`, §5.4).
   - `msg`: one fixed sentence from the registry template, at most 200 bytes.
   - `remedy`, at most 3 of: `op{op, args, approval}`, `fix{field, expect}`, `wait{pending, approval, expiresAt, notified, human}`, `poll{op: ask.status, afterMs}`, `retry{afterMs}`, `human{admin}`, `none`.

   **Leak rules.**
   1. Echo only the caller's own grammar-checked fields. Never echo free text (`reason`, `title`, `body`, `content`, commit messages), request bodies, upstream text, IP addresses, or hash- and drop-class values (§9).
   2. Name only the requested item. Never list allowlist entries, models, policies or remotes. Never name other agents' tasks, apps, leases, budgets or asks.
   3. Missing and not-owned give `NOT_FOUND_OR_NOT_OWNED` through one lookup or one Cedar path (§5.2), sent at receipt + 200 ms.
   4. Taint shows only as `taint = {tainted, workspaceBound}` (§8).
   5. Cedar `rule` is `cedar.hop-limit`, `cedar.memory-procedural-never`, `cedar.chain` (an ancestor's block; no ancestor named) or `cedar.custom` (any other forbid, including every human-written policy). An ask's `reason` is `tainted` when the effective taint is set, and `signed` otherwise: the floor default, a custom policy, a missing push policy, spend (`budget-ask`), risk and decide all look the same. Every `NEEDS_SIGNATURE` leaves at receipt + 2 s.
   6. Box-wide limits give one `BUSY` with no sub-reason: the OS lock, the box's 3 CAS-binding asks, the disk reserve and the box-wide daemon-ask pool. `LEASE_HELD` always says `afterMs` 30000 and never names the holder. `KEYS_BUDGET` covers only the caller's own day share or trace (§6.3).
   7. Remedies are static per (code, what, op). A remedy op is listed only if its binder admits those args. agentbox never re-runs policy to find one.
   8. A denial never sends ntfy, never waits for an auditd ACK, and never enters P, H, ntfy text or the approval renderer.

   **Codes** (append-only; v1 has 36):

   | Code | next | By | Notes |
   |---|---|---|---|
   | `INVALID_ARGS` | fix | gate, floor | `field` (JSON pointer) and `expect`; never the value; also a body that contains `agentbox-op:` |
   | `UNKNOWN_OP` | stop | gate | also any op no agent may call |
   | `TASK_ENDED` | stop | any | |
   | `NOT_FOUND_OR_NOT_OWNED` | stop | gate, cedar, floor, git | one path (§5.2); at receipt + 200 ms |
   | `DENIED_POLICY` | stop | cedar | `rule` per leak rule 5 |
   | `POLICY_ERROR` | human | cedar | evaluation error; fails closed |
   | `MEMORY_QUARANTINED` | human | gate | the write was stored in quarantine; MCP `isError: false`; `agentbox memory review` |
   | `CAP_REACHED` | per what | gate, floor, git | `memory_writes_task`, `memory_rows`, `memory_bytes`, `bus_messages`, `artifacts`, `push_bytes_day` (human; `limit`, `resetsAt`); `quote_items_task`, `quote_items_trace` (stop) |
   | `BUS_REFUSED` | stop | gate | `hop`, `children`, `cycle`, `deadline`, `parent_ended`, `not_cancelable` |
   | `LEASE_HELD` | retry | gate | `afterMs` 30000 |
   | `NEEDS_SIGNATURE` | wait | floor | see Asks; at receipt + 2 s |
   | `ASK_DENIED` | stop | floor | a human denied this match key in this task; carries the denied row's id |
   | `ASK_EXPIRED` | ask | floor | a new ask counts against the budget |
   | `ASK_CAP` | per what | floor | `task_open` (wait; lists the task's own open ids); `task_budget` (human); `agent_24h`, `agent_ops_24h` (human; `resetsAt`) |
   | `STALE` | per what | floor | `ref_moved`, `cas_changed`, `stale_base` (fix); `policy_changed`, `signer_revoked`, `approval_expired` (ask); `taint_moved` (wait; carries the new pending id; uses no budget); `remote_changed` (human) |
   | `BUSY` | retry | floor | OS lock, box CAS-binding cap, disk reserve, box daemon-ask pool; `afterMs` 60000 |
   | `ADMISSION_REJECTED` | per what | floor | `closure_too_large`, `context_too_large` (fix; `limit`); `own_cas_pending` (wait); `batch_shape`, `batch_remote_not_private`, `batch_text_cap`, `batch_member_open` (fix); `batch_open` (wait) |
   | `FLOOR_NEVER` | stop | floor | `protected_package`, `protected_unit`, `exposure_443`, `vendor_host`, `never_available` |
   | `HUMAN_ONLY` | human | floor | `deputy_host`; remedy `human{agentbox egress allow}` |
   | `REF_OUTSIDE_NAMESPACE` | fix | floor, git | expect `refs/heads/agent/<name>/*` |
   | `OID_NOT_IN_REMOTE_REPO` | fix | floor | push the commit through `:8082` first |
   | `REMOTE_REJECTED` | per what | floor | `pr_exists`, `no_commits` (fix); `ruleset` (stop); `not_found`, `other` (human); `rate_limited`, `unreachable` (retry); no GitHub text or status |
   | `EFFECT_FAILED` | human | floor | no effect output |
   | `INDETERMINATE` | human | floor | a human runs `op.resolve` |
   | `BATCH_ABORTED` | ask | floor | an earlier member of the batch failed or was refused; this member never ran |
   | `NESTED_SUBVOLUME` | human | floor | name kept from §4.1 |
   | `AUDIT_UNAVAILABLE` | retry | floor, brokers | auditd down or in disk-full mode |
   | `EGRESS_NOT_ALLOWLISTED` | ask; human for `deputy_host` | proxy | remedy `net.allow_domain{host, port}`, CLI; for a deputy host, `human{agentbox egress allow}` |
   | `EGRESS_WRONG_ROUTE` | fix | proxy | `llm_vendor` (use `:8081`), `plain_http` |
   | `EGRESS_NAME_REJECTED` | stop | proxy | `non_public` only; no IP, no check name |
   | `EGRESS_RESOLVE_FAILED` | per what | proxy | `no_such_name` (fix), `unavailable` (retry) |
   | `EGRESS_DIAL_FAILED` | retry | proxy | `timeout`, `refused`, `blocked` |
   | `KEYS_REQUEST_REJECTED` | per what | keys | `max_tokens`, `server_tool`, `dropped_field` (fix; `limit`); `endpoint`, `field`, `block_type`, `tool_type`, `url_field`, `source_type`, `beta_header`, `model` (human); `field` pointer |
   | `KEYS_BUDGET` | human | keys | `agent` (own day share; `resetsAt`), `trace`; remedy `human{budget.set}` |
   | `KEYS_UPSTREAM` | per what | keys | `unreachable`, `timeout` (retry); `auth` (human, `secret.set`) |
   | `BROKER_UNAVAILABLE` | retry | forwarder, `agentbox mcp` | `gate`, `egress`, `llm`, `git`; also a gate reply that fails the in-task check |

   Human CLI replies (`approval_stale`, `CACHE_PURGE_FAILED`) are not agent codes; no task sees them.

   **Asks.** `NEEDS_SIGNATURE` (next `wait`) carries: `reason` (`tainted` or `signed`); `taint`; the pending id (the batch id for a batch); `channel` (`cli` or `cli_or_phone`, from `reversible`); `expiresAt` (= `pending.expires_at`); `notified` (false only if the ntfy push failed); `asksLeft`; the fixed human command `agentbox approve <id>`; and the remedies `wait` and `poll{ask.status, afterMs 30000}`. `msg`: `<op> waits for a human (<channel>) until <time>. It stays open if your task ends normally. Run: agentbox ask wait <id>.` Re-submission follows the §5.4 match key.

   **Surfaces.**

   | Caller | The denial arrives as |
   |---|---|
   | MCP (`agentbox mcp`) | `isError: true` plus one text block: `msg`, a newline, then `agentbox-denial/v1 <JSON>`; no `structuredContent` on errors. `MEMORY_QUARANTINED` uses `isError: false`, because the write was stored. |
   | gate JSON-RPC | error -32010; message = code; data = the denial |
   | proxy CONNECT | 400, 403, 502, 503 or 504; reason phrase `agentbox <CODE> (run: agentbox why)`, ASCII, at most 64 bytes; header `Agentbox-Denial: v1 code=<c> next=<n> id=<id>`; JSON body `{error, agentbox}`; `Connection: close` |
   | keys | the v5.2 statuses in the vendor's own error envelope; the message starts `agentbox <CODE>:`; plus the `agentbox` member and the header; `x-should-retry: false` unless next is `retry`; `retry-after` if the reset is under 1 h |
   | git | `text/plain; charset=utf-8` (404 for `NOT_FOUND_OR_NOT_OWNED`, else 403 or 503), two lines: `msg`, then the JSON; git prints them as `remote:` lines; `pre-receive` prints the same |
   | task result | `result.denials`: up to 20 entries. agentboxd keeps {id, code, what, op, subject}, re-renders `msg` and `remedy` from the registry, labels them task-reported, and gives them the result's taint (§7) |
   | errno only | seccomp `EOPNOTSUPP`, `EDQUOT`, `EROFS`, `ENETUNREACH`, failed lookups, pids `EAGAIN`: `agentbox why --errno <E>` prints fixed guidance |

   **Integrity.**
   - Floor codes come only from rootd. Each has a rootd-minted id, kept in `task_denials` or `pending` until the task ends.
   - The in-task `agentbox` binary (rootd-mounted, read-only, same release) checks every gate reply against the schemas compiled into it. It replaces `subject` with the request it sent, re-renders `msg` and `remedy` from its own registry, and drops any `ask.status` result field that is not an agentbox-generated output. A reply that fails becomes `BROKER_UNAVAILABLE{gate}`, plus a `malformed` witness line.
   - So agentboxd can choose a code, an id or a number, never text.

   **Witness.**
   - The forwarders and `agentbox mcp` write `<id> <op> <code> <by>` for each denial to the task's witness FIFO. taskrun ends with `END <sha256 of the posted result>`.
   - The agent uid can write the FIFO but not read it. agentboxd's uid can't open it. rootd parses each line with one anchored pattern and keeps the first 64 KiB of lines. It counts every later line in `dropped`, and still keeps the last `END` line.
   - At `task.stop`, rootd checks each Safe, live or os line it kept: the id must be in `task_denials` or `pending` for that task, with the same code. A miss gives `GATE_MISMATCH{forged|unseen}`. A witness with `dropped` > 0 also gives `GATE_MISMATCH{flooded}`. The kept lines are still checked, so an overflow never hides a mismatch among them. If the task is clean at stop and has any mismatch kind, rootd sends one ntfy alert with fixed text: `agentbox: gate mismatch, task <id>`. A tainted task gets the record only.
   - Only a rootd restart marks a witness `incomplete`, and only that case raises no alert: the gap is rootd's own, because lines written while it was down are lost. Its WITNESS and GATE_MISMATCH records and its `agentbox task show` lines stay. A flood is the task's doing, so it never suppresses the alert.
   - **Scope.** The witness proves what a task saw only while the task itself doesn't collude. Task code runs as the agent uid, so it can forge, flood or withhold its own lines, or call the gate socket directly. A flood still raises `flooded`; a withheld line raises nothing. A compromised agentboxd that also writes the task's prompt can make the task collude, which the v5.2 bound already allows (it can drive any clean task, §5.2). v2: `agentbox-gate` binds the prompt hash of human-started tasks (§10).
   - `agentbox task show <id>` reads rootd and audit, never agentboxd. It shows the task's asks with their final states and fine reasons (§4.2), its quote counters, the witness lines and any mismatch, and prints `result_altered` if the stored result doesn't hash to `END`.

   **In-task fallback.** The forwarders and `agentbox mcp` log to `$HOME/.agentbox/denials.jsonl` (a 64-entry ring in the tmpfs HOME). `agentbox why` prints the newest 10. Task code can write this log, so it is help text, not evidence. `agentbox why` is not an MCP tool, so the §5.1 bijection is unchanged.
   ````
10. **§6.1**, add one line: "No ruleset change for denials. nft never logs to agents, and a drop stays silent. Structured refusals come from the brokers (§5.6). A broker dial that nft drops ends in `EPERM` or in the 10 s dial budget, which becomes `EGRESS_DIAL_FAILED` or `KEYS_UPSTREAM`."
11. **§6.2:** M7.
12. **§6.3 `agentbox-keys`.**
    - Replace "Anything else gets 403." (both endpoint lists) with "Anything else gets 403 `KEYS_REQUEST_REJECTED{endpoint}`."
    - Replace "A failed check gets 403." with "A failed check gets 403 `KEYS_REQUEST_REJECTED{what}` with the JSON pointer. It echoes a rejected name only for closed vocabularies (model, block type, beta name), and gives the numeric cap for `max_tokens`. No other request byte."
    - Budget reservation: replace "against the agent, trace and day budgets, and rejects with a typed 429 above the hard cap" with "against the agent's day share and the trace budget. Above either, it rejects with 429 `KEYS_BUDGET{agent|trace}` and `x-should-retry: false`; it sets `retry-after` only for `agent`, and only when the reset is under 1 h. The box day cap is split into per-agent day shares: equal by default and recomputed at `agent.add` and `agent.remove`, unless `budget.set` gave explicit shares (it refuses shares that sum above the box cap). So keys never refuses one agent because of another agent's spend or reservations. keys still checks the box total as a backstop; a backstop hit gives `KEYS_BUDGET` with no `what`, plus an alert."
    - Local LLM field allowlist, append: "When fields are dropped, the 200 carries `Agentbox-Denial` with `KEYS_REQUEST_REJECTED{dropped_field}` and the dropped names."
    - Add: "**Upstream responses.** keys strips every upstream `Agentbox-*` header, and the top-level `agentbox` member of any non-2xx JSON body. An upstream 401 or 403 becomes 502 `KEYS_UPSTREAM{auth}`, with no upstream bytes. Other upstream errors pass through unchanged, so CLIs still see context-length errors. Pinned dials share one 10 s budget; on timeout: 504 `KEYS_UPSTREAM{timeout}` with `x-should-retry: true`."
13. **§6.3 `agentbox-git`.**
    - Replace "a root-owned `pre-receive` hook that allows `agent/<name>/*` only" with "a root-owned `pre-receive` hook that allows `agent/<name>/*` only. For any other ref it prints the two §5.6 lines (`REF_OUTSIDE_NAMESPACE`), which git shows as `remote:` lines. It enforces the daily byte cap from the size of `$GIT_QUARANTINE_PATH` (`CAP_REACHED{push_bytes_day}`, `resetsAt`), so a rejected pack is never stored. `receive.maxInputSize` keeps git's own message."
    - Add: "Smart-HTTP refusals are `text/plain` bodies in the §5.6 format. A repo agentbox-git doesn't serve to the caller and a missing repo are one lookup miss: both get 404 `NOT_FOUND_OR_NOT_OWNED`, sent at receipt + 200 ms."
    - Remote writes, append: "A GitHub refusal at execution becomes `REMOTE_REJECTED{what}`. GitHub's text and status never reach a task; they go only to the DENY detail."
14. **§7 Agent-to-agent bus**, add after Limits: "Bus task ids are ULIDs (§5.1); rootd's `task.start` refuses any other grammar. Refusals are §5.6 denials: `BUS_REFUSED{hop|children|cycle|deadline|parent_ended|not_cancelable}`; the `not_cancelable` case keeps the A2A name `TaskNotCancelableError`. Cedar `hop-limit` shows as `BUS_REFUSED{hop}` with rule `cedar.hop-limit`. A task id the actor has no right to and a missing one run the same Cedar pass (a miss is a synthetic Task with no executor, skill or originator); both give `NOT_FOUND_OR_NOT_OWNED` at receipt + 200 ms. Originator get and cancel, linked-peer messages and `skills:` claims are unchanged. `LEASE_HELD` gives `afterMs` 30000 and never names the holder. A child's `NEEDS_SIGNATURE` shows only its own `taint` shape, never an ancestor's task or agent."
15. **§8:** `MEMORY_QUARANTINED` and `CAP_REACHED` are in B's §8 text; the fixed lines are M9.
16. **§15 Build rows:** section 10.3.

### 5.4 Threats and mitigations

| # | Red-team attack (severity) | How v5.3 closes it |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Cross-agent existence mapping by timing: a missing id short-circuits before Cedar while another agent's id runs the full Cedar and floor path, so latency shows which ids exist (low) | Owner-only kinds are looked up only in the caller's namespace, so another agent's app, remote, snapshot, pending row, fact or inbox message is never read: missing and not-owned are the same index miss. App names are per owner, so `app.deploy` can't probe either. Bus task ids are 80-bit-random ULIDs, and a miss runs the same Cedar pass on a synthetic Task. Every `NOT_FOUND_OR_NOT_OWNED` leaves at receipt + 200 ms; overruns are counted. The two-agent gate tests latency (KS test), not only bytes. |
| 2 | A colluding-agent bit through box-wide `BUSY` and `KEYS_BUDGET{day}` (low) | `KEYS_BUDGET` now depends only on the caller's own day share and trace. `LEASE_HELD` uses a fixed 30 s and names no holder. `BUSY` keeps one box-wide bit, which has no safe partition (one OS, one OS lock, 3 CAS-binding slots for up to 999 agents); it is stated in §10. |
| 3 | `op.get` as an untainted channel under a compromised agentboxd (medium) | `op.get` is folded into `ask.status` (R11), which is in the §5.2 bound and the §10 residual. Its results hold only agentbox-generated tokens, never GitHub-assigned numbers or URLs (R12), and the in-task binary drops anything else. So a compromised agentboxd gains no text channel beyond the prompt it already writes. v2: rootd answers `ask.status` through `agentbox-gate`. |
| 4 | A fabricated `ASK_DENIED` (next `stop`) silently suppresses a clean task's wanted push (medium) | Floor codes come only from rootd, each with an id rootd records; `ASK_DENIED` carries the denied row's id. The witness FIFO gives rootd its own copy of every denial the task saw, which agentboxd can't read or change. At `task.stop` rootd flags forged floor codes and Safe, live or os denials it never received; a clean task also gets one fixed ntfy alert. `agentbox task show` reads rootd and audit, and flags a stored result that doesn't hash to `END`. The fixed lines ask agents to list every blocked action in their result. This holds while the task itself doesn't collude. A task that floods its witness to push the real line out still gets `GATE_MISMATCH{flooded}` and the alert; only a rootd restart skips the alert (§5.6 Witness). |

Other threats:
- **A denial as a step toward exfiltration.** A denial adds no capability: remedy ops go through §5.2; `net.allow_domain` stays signature plus CLI with the typed host:port; the binder refuses LLM vendor hosts; deputy hosts are human-only. A denial holds only the caller's own fields and release constants, so it gives the agent no new data to send out.
- **Taint laundering through denial bytes.** Denials echo only the caller's own grammar-checked fields. `result.denials` is re-rendered from the registry and carries the result's taint. DENY and WITNESS records, which hold the human-only detail, never leave auditd through the query call.
- **Policy oracle and classifier evasion.** No predicate text, nearest allowlist entry or model list. Every non-taint ask reads `signed` and leaves at the 2 s floor, so decide latency doesn't show. Human-written policy ids show as `cedar.custom`. Ask cost per op is static public registry text.
- **LAN and split-horizon mapping.** Every Appendix B cause gives the same `EGRESS_NAME_REJECTED{non_public}`, with no address and no check name. Only allowlisted names are ever resolved. `--dns=none` keeps the host's resolver and search domains out of tasks.
- **Secret leakage.** Values are never echoed (`INVALID_ARGS` gives only the pointer); hash and drop fields never fill a slot; an upstream 401/403 becomes `KEYS_UPSTREAM{auth}` with no upstream bytes; GitHub text never reaches a task; the sink NACKs a malformed DENY record.
- **Prompt or terminal injection through a denial.** Printable ASCII only; A-label hosts; capped pointers; a 64-byte reason phrase and a 1 KiB denial; no upstream text. The human sees task-reported denials and witness lines only as registry tokens, through the §5.4 escaper.
- **Spoofed denials from upstream or task code.** keys strips upstream `Agentbox-*` headers and members. Forwarders log only the broker's response head. A planted log line can only produce a registry-template remedy, which still needs the floor and a human. agentboxd re-renders task-reported denials from the registry, so a social-engineering remedy can't reach the human. A forged witness line costs at most one alert, and only from a clean task.
- **Approval fatigue from remedy hints.** A denial never mints a P. A remedy op is listed only when its binder admits the args. A human no is final per match key. Executed pushes and PRs return their result instead of a new ask. `ASK_CAP` creates no P and sends no ntfy. C-G9 requires asks per 100 with denials on to be no higher than with them off.
- **Approval path tampering.** Denials, remedies, witness lines and task-reported denials never enter P, H, ntfy or the renderer. The human command is fixed text with no agent bytes.
- **Floods.** DENY records are best-effort: coalesced per (task, code, what, subject) per 60 s, capped at 500 per task (then one DENY_FLOOD record), and dropped in disk-full mode. The witness is capped at 64 KiB per task, and an overflow is flagged as `GATE_MISMATCH{flooded}`. Reply-floor timers hold no lock.
- **Judge findings folded in:** no `remote_unregistered` versus `remote_not_owned` split (one code); not-found is a mapping of Cedar and floor refusals, never a pre-Cedar short-circuit, so originator, linked-peer and `skills:` flows keep working; `resetsAt` and lease hints are rounded or fixed; forwarders retry for 5 s instead of failing on a broker restart; one dial budget of 10 s fits under pip's 15 s timeout; human-only detail is kept out of episodic views.

### 5.5 Gates
- **C-G1 (week 4, codes, constructor, in-task check, ids).** The codes table, `ops.Deny` and `gen/denial.v1.json` are generated. A code missing from the table, or renamed or removed against `gen/codes.golden`, fails the build. A `NEEDS_SIGNATURE` channel that disagrees with `reversible` refuses startup. 24 h of fuzzing `ops.Deny` with ESC/CSI, U+009B, U+202E, U+2066–U+2069, U+0000, 64 KiB strings, URLs, base64 and secret-shaped values in every slot: every output is schema-valid printable ASCII of at most 1 KiB, and no byte of a free-text, hash- or drop-class input appears. A schema-invalid call gives `INVALID_ARGS` with the pointer only; an unknown op gives `UNKNOWN_OP`; `agentbox mcp` returns both as `isError: true` with one `agentbox-denial/v1` block. A DENY record with an unknown code, a free-text field or an unhashed hash field is NACKed. Fed gate replies with free text, URLs, non-hex OIDs, a GitHub-assigned number in an `ask.status` result, a subject that differs from its request, or an `ASK_DENIED` without a pending id, the in-task binary shows none of those bytes, returns `BROKER_UNAVAILABLE{gate}` and writes a `malformed` witness line. Every minted bus task, message, fact, pending, snapshot and denial id matches `<kind>_<ULID>`; rootd `task.start` refuses the id `T42`. The `ask.status` and witness parsers pass the fuzz run.
- **C-G2 (weeks 5–6, DNS, forwarders, witness).** With the proxy env removed, `getent hosts example.com` and `curl https://example.com` each fail in under 1 s; `resolv.conf` names only 127.0.0.1 (if podman overrides it, the read-only bind of R26 is in place and the check passes). `HTTPS_PROXY`, `https_proxy`, `NO_PROXY` and `no_proxy` are set; `HTTP_PROXY` and `ALL_PROXY` are not. A broker stopped for 3 s: the request succeeds after reconnect. Stopped for 10 s: the client gets 503 `BROKER_UNAVAILABLE` within 6 s, and `agentbox why` prints it. The btrfs ioctl test followed by `agentbox why --errno EOPNOTSUPP` prints the seccomp guidance. The log never holds more than 64 entries, and the next task starts with none. The generated `CLAUDE.md` and `AGENTS.md` carry exactly the eight fixed lines, identical for two agents. Under rootless podman with `keep-id`, the task writes the FIFO through the read-only mount but can't read it or create files there; uid `agentbox` can't open it; 1 MiB written leaves rootd at most 64 KiB; after `task.stop` the dir is gone; a rootd restart mid-task marks that witness incomplete and raises no alert.
- **C-G3 (weeks 7–8, proxy).** An off-list CONNECT gives `403 agentbox EGRESS_NOT_ALLOWLISTED (run: agentbox why)` with the header and body, and rootd sees no resolve call. With 50 entries, the response names none of the other 49. Errors from Python `urllib.request` and Go `net/http` contain the code; `agentbox why` shows host:port and the `net.allow_domain` remedy within 1 s. `api.anthropic.com:443` gives `EGRESS_WRONG_ROUTE{llm_vendor}`, and `net.allow_domain` for it gives `FLOOR_NEVER{vendor_host}`. A CONNECT to `proxy.golang.org:443` for an agent without it gives `EGRESS_NOT_ALLOWLISTED{deputy_host}` with next `human`. An origin-form `GET /_cache/go/...` reaches the cache grammar; an absolute-form `GET http://…` gives `EGRESS_WRONG_ROUTE{plain_http}`. Names that answer 192.168.1.10, NAT64-wrapped 192.168.x, the box's own SLAAC address or an on-link global IPv6 each give the same `EGRESS_NAME_REJECTED` bytes apart from the id, with no address. An allowlisted host whose address drops SYNs gives 504 or 502 `EGRESS_DIAL_FAILED` within 11 s. With the env set, replayed Claude Code and Codex sessions send 0 proxy requests for 127.0.0.1.
- **C-G4 (weeks 7–8, keys and git).** Each keys 403 case gives the vendor envelope with the code and pointer, and no other request byte. An agent at its own day share gets 429 `KEYS_BUDGET{agent}` with `x-should-retry: false`; replayed Claude Code and Codex stop within their own retry limits, and the task result carries `KEYS_BUDGET`. `budget.set` with shares that sum above the box cap is refused. Agent B's keys replies are byte-identical apart from ids whether agent A is idle, at its share, or holding its largest reservations. An upstream `Agentbox-Denial` header or `agentbox` member is stripped. An upstream 401 gives 502 `KEYS_UPSTREAM{auth}` with no upstream byte. A push to `refs/heads/main` prints `remote: agentbox REF_OUTSIDE_NAMESPACE` and the JSON line. A push over the daily cap prints `CAP_REACHED{push_bytes_day}` and stores no objects. Fetching a repo agentbox-git doesn't serve to the caller and fetching a missing repo give byte-identical `text/plain` 404s apart from the id, both sent 200–205 ms after receipt. A fake GitHub 422 carrying a canary gives `REMOTE_REJECTED`, and the canary is in no task-visible byte. The keys and git nft drop counters stay at 0.
- **C-G5 (weeks 9–10, asks and isolation).** A golden table gives every op and floor outcome its code, reason and channel: tainted `repo.push` gives `{tainted, cli}`, tainted `file.write` `{tainted, cli_or_phone}`, clean `gh.pr.create` `{signed, cli}`. For a clean task, asks caused by the floor default, a custom human policy, a missing push policy, `budget-ask`, `risk-ask`, a decide escalation and a missing Run permit are byte-identical apart from ids, and all leave at 2 s or later. `ask.status` returns the caller's own P in every state; an executed `gh.pr.create` shows the remote id and head OID and no PR number or URL, while `agentbox task show` shows the URL. Re-submitting an executed `repo.push` or `gh.pr.create` returns its result, creates no ask and opens no second PR. After `pending.deny`, a re-submission with the same match key (reason reworded) gives `ASK_DENIED` with the denied row's id and no ask; a new PR body gives exactly one new ask, with the "related … was denied" header; after a revocation or expiry, exactly one new ask. A 4th open ask gives `ASK_CAP{task_open}` listing the 3 ids, with no P and no ntfy. Approval isolation: with control, bidi and `<img onerror>` in echoed fields, task-reported denials and witness lines, the renderer output and the ntfy title and body are byte-identical to the no-denial run, and H is unchanged.
- **C-G6 (weeks 9–10, fake agentboxd).** A client posing as agentboxd, against a clean task: (a) answers `repo.push` with a fabricated `ASK_DENIED` and never calls rootd: at `task.stop` there is `GATE_MISMATCH{forged}` and exactly one ntfy alert with the fixed text; (b) invents `DENIED_POLICY{cedar.custom}` without sending it to rootd: `GATE_MISMATCH{unseen}`; (c) strips the code and id from the stored result: `agentbox task show` prints `result_altered`; (d) puts text, a URL or an IP into `ask.status` result fields or a denial: the task sees none of it, and the witness has a `malformed` line; (e) does the same against a tainted task: records only, no alert; (f) relays forged denials: no rootd outcome changes; (g) a clean task writes 1 MiB of well-formed junk lines to its witness, once before and once after the fake agentboxd answers its `repo.push` with a fabricated `ASK_DENIED`: each run ends with `GATE_MISMATCH` of kind `forged`, `flooded` or both, and exactly one ntfy alert, never neither; a third run that also restarts rootd mid-task gives `incomplete`, the records and no alert. An honest run of 30 clean tasks gives 0 `GATE_MISMATCH`.
- **C-G7 (weeks 9–10, rerun in 13–14, two-agent oracle and latency).** Agents A and B differ in allowlists, remotes, apps, workspaces, taint, leases, asks and spend. Canaries go in B's hosts, remote ids, app names, task, ref and fact ids, a decide label, a human-written `@id`, an approver name and B's CONNECT host: none appears in any byte A sees, and no IP address appears. For every id A sends (in a submit, an `ask.quote` item or an `ask.batch` member), the code and bytes A gets are identical whether B's item exists or not, apart from denial ids. Latency: for `app.status`, `app.restart`, `workspace.restore`, `repo.push{remote}`, `ask.status{id}`, `memory.forget`, `bus.task.get` and `git fetch`, 10,000 interleaved probes from A, each naming either B's live item or a missing one: every `NOT_FOUND_OR_NOT_OWNED` leaves 200–205 ms after receipt, and a two-sample KS test on A's timings gives p > 0.01; 0 `PAD_OVERRUN` at 50 concurrent tasks. `NEEDS_SIGNATURE` from decide and from a missing permit both leave at 2 s or later, KS p > 0.01. `app.deploy` of a name B uses creates A's own app, and A's outputs show no M, uid or port. A can't read DENY, WITNESS or GATE_MISMATCH records through `memory.read`.
- **C-G8 (weeks 13–14, bus and memory).** A tainted shared-scope `memory.write` gives `MEMORY_QUARANTINED` with `isError: false`, 0 asks and 0 committed rows; a tainted own-scope write by a task executor with W tainted returns `{bound: true}` and no denial; the same write from an attach session returns `MEMORY_QUARANTINED`. Hop 5, a 6th child, a cycle and cancel on a completed task each give `BUS_REFUSED` with the right `what`; the cancel case keeps the `TaskNotCancelableError` shape. An originator's `bus.task.get` and cancel, a message to a linked peer, and a `skills:` claim still get Run. `LEASE_HELD` names no holder and always says 30000. A child's `NEEDS_SIGNATURE` names no ancestor task or agent.
- **C-G9 (weeks 15–16, benchmark).** Every blocked step gives a coded denial within 25 s, with no silent hang. At least 95% of tasks that hit a denial follow the remedy or name the code in their result. The median number of model turns from a denial to the next useful action is at most 2. With a scripted approver that answers at minute 5, 0 asks are lost because their task ended. 0 asks for ops the floor rejects outright, and 0 re-asks of a denied match key. Asks per 100 with denials on are at most the figure with denials off, on the same 30 tasks. 0 `GATE_MISMATCH` on clean tasks. A canary scan of the 20 injection tasks finds no untrusted byte in any denial a clean task sees.

### 5.6 PLAN weeks

| Weeks (PLAN row) | Days | What lands |
|---|---|---|
| 4 | 3.5 | Codes table and codegen; `ops.Deny` and the schema; the in-task check and re-render; ULID ids; gate and MCP mapping; DENY schema and sink check; fuzzers. C-G1. |
| 5–6 | 2.0 | HTTP-aware forwarders with the 5 s retry; the in-task log; `agentbox why`; the proxy env; `--dns=none` and the image `resolv.conf`; the eight fixed lines; the witness FIFO (create, hold, drain, remove). C-G2. |
| 7–8 | 2.0 | Proxy refusals; rootd resolve reasons; the 10 s dial budget in proxy, keys and git; keys envelopes and header strip; per-agent day shares; git `text/plain` bodies and `pre-receive` lines; the quarantine-size cap; the git 404 floor. C-G3, C-G4. |
| 9–10 | 3.25 | Cedar and floor mapping; owner-scoped resolution; the synthetic Task; reply floors and `PAD_OVERRUN`; `NEEDS_SIGNATURE`; `pending.result`; `pending.deny`; `task_denials` and Cedar-deny forwarding; the witness check and alert; `agentbox task show`. C-G5–C-G7. The match key and `ask.status` land with D in the same weeks. |
| 11–12 | 0.25 | Per-owner app names: `app-<M>` users, `svc:<agent>-<app>`. |
| 13–14 | 0.5 | Bus and memory codes; bus ULIDs; the C-G7 rerun. C-G8. |
| 15–16 | 0.5 | Behavior metrics. C-G9. |
| **Total** | **~12** | No new socket, service or broker endpoint. |

### 5.7 Residual risks
1. **The ask count doesn't drop.** C removes waste the model leaves out: futile asks, re-asks after a no, duplicate PRs, asks lost when a task exits, and silent hangs.
2. **Stock clients hide the reason.** curl, git (libcurl) and Chromium show only the CONNECT status; npm and undici behavior is unchecked. They rely on `agentbox why` and the task result. How Claude Code, Codex and opencode show MCP `isError` text, and whether they honor `x-should-retry`, can change between versions; the gates pin the CLI versions.
3. **errno-only limits carry no code:** seccomp `EOPNOTSUPP`, `EDQUOT`, `EROFS`, `ENETUNREACH`, pids `EAGAIN`, and the `RuntimeMaxSec` SIGTERM. Mid-stream cuts (an allowlist revocation, a firewall reload) end in a reset with no denial.
4. **Task code can forge its own log, `result.denials` and witness lines.** agentboxd re-renders task-reported denials from the registry, and witness lines are grammar-limited registry tokens. A forgery buys at most one alert from a clean task, and that alert is itself a signal.
5. **The coarse code is still an oracle.** Run, ask or refuse, and whether a name resolves publicly, stay observable as in v5.2. Reply floors cover only `NOT_FOUND_OR_NOT_OWNED`, `NEEDS_SIGNATURE` and quote-shaped replies; other codes can differ in time, but carry only own-scope facts. A reply past its floor under heavy load leaks; it is counted (`PAD_OVERRUN`) and gated at 0.
6. **`BUSY` keeps one box-wide bit.** No safe partition exists for one OS lock and 3 CAS-binding slots. Each toggle needs a human-visible ask and lasts up to 15 min, and colluding agents already share wider shared-kernel timing channels. Day shares cost flexibility: an idle agent's share isn't lent to a busy one.
7. **Repo and project names are not secret** in v5.2: `repo.create` can tell a free name from a taken one. The `NOT_FOUND_OR_NOT_OWNED` guarantee covers the kinds listed in §5.2 only.
8. **A compromised agentboxd** can still choose wrong codes, ids and numbers within the grammar, drop requests, and forward consistent fake Cedar denials to rootd. The witness shows those to a human but raises no automatic alert, because rootd doesn't run Cedar. Gate-only classes (reads, coordination, memory) are recorded, not checked. v2: `agentbox-gate`.
9. **Witness gaps:** lines lost during a rootd restart (that witness is marked incomplete and raises no alert); the 64 KiB cap (an overflow is flagged `GATE_MISMATCH{flooded}`, so it can't buy silence); and no alert for a task that becomes tainted before it stops. The witness also proves nothing about a task that colludes: its own uid can withhold lines or call the gate socket directly. A compromised agentboxd that writes the task's prompt can make it collude, which the v5.2 bound already allows (§5.2). v2: `agentbox-gate`.
10. **Remedy hints may raise `net.allow_domain` asks** from honest agents. The caps bound it, and C-G9 measures it.
11. **DENY audit is best-effort.** Denials during an auditd outage or in disk-full mode are not recorded, and coalescing hides exact repeat timing. Effects still fail closed.
12. **Codes are permanent.** A bad early name needs an alias row, never a rename.

## 6. Proposal D: ask budget, quotes and delivery batches

### 6.1 Problem
- 74 of 100 tasks deliver a push plus a PR, or a push plus a PR reply. In v5.2 each delivery costs two separate signatures: the push under the taint rule (every task is tainted) and the always-signed `gh.*` write. That is 148 of the 182.7 asks per 100 tasks.
- An agent can't see what an op will cost before it runs it. It finds asks by hitting them, and a confused or injected task can spray asks up to the caps (3 open per task, 20 per agent per 24 h).
- Delegated children ask like top-level tasks. Nothing ties a child's asks to what its parent was allowed.

### 6.2 Design
**Ask budget** (rootd, per task). Each ask, single or batch, uses 1 from the task's budget when its row is created.

| Task origin | Budget | Set by |
|---|---|---|
| admin (`agentbox task new`) | `--asks N`, 0 to 10; default `ask.per_task` = 3 | the human, on `rootd-admin.sock` |
| daemon | `ask.per_daemon_task` = 1, inside a box-wide pool of `ask.daemon_per_day` = 5 | rootd |
| parent, `to:` routing | the `asks: k` grant in `bus.task.send` (default 0), moved from the parent's budget in the same transaction and never returned | the parent |
| parent, `skills:` claim | 0 | fixed |

**Three new ops.**
- **`ask.quote{ops}`** (read). The cost of 1 to 8 planned ops before acting, computed by the submit's own floor function and Cedar pass with fewer inputs: no decide, no network, no file or repo reads, no writes. A missing input counts at its worst case. Replies use coarse, closed enums and carry no agent-wide value. Each item counts against per-task, per-trace and per-agent quote counters, and quote-and-vanish patterns raise alerts.
- **`ask.status{id?, waitMs?}`** (read). The task's ask budget, its `taint` view, its open asks, and, with an id, that ask's state and agentbox-generated results. It can wait up to 25 s. It folds in the `op.get` op from C and the `taint.status` op from B (R11).
- **`ask.batch{shape, ops}`** (live, signed, CLI only). One signature for one delivery. Only two shapes exist:

| Shape | Members | Rules |
|---|---|---|
| `push+pr` | 1 `repo.push`, then 1 `gh.pr.create` | One registered remote of the agent, any visibility. The PR's `head` is the pushed ref, and its bound head OID is the pushed OID. |
| `push+reply` | 1 `repo.push`, then 1 to 3 `gh.pr.comment` | One registered remote with visibility `private`. Every reply names the same PR number. The agent supplies it (from its prompt or `gh.pr.read`), each reply binds it in its own H, and the human types it before signing to confirm it. |

Nothing else ever joins a batch: not `gh.issue.comment`, `net.allow_domain`, `app.*`, `file.write`, os, admin, `memory.*`, `workspace.*` or `repo.merge`. Each member keeps its own P, binder, re-check, taint epochs and INTENT; the batch H commits to the ordered list of member H values; the human signs once, under its own SSHSIG namespace, after paging through every member and typing the member count. Execution is ordered, not atomic. A human can deny a batch and strike only the members they object to.

**One match key** (R6). `matchKey` = SHA-256(JCS{op, structural args, bound inputs}). Free text (`reason`, `title`, `body`, `content`, commit messages) is left out; bound inputs keep the body hashes, taken over the agent's own text before rootd appends the `gh.*` op marker (the marker holds the op id, so it would make every key unique). The same key drives dedupe of open asks, the rule that a human no is final for the task, and the rule that an executed push or PR returns its stored result.

**Why the red-team fixes hold.**
- No agent-wide value reaches a clean task for free. The per-agent cap surfaces only at submit, as an audited denial, exactly as in v5.2.
- Every quote-shaped read (`ask.quote`, and an `ask.batch` refused at admission) feeds the same counters, caps, tripwire line and alerts, per task, per trace and per agent. A budget-0 child can't map for free, because its quotes raise the trace counter.
- The reason enum doesn't separate a custom policy, push-policy coverage, spend or decide from the floor default. `already_allowed` reveals no more than a CONNECT probe already does: a refused CONNECT is visible, and costs nothing.
- A comment can't ride a wanted push + PR: `push+pr` holds no comments, issue comments never batch, and replies batch only on a private remote, to one PR the human names.
- A denied member can't come back unchanged as a single: one match key across singles and members.
- A compromised agentboxd is bounded in attention by the per-agent caps plus the small daemon pool, and the 40-reviewed-ops bound is stated in §10.

**Not adopted** (from the judged designs): an egress batch family (R3: it saves 0 asks in the model and lets a deputy host ride with wanted hosts); phone-eligible batches of app files (they break §5.1's "reversible is the only phone input"); a shared per-trace ask pool (a tainted child could drain or signal its parent); returning GitHub-assigned numbers from `ask.status` (R12).

### 6.3 Spec changes (ready to paste)
Shared spots: M1 (§0.8), M3 (§5.2 result codes), M4 (`net.allow_domain`), M5 (§5.4 budget, caps, match key), M6 (§5.5), M10 (§9), M11 (§10), M12 (§11), M13 (§13), M15 (App. D), M16 (App. E).

1. **§4.2 Tasks, Provenance list, new bullet after `origin: daemon`:**
   ```markdown
   - **Ask budget** (rootd, per task; §5.4). Each ask, single or `ask.batch`, uses 1 of the task's budget when its row is created.

     | Task origin | Budget | Set by |
     |---|---|---|
     | admin (`agentbox task new`) | `--asks N`, 0 to 10; default `ask.per_task` = 3 | the human, on `rootd-admin.sock` |
     | daemon | `ask.per_daemon_task` = 1, inside the box-wide `ask.daemon_per_day` = 5 | rootd |
     | parent, `to:` routing | the `asks: k` grant in `bus.task.send` (default 0), moved from the parent's budget in the same transaction and never returned | the parent |
     | parent, `skills:` claim | 0 | fixed |

     `budget.set` can raise a live task's budget, up to 10. `task_registry` keeps `asks_budget`, `asks_used`, `asks_granted`, `asks_created`, `quote_items` and `quote_deny`; `trace_quota` keeps the per-trace sums (App. E). `agentbox task show <id>` shows the task's asks with their final states and their fine reasons (`always`, `tainted`, `tainted_commits`, `remote_not_private`, `no_push_policy`, `spend`, `review`), the task and trace quote counters, cap denials, and the witness lines (§5.6). It reads rootd and audit, never agentboxd. Agents see only the coarse reasons (§5.6).
   ```
2. **§5.1 Operation registry**, add:
   ```markdown
   - **Batch and quote fields.** Each op declares its batch role (`push+pr`, `push+reply`, or none) and whether it is quotable.
   - **Batch check.** A golden test pins the shapes to exactly `push+pr` = {`repo.push`, `gh.pr.create`} and `push+reply` = {`repo.push`, 1 to 3 `gh.pr.comment`, private remote only}, the text caps (16 KiB of agent text per member, 64 KiB per batch), and that every member op has `reversible=false`, no CAS-writing binder and no phone path. rootd refuses to start on a mismatch.
   - **Quote check.** The quotable set is every Safe, live and os op an agent may submit, plus `memory.write` and `memory.forget`. The always-exact set (ops whose cost a quote can compute from its own inputs) is pinned too.
   ```
3. **§5.2 Request flow.**
   - H formula: insert `quotes, batch,` after `updatePolicyDigest,`. Under the flow block, add: "`quotes` = {taskItems, taskDeny, traceItems, traceDeny}: this task's and this trace's quote counters at bind time. Every P carries it. `batch` = {id, idx, n} for a batch member; absent otherwise. The renderer's tripwire line shows the trace totals: `quotes by this trace: <N> items, <D> deny`."
   - Result codes: M3.
   - Add after Binding admission:
     ````markdown
     **Quotes (`ask.quote{ops}`).** A read op for 1 to 8 quotable ops (§5.1), own task only. rootd runs the submit's own floor function, and agentboxd its Cedar pass, with fewer inputs: no decide call, no name resolution, no GitHub call, no `osbuild-fetch`, no read of any workspace, `git/`, `cas/` or `/srv` file, no binder write, and no taint outside the caller's effective set. A skipped check that can raise the class counts at its worst case, with reason `unchecked`. A skipped check that can only refuse counts as passed. Either way the item is `exact: false`. Args may be partial.
     - Per item: `cost` ∈ {`run`, `ask_phone`, `ask_cli`, `quarantine`, `deny`, `none`}; `reason`: one of {`tainted`, `signed`, `unchecked`} for asks, {`tainted`, `shared_scope`} for quarantine, the submit's own §5.6 code for deny, `already_allowed` for none; `exact`; and `shape`, the batch shape the item could join, or none. A `memory.write` that would bind quotes as `run`.
     - With 2 or more items: `batch = {ok, shape, asks}`, or the `ADMISSION_REJECTED` what that `ask.batch` would return.
     - The reply also carries `asks` and `taint` (§5.4).
     - Every item counts against this task's quote counter (`ask.quote_items_task`, 32) and its trace's (`ask.quote_items_trace`, 64) in one transaction; deny items also count as `quote_deny`. Over either cap gives `CAP_REACHED{quote_items_task|quote_items_trace}`, and nothing is returned.
     - rootd writes one QUOTE record and replies after its ACK, at receipt + 2 s. A quote writes no pending row, nonce, CAS object or taint record, and sends no ntfy. One quote per task is in flight at a time. With auditd down: `AUDIT_UNAVAILABLE`.

     **Batches (`ask.batch{shape, ops}`).** One signature for one delivery. Class live, Cedar group SideEffect, `reversible=false`, CLI only.

     | Shape | Members | Rules |
     |---|---|---|
     | `push+pr` | 1 `repo.push`, then 1 `gh.pr.create` | One registered remote of the agent, any visibility. The PR's `head` is the pushed ref, and its bound head OID is the pushed OID. |
     | `push+reply` | 1 `repo.push`, then 1 to 3 `gh.pr.comment` | One registered remote with visibility `private`. Every reply names the same PR number. |

     - No other op is ever a member. `gh.issue.comment`, `net.allow_domain`, `app.*`, `file.write`, os, admin, `memory.*`, `workspace.*` and `repo.merge` stay singles.
     - Each member's agent-controlled text is at most 16 KiB, and the batch's at most 64 KiB.
     - **The `push+reply` PR number.** The agent supplies it as the PR number argument of each `gh.pr.comment` member. It takes it from its prompt or from `gh.pr.read` output. `gh.pr.read` taints the caller before any output (§5.3), and a task that answers review comments reads them with it anyway. `ask.status` never returns the number (§5.3). At admission each member binds it into its own H as one of its canonical args, next to the remote's `remote_url` and `gh_node_id`, which rootd binds from the `remotes` row as in v5.2. Replies that name different numbers get `ADMISSION_REJECTED{batch_shape}`. The number the human types at signing only confirms the bound value (§5.4 step 7): it never enters P or H, and any other number signs nothing. A number that names no PR on the remote fails at execution with `REMOTE_REJECTED{not_found}`.
     - **Admission.** agentboxd runs Cedar for each member as its own action, per chain member. rootd checks the shape, the remote, the head rule, the visibility rule and the text caps, then runs binding admission, the binders and the floor per member. Batchable ops have no CAS-writing binder, so admission writes nothing. Any refusal refuses the whole batch with `{idx, code}` (codes from §5.6; missing and other-principal resources give `NOT_FOUND_OR_NOT_OWNED`, byte-identical) and writes no pending row. Before it replies, rootd charges one quote item per attempted member and one `quote_deny` against the task and trace counters, under the same caps, with a QUOTE record marked `batchAdmissionDeny`. A batch whose member matches an open single gets `ADMISSION_REJECTED{batch_member_open}`. Every `ask.batch` reply leaves at receipt + 2 s.
     - **Rows.** On success rootd inserts one P per member, each bound as if submitted alone and carrying `batch{id, idx, n}` in its H, plus one batch P of op `ask.batch` with H = SHA-256(JCS{name, shape, remote, members: [{idx, name, h}], agent, task, chain, trace, quotes, nonce, expiresAt}). The batch uses 1 ask of the task budget and `n` reviewed ops of the agent's 24 h count. The reply is one `NEEDS_SIGNATURE` with the batch id.
     - **Approval.** The CLI signs hex H with `ssh-keygen -Y sign -n agentbox-approve-batch-v1`. Batch rows verify only under that namespace, and singles only under `agentbox-approve-v1`. In one `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`, rootd inserts all n+1 nonces and moves all n+1 rows to `approved`, with checked row counts; anything else rolls back and replies `approval_stale`. A member can't be approved alone. WebAuthn is refused (`reversible=false`).
     - **Execution.** Ordered, not atomic. Each member runs its own claim, re-check, INTENT (which also carries the batch H), effect and RESULT. The push's RESULT comes before the next member's INTENT. A member that is refused or fails stops the batch: later members become `refused` with `BATCH_ABORTED`, earlier effects stay, and the batch row takes that member's state. After a rootd restart, a member with no INTENT becomes `refused` with `BATCH_ABORTED`; nothing re-runs.
     - **Deny.** `pending.deny{id, strike?}` on a batch denies every member. Struck members get reason `ASK_DENIED`, final for their match key in this task (§5.4). The others get `batch_denied` and may be asked again. The CLI requires `--strike <idx>,…` or `--all`.
     ````
4. **§5.3 Ops.** Add rows:
   ```markdown
   | `ask.quote{ops}` | read | allowed; own task only; output generated by agentbox, so untainted; counted and capped per task and per trace; reply at receipt + 2 s (§5.2) | n/a | n/a |
   | `ask.status{id?, waitMs?}` | read | allowed; own task only. Returns `asks`, `taint` and the task's open ask ids; with `id`, also that row's `{id, op, state, code, channel, expiresAt, result, members}`. `result` holds only agentbox-generated outputs (OIDs, digests, gen id, remote id), never GitHub-assigned numbers or URLs. `waitMs` (at most 25000) returns early when the row leaves `pending`, `approved` or `executing`. 1 call per second and one open wait per task; not audited; not a quote. Untainted while agentboxd is honest (§5.2 bound). | n/a | n/a |
   | `ask.batch{shape, ops}` | live | §5.2 Batches; signature, CLI only | each member's bound inputs; the member H list | per member |
   ```
   Add a bullet under the table: "**Batch shapes.** `push+pr` and `push+reply` only (§5.2). Never mixed with any other op."
5. **§5.4 Approvals.**
   - Ask budget, caps, match key, budget view, task end and isolation: M5.
   - CLI rendering, new step 7:
     ```markdown
     7. For `ask.batch`: a header with the shape, the remote and its visibility (a non-private remote is flagged `DELIVERS TO A <visibility> REMOTE`), one summary line that lists the member ops in order, the tripwire line from the bound counters, and the words "ordered, not atomic". Then one page per member, each with that member's own template, untrusted text and footer. The human types the member count, and for `push+reply` the PR number that every reply member binds, before the key signs. As in step 4, a typed value only confirms what P already holds: it never enters P or H, and the agent's bound number stays the one that runs. Any other answer signs nothing. Golden tests add: a member string holding a fake `[2/2] net.allow_domain evil.example:443` line, ESC/CSI or U+202E leaves the header, the summary, the bound-field lines, the footers and both prompts byte-identical.
     ```

7. **§7 Limits**, append: "A parent can grant asks to a `to:` child with `asks: k` in `bus.task.send` (default 0). The grant leaves the parent's budget at send and is never returned. A `skills:` child gets 0. Quote counters roll up per trace, so a grant or a fan-out can't multiply silent recon; a trace's counters and `asks_created` drive the recon alerts (§9)."
8. **§9:** M10 (QUOTE and ALERT records, batch fields). **§10:** M11. **§11:** M12. **§13:** M13.
9. **§16 Out of scope**, add: "batch shapes beyond `push+pr` and `push+reply`; reply batches on an internal or public remote; an egress (`net.allow_domain`) batch; agent-wide values in `ask.quote` or `ask.status`; a quote reason that tells a custom policy, push-policy coverage, spend or decide apart from the floor default; closing the write-capable allowlisted-host residual (v2: the GET-only package broker)".
10. **Appendix D:** M15. **Appendix E:** M16.

### 6.4 Threats and mitigations

| # | Red-team attack (severity) | How v5.3 closes it |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Exfiltration through a write-capable allowlisted egress host, driven by injection (high) | Not closed here; no safe form exists inside an ask design. It is the v5.2 CONNECT residual (§0.5, §6.2, §10), and D neither opens nor widens it. v2: the GET-only package broker. Until then: allowlist only read or API hosts for agents that read untrusted content. |
| 2 | `agentCap` as a free, silent cross-task read: a clean task polls the agent-wide cap bit (low) | The agent-wide cap is in no quote or status reply. `asks.left` comes from the task's own budget and open asks only. The per-agent cap surfaces only at submit, as v5.2's audited denial. |
| 3 | Cross-task denial: one task uses up the shared per-agent cap and starves a sibling (low) | Pre-existing v5.2 availability residual: availability only, noisy to drive (every ask is an ntfy push). The per-task budget gives partial isolation. Stated in §10. |
| 4 | Silent recon that quotes then ends without an ask, multiplied by 0-budget children (medium) | Quote counters roll up per trace (64) as well as per task (32), and per agent over 24 h. The tripwire line, bound into every P of every task in the trace, shows the trace totals. A task or trace that ends with no asks and with ≥4 deny items or ≥24 items raises `quote_recon`. A 0-budget child's quotes raise the same trace counter. |
| 5 | `ask.batch` admission as a free, uncapped binder oracle: submit batches built to be refused and read `{idx, code}` (medium) | A refused admission charges one quote item per attempted member and one `quote_deny`, under the same caps, records and alerts, and replies at receipt + 2 s. Its codes are the closed submit enum, so it reveals nothing a single submit or quote wouldn't. |
| 6 | A denied batch member re-asked as a single, stripped of the context that got it denied (medium) | One match key across singles and members (R6): an identical single gets `ASK_DENIED{id}`. A member with changed bound inputs is a new question, and its header names the earlier denial. The strike list (R7) makes a partial "no" explicit. |
| 7 | Rider comments: a wanted push + PR carries two attacker comments to a public sink under one signature (medium) | Closed by the shapes (R5). `push+pr` holds no comments. `gh.issue.comment` never batches. `push+reply` exists only on a private remote, with every reply on one PR whose number the human types. Agent text is capped at 16 KiB per member and 64 KiB per batch. A non-private remote is flagged in the header. |
| 8 | Cheap, silent policy and allowlist mapping: `policy` and `no_push_policy` reasons reveal custom policies and push-policy coverage; `already_allowed` lists the allowlist (low) | One coarse reason, `signed`, for every non-taint ask (R8). `already_allowed` stays, because the agent needs it to avoid redundant asks, and it reveals no more than a CONNECT probe. Every probe is counted, and a trace with ≥16 deny items raises `quote_enum`. |
| 9 | A compromised agentboxd spins up daemon tasks and uses batches to burn the owner's attention: 40 reviewed pages a day, not 20 (low) | A box-wide `ask.daemon_per_day` (5) bounds daemon-origin asks. The 40-reviewed-ops bound per agent per 24 h is stated in §10, not hidden. |

Other threats:
- **Taint laundering through quote or status replies.** Replies hold only enums and integers rootd picks, never an arg byte or an upstream byte. `ask.status` gives states, fixed codes and agentbox-generated outputs only. Its `taintsOutput=false` holds by schema and is checked with control and bidi payloads.
- **Signature reuse, member swap or replay.** H is over the ordered member list; each member's h is over its full P, which names its batch. The CLI recomputes every h and H. Batch rows verify only under `agentbox-approve-batch-v1`. A member can't be approved alone. All n+1 nonces burn in one transaction with checked row counts.
- **A wider phone channel.** `ask.batch` is `reversible=false`, so `pending.get` returns not-found to approvald and rootd rejects WebAuthn. The phone set stays exactly the four v5.2 ops.
- **Quotes as a load or side-effect channel.** A quote does no binder write, CAS write, `osbuild-fetch`, GitHub call, DNS lookup, decide call or file read. 8 items per call, one call in flight, 32 per task, 64 per trace.
- **Judge findings folded in:** families never mix and egress never shares a signature with delivery (no egress family at all); the batch data model is ordinary pending rows (revocation, task end, recovery and op markers work per row); completed-task asks survive until expiry (R13); children get explicit grants, never a shared pool; ops reviewed per 100 are reported beside asks.

### 6.5 Gates
- **D-G1 (week 4, registry).** If `ask.quote`, `ask.status` or `ask.batch` is missing from the registry, the Cedar schema, the JSON schemas or the MCP tools, startup is refused. A batch member op with `reversible=true`, a CAS-writing binder or a phone path refuses startup. Golden shapes are exactly `push+pr` and `push+reply` as in §5.1; golden tests pin the private-remote rule for replies, the 16 KiB and 64 KiB caps, the quotable and always-exact sets, and the phone-eligible set (still exactly the four v5.2 ops).
- **D-G2 (week 4, reply schemas).** No field in the `ask.quote` or `ask.status` reply schemas is free text, and neither has an agent-wide field. ESC/CSI, U+009B, U+202E, U+2066–U+2069 and U+E0001 in any quoted arg leave the reply byte-identical. The three schemas pass 24 h of fuzzing.
- **D-G3 (weeks 9–10, no agent-wide value, coarse reasons).** With the per-agent cap hit, a clean task's `ask.quote` and `ask.status` replies are byte-identical to the no-cap case. A task behind a custom human Cedar policy that adds an ask and a task behind the plain floor both quote `signed`, and the replies are indistinguishable. With and without a standing push policy, a push that would ask quotes `signed`.
- **D-G4 (weeks 9–10, quote = submit).** On a fixture grid with decide forced to pass: every exact quote equals the submit's `{cost, reason}`, and every inexact quote equals the submit with the skipped inputs at their worst case; 10,000 fuzzed cases give 0 mismatches. Other-agent and missing resources give byte-identical `NOT_FOUND_OR_NOT_OWNED` items in quotes and submits. Every quote reply leaves at receipt + 2 s or later.
- **D-G5 (weeks 9–10, counters and alerts).** 32 quote items in one task: the 33rd gets `CAP_REACHED{quote_items_task}`. A parent and 5 children that each quote 13 items reach 64, and the 65th item anywhere in the trace gets `CAP_REACHED{quote_items_trace}`. A task that quotes 8 items with 1 deny and ends with no ask raises no alert (honest planning). A task that quotes 6 items with 4 deny and ends with no ask raises `quote_recon{task}`. A parent with 5 budget-0 children that quote 5 items each and return with no ask in the trace raises one `quote_recon{trace}`, and every later P in the trace shows the trace totals. A trace that reaches 16 deny items raises `quote_enum`. An agent whose traces sum to 128 items in 24 h raises the per-agent alert. No alert reaches any task (X-G3).
- **D-G6 (weeks 9–10, refused admission is charged).** A batch of [valid `repo.push`, `gh.pr.create` naming a missing base] gives `{idx: 1, NOT_FOUND_OR_NOT_OWNED}`, writes no pending row, adds 2 quote items and 1 deny on the task and the trace, and writes a QUOTE record marked `batchAdmissionDeny`. Repeating it reaches the caps and gets `CAP_REACHED`. A batch that names another agent's remote is refused byte-identically to a missing remote.
- **D-G7 (weeks 9–10, shapes and rendering).** `push+pr` on a private remote: 2 pages, the count prompt, no PR prompt, 1 ask. `push+pr` on a public remote: the flagged header, still 1 ask. `repo.push` plus `gh.issue.comment`: `ADMISSION_REJECTED{batch_shape}`. `push+reply` on a public remote: `ADMISSION_REJECTED{batch_remote_not_private}`. `push+reply` on a private remote: the human must type the PR number bound in the reply members, any other number signs nothing, and the typed value appears in neither P nor H. Replies naming two PR numbers: `batch_shape`. A PR whose head differs from the pushed ref: `batch_shape`. A 17 KiB reply, or a 65 KiB batch: `batch_text_cap`.
- **D-G8 (weeks 9–10, deny is final, strike list).** The human denies [push, PR] with `--strike 2`. Re-submitting the push alone makes one new ask. The same PR body gets `ASK_DENIED{id}`. A new PR body makes one new ask, and its header names the earlier denial. `agentbox pending deny` on a batch without `--strike` or `--all` is refused by the CLI. `--all` makes both members final.
- **D-G9 (weeks 9–10, daemon pool).** With `ask.daemon_per_day=5`, the 6th ask from any `origin: daemon` task in 24 h gives the task `BUSY`, sends no ntfy, and is audited with its true reason. Human- and parent-origin tasks are unaffected.
- **D-G10 (weeks 9–10, batch execution).** Happy path: 2 pages, 1 ntfy, the push's RESULT before the PR's INTENT, both INTENTs carry the batch H, and `ask.status` shows both executed with no PR number. A PR member refused at re-check leaves the push executed and the batch `refused` at member 1. rootd killed between members: after restart, member 1 is `refused` with `BATCH_ABORTED`, and nothing re-runs. A moved workspace epoch NACKs member 0's INTENT: `STALE{taint_moved}`, and the re-pend uses no budget. Approving one member alone, signing the batch H under `agentbox-approve-v1`, and a phone attempt are each refused. Each pinned CLI (Claude Code, Codex, opencode) finishes a 25 s `ask.status` wait through `agentbox mcp` without a tool timeout; for a CLI that can't, its generated tool description says to use `agentbox ask wait` from the shell instead (R34).
- **D-G11 (weeks 13–14, grants and trace rollup).** `agentbox task new --asks 2`. A `to:` child sent with `asks: 1` leaves the parent `left=1`. The child's second ask gets `ASK_CAP{task_budget}`. The parent's `ask.status` is byte-identical before and after the child's asks. The child's quotes raise the trace counters and show in the parent's next P tripwire line.
- **D-G12 (weeks 15–16, benchmark).** The section 2.7 gate. Also: ops reviewed per 100 are within 5% of the same seeds with `ask.batch` off; task success is not lower than with the `ask.*` ops off; the injection, deputy and approval-drift sets show 0 floor bypasses through quotes or batches; a recon trace in the injection set fires `quote_recon`.

### 6.6 PLAN weeks

| Weeks (PLAN row) | Days | What lands |
|---|---|---|
| 4 | 0.5 | Batch and quote fields; the three schemas; Cedar actions; golden lists; fuzz targets. D-G1, D-G2. |
| 9–10 | 6.25 | Quote mode with the coarse reasons; per-task, per-trace and per-agent counters; the admission charge; the alerts; `ask.status` with its wait (it absorbs `op.get` and `taint.status`); batch admission, rows, the approval transaction, ordered execution and recovery; the renderer with the typed count and PR number; `agentbox-approve-batch-v1`; budgets, caps, the match key and the strike list; the daemon pool. D-G3–D-G10. Needs `repo.push` and `gh.*` from weeks 7–8. |
| 13–14 | 0.5 | Ask grants in `bus.task.send`; the per-trace rollup across children (the bus lands here). D-G11. |
| 15–16 | 0.5 | The ops-reviewed metric; the delivery-mix check; alert firing in the injection set. D-G12. |
| **Total** | **~7.75** | |

### 6.7 Residual risks
1. **108.7 asks per 100 tasks.** The new gate (section 2.7) passes; the floor of 91 always-signed asks doesn't move. Lower needs a separate proposal that changes which ops need a signature.
2. **Review load in ops doesn't shrink.** About 183 ops per 100 tasks are still approved, now in about 109 sittings. One signature covers up to 4 effects, so a human may review up to 40 ops per agent per 24 h (v5.2: 20 asks). Pages, the typed count and the typed PR number slow a skimming human but don't stop one.
3. **Bounded, visible recon remains.** Within 32 quote items per task and 64 per trace, a clean task can still learn whether each planned op runs or asks, and whether a named host is already allowed. It is counted, bound into every P in the trace and alerted on recon patterns, but a small, low-volume probe raises no alert. Ship audit off-box.
4. **Write-capable allowlisted-host exfiltration** is unchanged and not closed here (red-team 1).
5. **The shared per-agent cap** can still let one task deny a sibling's asks (red-team 3). Availability only.
6. **A batch is ordered, not atomic.** A push can land and its PR fail, leaving a branch with no PR; the agent re-asks the PR alone.
7. **Budgets can block real work.** The default of 3 stops a task that needs a 4th ask until the human runs `budget.set` or starts a follow-up with a higher `--asks`. Children get 0 asks unless granted. The daemon pool (5 a day, box-wide) can block a burst of onfailure asks.
8. **Quotes are advisory and often inexact.** Skipped checks, decide and state changes between quote and submit can raise the cost. Agents must handle an ask where the quote said `run`.
9. **Ask shopping.** A quote still shows that a phone-eligible `file.write` is cheaper than a CLI-only `app.deploy`. v5.2 already allowed that choice; quotes make it easier to find. Every permitted op must be acceptable on its own.
10. **A completed task's batch runs after its container is gone,** so the agent can't report the result; the human sees it in `agentbox task show`.
11. **The gain depends on the CLIs following the generated tool descriptions.** Unmeasured until weeks 15–16.

## 7. Proposal E: delivery lane (Rejected (owner, 2026-11-20))

**Rejected (owner, 2026-11-20).** The owner rejected Proposal E: it cuts asks only from about 109 to about 91 per 100 tasks with 3 agents (108.7 to 91.0, section 2.9); it needs a GitHub Enterprise Cloud org and a CI-less mirror repo; and 12 red-team attacks were still open (section 7.5b). It is kept here as a record. ARCHITECTURE.md v5.3 and PLAN.md v5.3 hold none of it.

**Status: opt-in, Enterprise Cloud mirrors only (R77).** A second red-team of the round-2 lane found 12 working attacks. Section 7.5b lists each with its decision, and this section carries the fixes (R82–R89). The lane ships only if E-G2, E-G3, E-G5 and E-G6 pass on a live Enterprise Cloud test org; if any fails, v5.3 ships A–D only. Its effect is 92.0 human-gate actions per 100 tasks with three agents, a once-daily review and every remote an inert Enterprise Cloud mirror, and 108.7 for CI repos (section 2.9, R78).

This is round 2 of the inert same-repo delivery lane (base: Design 2, round 1). Every working red-team attack from round 1 is closed or explicitly narrowed, and three round-1 overclaims are re-grounded. The central correction: the lane's real guarantees are GitHub-side controls the delivery App cannot evade, enforced by GitHub against the App's own token, not on-box checks. A fully compromised `agentbox-git` can push `agent/*` branches and open draft PRs within the App's permissions, but it cannot merge, cannot push to the default branch, cannot create or move any other branch or tag, cannot push a control-file path, cannot plant an auto-run-on-open payload, cannot change settings, and cannot cause a review-time RCE, because each of those is blocked by a bound, zero-bypass enterprise ruleset or org policy. This revision adds the fixes for the second red-team's 12 attacks: Enterprise Cloud only, zero-bypass rulesets on every ref, verifier-signed snapshots at the floor, readable Codespaces outcomes, the bound org IP allow list with the owner's review IPs, no other app installation, and no `issues:write`.

### 7.1 Problem
- After Proposal D, 74 of 100 tasks still spend one `ask.batch` signature on delivery (section 2.2). Every PR to the agent's own registered private remote needs a laptop signature, even when the owner would review and merge on GitHub anyway. The human gate sits on the box (the SSHSIG over the `push+pr` batch), and then the owner reviews again on GitHub before merge: two gates for one delivery.
- A no-ask delivery is only safe if the remote can't turn a pushed branch or draft PR into code execution, a merge, or a changed setting without the human. The main risk is GitHub's own compute: a same-repo branch gets repository secrets on `on: push` / `on: pull_request`; a fork PR does not by default. A committed `.devcontainer`, `.vscode` or Codespaces payload runs with the owner's credentials the moment the owner opens a Codespace at review time.
- v5.2 and v5.3 have no op that pushes a branch and opens a PR as one taint-exempt action: `gh.pr.create` is always signed, CLI only (§5.3), and `repo.push` asks whenever the task is tainted (every task is, section 2.1).

### 7.2 Design
Base: a new registry op `repo.deliver`, laneOnly, that a tainted task may call with no ask. It pushes the task's `agent/<executor>/<slug>` branch and opens a DRAFT pull request on a remote the owner registered as a private delivery remote. The on-box human gate moves to the owner reviewing and merging on GitHub. Every guarantee is bound to a GitHub-side, App-unbypassable control (a ruleset or org policy GitHub enforces against the App's own token), re-read before each delivery, and watched off-box.

| Part | What it does |
|---|---|
| `repo.deliver` | A new live op, `laneOnly`, `reversible=false`, never an `ask.batch` member, never phone-eligible. Cedar `deliver-lane` permits it and `tainted-ask` exempts it; `risk-ask`, `budget-ask` and human forbids still apply. The taint exemption set is exactly `{repo.deliver}`, pinned by a golden test. |
| Lane grant | `lane.set{remote, settings, settingsHash, verifierKey, expiresAt}`, SSHSIG-signed by the owner at enrollment and every 30 days, like `remote.set` or `push.policy.set`: human-started, not an ask. It approves the `push+pr` batch for that remote; there is no per-delivery signature. |
| rootd floor | Requires a live non-daemon task, the executor's own enrolled private delivery remote with an active, unexpired, non-`forbidden` lane, a new create-only `agent/<executor>/<slug>` ref at exactly `oid`, the title/body grammar, and a verifier-signed `lane-settings/v1` snapshot under 5 minutes old whose repo `node_id` and `settingsHash` match the grant. Before the hash compare, rootd parses the snapshot and evaluates a pinned predicate list itself, so a verifier bug or a stale grant signed over a bad snapshot still fails (section 7.3, R85). The `forbidden` lane state is Cedar- and agentboxd-independent (R67). |
| Binder | Over `base..head`, refuses the no-ask lane (`ADMISSION_REJECTED{lane_unavailable}`, remedy: the `push+pr` `ask.batch`) for a refused control/auto-run path, a changed source file GitHub would auto-collapse, a renderable file carrying an external resource reference, an over-cap source diff, or a commit message that fails the §5.4 escaper. |
| Delivery App | A write GitHub App (contents and pull requests write; metadata, administration, checks, statuses and deployments read; no issues, workflows, actions, secrets or environments permission; no admin write; `repository_selection=selected`) whose private key lives in the box TPM and whose installation JWT is TPM-signed. It is installed on the org, never on a user account, because the org IP allow list does not restrict an App installed on a user account. Labels go through the issues-labels endpoint, which pull requests write covers (R89). Its tokens are bound to the box egress IP by the org IP allow list, with installed-App inheritance off (section 7.3, R87). |
| Off-box verifier | A separate principal off the box, on a stable egress IP that is a named entry in the bound org IP allow list, with a verifier GitHub App (installed on the org) and an enterprise-owner classic PAT used read-only by code (section 7.3). It reads settings, rulesets with their bypass actors, the IP allow list and the audit log; signs each `lane-settings/v1` snapshot with its own key, enrolled in `lane.set`; and is the authority for `lane.revoke`. rootd checks the signature and freshness and evaluates the pinned predicates over the snapshot; it does not read GitHub (R85). On-box tripwire and sweep are best-effort liveness only. |
| Trustworthy header | rootd (not the agent) prepends an HTML header to the PR body, every agent-derived value HTML-entity-escaped and Markdown-neutralized, never inside `img`/`a`. |

**Mechanism.**
1. **Setup, once per remote** (human; about 45 to 70 min the first time). The owner makes the delivery repo an inert mirror and binds every guarantee to a GitHub-side control: a private repo in an org inside a GitHub Enterprise Cloud enterprise (enrollment refuses any other org, Team orgs included, R88), NOT a fork (`fork=false`, no parent or source), with forking disabled (`allow_forking=false`, org private-forking off) and `forks_count==0`; Actions disabled; no webhooks; Pages off; no tags and no releases; no `.devcontainer/`, `.devcontainer.json`, `.vscode/`, `.github/codespaces/`, `.github/dependabot.yml` or `.github/copilot-*` path in the default-branch tree, with the org Codespaces policy set to disabled (R86); Dependabot security updates and vulnerability alerts off, no Dependabot secret at repo or org level, code scanning default setup not configured, and Copilot coding agent disabled for the repo (R83); no GitHub App installation in the org other than the write App and the verifier App, both installed on the org and not on a user account, and OAuth app access restrictions on (R83); the enterprise rulesets of section 7.3 (the restricted-file-paths push ruleset, the default-branch ruleset that requires a pull request, one approving review, dismissal of stale approvals on push, `require_last_push_approval=true` and a review from a CODEOWNERS team of human logins that does NOT contain the App, with a catch-all `*` line as the first CODEOWNERS rule, the non-agent branch and tag rulesets, and the agent-branch ruleset), all with `enforcement=active` and zero bypass actors (R82, R84); and the org IP allow list enabled, with installed-App inheritance off and no "Managed by" entry, holding exactly the box egress IP, the verifier egress IP and the owner's review IPs (R87). The effective reader set is minimized (org base permission `none`; collaborators owner-only where possible; watchers and org owners surfaced and counted). Then `agentbox lane enroll <remote>`: the CLI renders the bound settings from a verifier-signed snapshot, the rulesets and their enforcement level and bypass actors, the effective reader set, the resolved CODEOWNERS human logins, the IP allow list entries, the installation list, and the owner-duty lines. The owner types the repo `full_name`, the effective-reader count, the resolved-CODEOWNERS count, each review IP and the verifier egress IP; type-confirms the org Codespaces policy as `disabled`, OAuth app restrictions as on and Copilot coding agent as disabled for the repo (recorded as attestations, backed only by outcome checks); then SSHSIG-signs `lane.set`, which also enrolls the verifier's snapshot key.
2. **Delivery, no ask.** The agent pushes its branch to its bare repo through `:8082`, then calls `repo.deliver{remote, ref, oid, title, body}`. The binder, over `base..head`, refuses the no-ask lane if the range touches a refused control/auto-run path (`.github/workflows/**`, `.devcontainer/**`, `.devcontainer.json`, `.vscode/**`, `.github/codespaces/**`, `.github/dependabot.yml`, `.github/copilot-*`, `CODEOWNERS`, `.gitattributes`, or the §4.2 agent-steering set; on an enrolled mirror the remedy is `HUMAN_ONLY`: apply the change by hand on the CI repo, R76); and routes it to a signed `ask.batch` (`lane_unavailable`) if the range contains any changed SOURCE file GitHub would auto-collapse (render-visible set from the head-tree gitattributes plus GitHub's linguist generated/vendored/binary heuristics); adds or changes a renderable file (`md`, `ipynb`, `svg`, `html`, `rst`) carrying any external resource reference; exceeds the human-attention source caps (25 changed source files, 600 source diff lines, 256 KiB source bytes, any single source file over 300 lines or 64 KiB, with recognized lockfiles and vendored trees excluded and summarized by depcheck); or carries a commit message that fails the §5.4 escaper. Build/manifest/test-autoload files (`package.json`, `pyproject`, lockfiles, `Makefile`, `conftest.py`, etc.) are NOT refused; they are header-flagged. Caps: 1 per trace; `lane.tainted_agent_24h` (3) per agent per 24 h; `lane.per_remote_day` (3) per remote per UTC day, a hard cap that nothing unlocks (R80); `lane.open_per_agent` (3) open lane PRs per authoring agent; and `lane.open_per_box` (5) box-wide.
3. **Admission.** rootd inserts a `push+pr` batch on the v5.3 path (section 6.3), approved by the lane grant (`signer lane:<remote>`, the monthly SSHSIG, not a per-delivery signature): member 0 `repo.push{createOnly}`, member 1 `gh.pr.create{draft:true, base=default_branch}`. The commit author and committer identity are forced to a fixed agentbox lane identity (noreply), so no real-person avatar renders.
4. **Execution, ordered, with recovery.** Member 0's re-check mints the write token NARROWED to the one bound repo and verifies the returned token's repo scope is exactly that repo before any push. rootd then requires a verifier-signed `lane-settings/v1` snapshot, relayed by `agentbox-git`, that is under 5 minutes old, names the bound repo `node_id`, and hashes to the grant's `settingsHash`; otherwise the delivery gets `lane_unavailable` (R85). rootd checks the signature and freshness and evaluates the pinned predicate list of section 7.3 over the parsed snapshot; it does not read GitHub: it has no network, and anything `agentbox-git` reads for it could be forged (attack 20).
