The verifier builds each snapshot off-box from: `fork=false`/`forks_count==0`/`allow_forking=false`, Pages off, Actions off, no `workflows` ruleset rule, an empty tag list, no release (draft included) from `GET /repos/{o}/{r}/releases`, every ref in `GET /repos/{o}/{r}/git/refs` either the default branch or under `refs/heads/agent/`, no dev container, Dependabot or Copilot config path in the default-branch tree, 0 codespaces for the repo, Dependabot security updates and alerts off, 0 Dependabot secrets, code scanning default setup not configured, every lane ruleset present with `enforcement=active`, `bypass_actors=[]` and the App at `current_user_can_bypass=never`, the PR-required rule, `require_last_push_approval`, the resolved CODEOWNERS membership, the restricted-file-paths rule, the effective reader set, the org installation list (each on the org, not a user account), the org's full repo list and the write App's `repository_selection=selected`, and the IP allow list settings (enabled, installed-App inheritance off) and entries. The on-box re-reads of the same values through `agentbox-git` stay as liveness. A mismatch or a stale snapshot suspends the lane. Then INTENT, create-only push, RESULT; member 1 PR create and labels (through the issues-labels endpoint under pull requests write). The PR number and URL stay human-only.
5. **The trustworthy header.** rootd, not the agent, prepends an HTML header with every agent-derived value HTML-entity-escaped and never placed inside `img`/`a`/`image` or as a bare URL (a Markdown-neutralization pass strips or encodes `! [ ] ( ) < > ` backtick and bare URLs, because the §5.4 terminal escaper passes the P and S categories on a Markdown surface): a diffstat; the true changed-file count with a flag when it exceeds GitHub's 300-file / 20,000-line / 1 MiB render window; the changed paths grouped with control, dependency and build files first and flagged, each renderable file flagged "may beacon on render, review as raw source only", each build/manifest/test-autoload file flagged "checking out or running this branch executes this code with your credentials"; a fixed line that all commit subjects and `Co-authored-by` / `Signed-off-by` / `Reviewed-by` trailers are agent-supplied and unverified; and the depcheck (M8) high-risk summary ranked ONLY on anomaly signals (first-seen package, new or changed install script, new maintainer/registry/scope, downgrade, hashless pin, resolved-host change), with routine version bumps shown as a separate low-risk count.
6. **Watching.** Off-box detection is primary: the off-box verifier reads the audit-log stream and repo settings on its own credential and triggers `lane.revoke` on any drift, any fork, any unexpected token use, or any push touching a restricted path; its latency bound is the stream/poll interval. It stops signing snapshots on drift, so the floor refuses deliveries within 5 minutes even if `lane.revoke` never reaches the box. It watches the audit log for `repo.fork`, `codespaces.*`, OAuth restriction changes, new app installations and `integration_installation.repositories_added`. Any ref outside the default branch and `refs/heads/agent/`, and any release, is permanent revocation. The on-box read-only path lists check suites, statuses and deployments 60 s after each push and re-reads active lanes on a short interval, but is best-effort LIVENESS only, because a compromised `agentbox-git` can forge both the write and the read path. A periodic digest lists open tainted deliveries distinctly. No auto-resume; fork, gate-gone and forking-observed are permanent and need re-enrollment.
7. **The human gate, on GitHub.** The owner reviews ONLY via the web Files-changed source view (never the rich/rendered view, never a Codespace, editor, clone, checkout or local build/test), reads the trustworthy header and the flagged files, confirms the PR's real head SHA from the commit UI against `agentbox lane show`, and reviews the on-box tainted-delivery digest before merging. GitHub refuses the merge until a human CODEOWNERS identity approves the final head, blocks direct pushes to the default branch, and dismisses that approval on a later push. The title prefix, labels and body are broker-sent and advisory only.

**Why no ask is safe (re-grounded).** The remote is inert and its merge, direct-push, control-path, Codespaces, Actions, forking and Pages behaviour are all fixed by bound, App-unbypassable GitHub-side rules, so a fully compromised broker cannot merge, cannot push to default, cannot create or move any other branch or tag, cannot plant an auto-run payload, cannot cause a review-time RCE, and cannot change settings; the only write that takes effect is a merge a human CODEOWNERS identity performs on the final head; injection into unsigned auto-run, merge-gate or render content never rides the no-ask lane; the header cannot carry a beacon; and off-box detection plus the org IP allow list bound an exfiltrated token. The floor admits only on a fresh verifier-signed snapshot, so the broker can't feed it a forged one.

**Not adopted** (from round 1). Trusting on-box drift detection against a git-broker compromise (re-grounded to off-box-primary, R55); binding only `allow_forking` rather than the fork outcome (R60); the on-box binder as the only control-path block (moved off-box to the push ruleset, R71); flagging renderable files rather than refusing them (R69); a render-window diff cap as the no-ask ceiling (replaced by human-attention source caps, R47/R48); generous caps (lowered to single digits, R65).

### 7.3 Owner preconditions on GitHub
Each precondition binds the OUTCOME, not only the config. The off-box verifier reads each one, binds it in a `lane-settings/v1` snapshot, and signs the snapshot every 60 s with its own key, enrolled in `lane.set`. rootd admits a delivery only on a verifier signature under 5 minutes old whose repo `node_id` and `settingsHash` match the grant; otherwise the delivery gets `lane_unavailable` (R85). rootd checks signatures and freshness, not GitHub: it has no network, and anything `agentbox-git` reads for it could be forged (attack 20). The on-box re-reads through `agentbox-git` are liveness only.

**rootd evaluates the preconditions itself (R85).** A hash compare alone would pass a verifier bug or a stale grant signed over a bad snapshot. So before the hash compare rootd parses `lane-settings/v1` and evaluates this pinned predicate list, golden-tested in E-G7; any false predicate gives `lane_unavailable` and an alert: `private=true`; `fork=false` with no parent or source; `forks_count=0`; `allow_forking=false`; Actions `enabled=false`; Dependabot security updates and alerts off and 0 Dependabot secrets; code scanning default setup not configured; every lane ruleset present, enterprise-level, `enforcement=active` and `bypass_actors=[]`; the push ruleset path set a superset of the refused control paths; every ref the default branch or under `refs/heads/agent/`; 0 tags and 0 releases, drafts included; the IP allow list enabled, installed-App inheritance off, no "Managed by" entry, and the entry set equal to the grant's (box egress IP, verifier egress IP, review IPs); rootd's own configured egress IP a member of that set; the org installations exactly `{write App, verifier App}`, both on the org; every org repo an enrolled lane remote and the write App's `repository_selection=selected`; CODEOWNERS with a catch-all `*` first rule, no empty-owner pattern and every owner in the bound human set. Two preconditions are attestations that no one can read back, rootd included: the org Codespaces policy and OAuth app access restrictions (and Copilot coding agent where no read exists). rootd checks only their outcomes, so the threat tables mark the attacks they cover "closed by outcome checks, policy attested", not plain "closed".
- **Enterprise Cloud only (R88).** The org must sit inside a GitHub Enterprise Cloud enterprise. The verifier confirms it through GraphQL `enterprise(slug:) { organizations }` with its enterprise-owner read credential, and enrollment refuses any other org, Team orgs included. Three controls the lane needs exist only on Enterprise Cloud: the org IP allow list, the audit log REST and GraphQL API, and enterprise rulesets, which org owners can't edit. On a Team org, org owners can always edit org rulesets, so no rule is org-owner-unbypassable, and the verifier loses its audit-log input (attack 2-10). The enterprise code-governance page documents enterprise push rulesets with file-path rules and enterprise tag rulesets (cited below). Fallback, decided now: if enterprise push rulesets with file-path rules are unavailable on the test enterprise, the lane does not ship, because an org-level ruleset is editable by org owners.
- **Registered, private and NOT a fork.** `GET /repos/{o}/{r}` must show `private=true`, `visibility=private`, `archived=false`, `fork=false` with no parent and no source, `allow_forking=false`, `forks_count==0`, `has_pages=false`, `allow_auto_merge=false` and `node_id=gh_node_id`. The org members-can-fork-private-repos setting must be off. The fork network list is not bound, because no endpoint reliably lists it. The verifier watches the audit log for `repo.fork`. Push rulesets apply to the repository's entire fork network (cited below), so a fork can't take a blocked path either. Any observed fork, any `forks_count` increment, or `allow_forking=true` observed even once is permanent, non-auto-resumable revocation requiring re-enrollment (attacks 9, 2-11); a later flip to Pages-on changes `settingsHash` and suspends.
- **No Codespaces auto-run, bound by outcomes that can be read (R86).** GitHub has no per-repository Codespaces switch, and the org setting has `PUT /orgs/{org}/codespaces/access` but no GET, so "Codespaces off" can't be re-read. The lane binds three things instead. (a) The default-branch tree has no `.devcontainer/`, `.devcontainer.json`, `.vscode/` or `.github/codespaces/` path. This is read with contents read and bound in `settingsHash`, and the push ruleset keeps these paths from being added on any ref. (b) The verifier checks that `GET /orgs/{org}/codespaces` lists 0 codespaces for the repo and that the audit log has no `codespaces.*` event for it. (c) At enrollment the owner type-confirms the org Codespaces policy as `disabled`; the record labels this an attestation, not a check. With no dev container config on any ref, a Codespace opened anyway runs no repo lifecycle command (attacks 1, 19, 2-7).
- **A restricted-file-paths PUSH ruleset** blocking `.github/workflows/**`, `.devcontainer/**`, `.devcontainer.json`, `.vscode/**`, `.github/codespaces/**`, `.github/dependabot.yml`, `.github/copilot-*`, `.gitattributes` (any location) and `CODEOWNERS` for every actor. It is an enterprise ruleset with zero bypass actors, bound in the signed snapshot. "Every actor" includes the owner: nobody can push these paths to the mirror, by a signed batch or by hand. Decision: on an enrolled mirror a control-path change is refused outright (`HUMAN_ONLY`, remedy: apply it by hand on the CI repo, never on the mirror; R76). The same zero-bypass rules mean the owner can't edit CODEOWNERS on the mirror without an enterprise owner lifting the ruleset, which changes `settingsHash`, suspends the lane and needs re-enrollment (R74). This is the off-box, App-unbypassable twin of the on-box binder refusal, so a compromised broker that bypasses `repo.deliver` still cannot push these paths (attack 19; hardens attack 12).
- **Every non-agent ref and every tag is closed to pushes (R82).** Enterprise rulesets with zero bypass actors and `enforcement=active`: (a) a branch ruleset on `~ALL`, excluding `refs/heads/agent/**` and `~DEFAULT_BRANCH`, that restricts creation, update and deletion; (b) on `~DEFAULT_BRANCH`, the PR-required rule below plus restrict deletion and block force pushes; (c) a tag ruleset on `~ALL` that restricts creation, update and deletion, which blocks published releases, because a published release needs a tag; (d) on `refs/heads/agent/**`, block force pushes and restrict deletion. Decision on (b): the default branch is left out of the update restriction, because with zero bypass actors that rule would block the owner's PR merge too; the PR-required rule already blocks direct pushes there. Branch and tag rulesets target only `refs/heads` and `refs/tags`. Nothing documented stops `contents:write` from creating other refs (`refs/notes/x`, `refs/foo/bar`) by git push or `POST /repos/{o}/{r}/git/refs`, which takes any name that starts with `refs` and has at least two slashes, or a draft release, which makes no tag and takes asset uploads under `contents:write`. No branch or tag ruleset covers either. So two outcomes are snapshot fields, read off-box with the enterprise credential: every ref in `GET /repos/{o}/{r}/git/refs` is the default branch or under `refs/heads/agent/`, and `GET /repos/{o}/{r}/releases` lists no release, draft included; the tag list is bound empty too. Any foreign ref or any release seen on an enrolled lane is permanent revocation. E-G3 tries `refs/notes/x`, `refs/foo/bar` and a draft release with an asset from the write App token; fallback: if GitHub accepts any of them, the lane does not ship, because detection after the fact is not a bound for a no-ask write. The owner can't create or delete branches on the mirror either: branch cleanup needs an enterprise owner to lift the ruleset, which changes `settingsHash`, suspends the lane and needs re-enrollment (R74). An inert mirror needs no owner branches (attack 2-1).
- **Actions disabled.** `GET /repos/{o}/{r}/actions/permissions` `enabled=false`. With `fork=false`/`forks_count==0` this removes both the same-repo and the upstream-network path to a secrets-bearing runner. No ruleset that applies to any branch may carry a `workflows` rule (`GET /repos/{o}/{r}/rules/branches/{branch}` lists the rules that apply). Enrollment refuses one, and one appearing later suspends the lane (attack 2-4, R83).
- **A merge needs a HUMAN identity on the final head AND no path around the PR.** The default-branch ruleset must require a pull request before merging (blocking direct pushes to the default branch, so the App's `contents:write` cannot bypass the PR), `required_approving_review_count>=1`, `dismiss_stale_reviews_on_push=true`, `require_last_push_approval=true`, and a CODEOWNERS rule naming a team of human logins that excludes the App. A CODEOWNERS-required review covers only files that match a pattern, so a change to an unowned path would merge on any single approval from someone with write access. The CODEOWNERS file must therefore start with a catch-all `* @<human-team>` (or named human users) as its first effective rule, no later pattern may have an empty owner list, and every owner on every line must be in the bound human set; the verifier parses the default-branch CODEOWNERS and binds this. The CODEOWNERS RESOLVED membership (team expanded to current logins), not merely the blob hash, is bound in each snapshot; or named-user CODEOWNERS entries are required so the blob fully determines identity (attack 7). Every lane ruleset must be an enterprise ruleset with zero bypass actors and report `enforcement=active` (evaluate or disabled is gate-gone and hard, non-auto-resumable revocation). The App must read `current_user_can_bypass=never`, not `exempt`, which the enum also allows. The rulesets REST API returns `bypass_actors` only to a caller with write access to the ruleset, so the App and rootd see only their own `current_user_can_bypass` and can't see a deploy key, another App or a role listed as a bypass actor. The verifier reads `bypass_actors` with its enterprise-owner read credential and binds `bypass_actors=[]` in the signed snapshot (attack 2-5, R84). The merge-gate state is in every snapshot, so it is re-checked every 60 s (attack 8). Trust assumption: whoever can edit an enterprise ruleset is in the trusted base, like the approver key.
- **Reached through two separated credentials, with the write App's installation enumerated.** A write GitHub App (permissions in section 7.2; `repository_selection=selected`, selected to exactly the registered remotes) whose private key lives in the box TPM and whose installation JWT is TPM-signed. No credential the verifier holds can be assumed to list another App's installation repos: `GET /user/installations/{installation_id}/repositories` lists only what the authenticated user's token can reach, and the on-box token is broker-controlled. So the design bounds the spread by what the org holds. The mirror org holds only lane repos. The verifier binds the org's full repo list (`GET /orgs/{org}/repos`), requires every repo in it to be an enrolled lane remote, binds the write App's `repository_selection=selected` from `GET /orgs/{org}/installations`, and watches the audit log for `integration_installation.repositories_added`. A new repo in the org changes the hash and suspends the next delivery, so the write App can reach only lane repos whatever its selected set. The verifier App's own installation repo list is bound and required equal to the org repo list. The on-box enumeration with the write App's own token is liveness only (attacks 6, 10). Per delivery, rootd mints a token narrowed to the one bound repo and verifies the returned token's repository scope is exactly that repo before any push; token narrowing is advisory and the authoritative bound is the installation's bound selected set (attack 11).
- **The org IP allow list, installed-App inheritance OFF, holding the box, the verifier and the owner's review IPs (R87).** The org IP allow list already restricts installation tokens, user-to-server tokens and personal access tokens on API access, owners included; it also covers web UI and Git access. The "installed GitHub Apps" setting does not turn that restriction on. It copies each installed App's declared IPs into the org list as entries labelled "Managed by the NAME GitHub App", which no one can edit or delete, kept in sync with whatever the App's creator declares. Turning it on would widen the list to IPs an App owner picks, and would make an exact entry set impossible to hold once any App declares an IP. The list also does not restrict an App installed on a user account, so both Apps must be installed on the org. The lane binds: the org IP allow list is enabled; installed-App inheritance is off; no entry is "Managed by" an App; and the entries are exactly three named kinds: the box egress IP, the verifier egress IP, and the owner's review IPs, each typed at enrollment and shown in the render. A list holding only the box IP would lock out both the owner's review and the verifier, whose classic PAT and App token are refused off-list; the verifier then could not sign, and the floor would refuse every delivery. The verifier gets its own entry instead of a review IP, so the two trust roles stay separate. rootd and the App can't read the list, so the verifier reads GraphQL `organization { ipAllowListEnabledSetting ipAllowListForInstalledAppsEnabledSetting ipAllowListEntries }` with its owner read credential and binds the result, and rootd checks that its own configured egress IP is one of the bound entries. An installation token exfiltrated off-box is rejected from any other IP (attacks 21, 2-8, 2-9). Residual: a token used from an owner review IP or the verifier IP, or proxied through the box's own egress, passes. The TPM covers at-rest key theft only; every TPM JWT signing is rate-limited and streamed to the off-box sink in near-real-time.
- **Audience bound as the EFFECTIVE reader set, minimized.** The org base permission is read and bound; enrollment refuses, or forces `none`, if the repo is readable by all org members. Org owners are counted, watchers surfaced, and direct collaborators and deploy keys bound; owner-only is recommended. Any widening suspends the lane (attack 5). Because the one-workspace-per-agent model (R27) lets a delivery carry ANY file the agent can read across all its projects, the owner is told at enrollment and type-confirms that this remote's audience can receive cross-project content; one project per lane-enrolled agent is recommended and a cheap project-subtree refusal is applied, but this stays a narrowing, not a content-level fix (attacks 4, 22).
- **Nothing else reacts, publishes or auto-runs (R83).** Repo hooks empty; no active org hook. No GitHub App installation in the org other than the write App and the verifier App (`GET /orgs/{org}/installations`, which needs organization administration read): a CI or deploy App (CircleCI, Vercel and the like) builds every pushed branch with its own secrets, and Actions-off does not stop it. Both Apps must be installed on the org, not on a user account. Enrollment refuses another installation, and one appearing later suspends the lane. The refusal is org-wide, because the lane's credentials can't read other installations' repo selections. GitHub's own runners are bound too. Dependabot runs on Actions runners even when Actions is disabled for the repo or org (cited below) and its jobs get Dependabot secrets, so the lane binds Dependabot security updates off (`GET /repos/{o}/{r}/automated-security-fixes`), vulnerability alerts off (`GET /repos/{o}/{r}/vulnerability-alerts` 404), 0 Dependabot secrets at repo and org level, and no `.github/dependabot.yml` on any ref (default-tree check plus the push ruleset). Copilot coding agent sessions default to an Actions-based environment, but Actions-off is not assumed to stop them, so Copilot coding agent must be disabled for the repo: an owner attestation, checked by outcome (0 Copilot sessions, no Copilot-authored PR, and the ref ruleset refusing any non-`agent/**` branch it would create), with `.github/copilot-*` blocked by the push ruleset. Code scanning default setup must read `not-configured` (`GET /repos/{o}/{r}/code-scanning/default-setup`). OAuth app access restrictions must be on: an owner attestation at enrollment, which the verifier checks in the audit log, where `org.disable_oauth_app_restrictions` or any OAuth app approval event suspends the lane (attacks 2-2, 2-3). No `workflows` ruleset rule; the default-branch head has no deployments, statuses or foreign check suites; no `copilot_code_review` rule; Pages off; no dev container paths; Actions off.
- **An OFF-BOX verifier with its own credentials is the primary detection authority.** It runs off the box, from a stable egress IP that is a named entry in the bound org IP allow list (typed at enrollment, bound in the snapshot, shown in the render), with two credentials: a verifier GitHub App with exactly organization administration read, members read, organization webhooks read and repository webhooks read, plus repository metadata, contents and administration read on the bound repo; and an enterprise-owner read credential for the reads the App can't make (bypass actors, the IP allow list, the org codespaces list, the audit log, the enterprise organization list, the org repo list). That credential is a write-capable `admin:org` classic PAT (with `read:enterprise` and `read:audit_log`), because the IP allow list and org codespaces reads require `admin:org`. Its code only issues GETs and GraphQL queries, so it is used read-only by code, held off-box, and in the trusted base (section 7.8 residual 6), as are the verifier host and its snapshot signing key. It signs the snapshots, stops signing on any drift, fork, control-path push or off-IP token use, and triggers `lane.revoke` / the rootd-floor `forbidden` state. Because the floor needs a signature under 5 minutes old, a drifted repo stops receiving deliveries within 5 minutes even if the revoke never reaches the box. On-box tripwire and sweep are best-effort liveness only, because a compromised `agentbox-git` drives both the write and the read-only path over its single egress and can forge a consistent clean snapshot (attack 20).
- **Signed and kept fresh.** The owner SSHSIG-signs `lane.set` over the snapshot hash and the verifier's public key after typing the `full_name`, the effective-reader count, the resolved-CODEOWNERS count, each owner review IP and the verifier egress IP, and after type-confirming the Codespaces, OAuth and Copilot coding agent attestations. The grant expires after 30 days. Owner duties shown at signing: review ONLY via GitHub's web Files-changed source view, never the rich/rendered view, a Codespace, an editor, a clone, a checkout, or a local build/test (any of these executes the branch's code with your credentials); read the trustworthy header and every flagged file as source before you merge; confirm the PR head against `agentbox lane show`, not the body; review the on-box tainted-delivery digest before merging; keep the off-box verifier and its audit-log stream running.

**Cited GitHub facts** (checked 2026-11). Each claim the lane rests on, its source and the gate that pins it on the live test org.

| Claim | Source | Gate |
|---|---|---|
| Secrets are not passed to the runner when a workflow is triggered from a forked repository; a same-repo branch's workflows get them | https://docs.github.com/en/actions/security-for-github-actions/security-guides/using-secrets-in-github-actions | E-G2 (Actions off, so no workflow runs at all) |
| Push rulesets block pushes to a private or internal repository and its entire fork network; restrict creations, updates and deletions and block force pushes apply to branches and tags; tag rulesets exist | https://docs.github.com/en/repositories/configuring-branches-and-merges-in-your-repository/managing-rulesets/available-rules-for-rulesets | E-G2, E-G3 |
| `bypass_actors` is returned only to callers with write access to the ruleset; `current_user_can_bypass` is one of `always`, `pull_requests_only`, `never`, `exempt` | https://docs.github.com/en/rest/repos/rules | E-G5 |
| Only organizations that use GitHub Enterprise Cloud can use IP allow lists; the org list applies to web UI, Git and API access for all members, owners included, and restricts personal access tokens, OAuth app tokens and "GitHub App user-to-server or installation tokens" | https://docs.github.com/en/organizations/keeping-your-organization-secure/managing-security-settings-for-your-organization/managing-allowed-ip-addresses-for-your-organization | E-G6 |
| With the installed-Apps setting on, "IP addresses from installed GitHub Apps are added to your allow list", described as "Managed by the NAME GitHub App", and "you cannot edit, delete, or disable" them; "IP allow lists don't currently restrict access when a GitHub App is installed on a user account" | https://docs.github.com/en/organizations/keeping-your-organization-secure/managing-security-settings-for-your-organization/managing-allowed-ip-addresses-for-your-organization (checked 2026-11-20) | E-G6 (inheritance off; an off-IP installation token refused) |
| Enterprise push rulesets exist for private and internal repos and cover the whole fork network; they block pushes by file and folder paths; enterprise tag rulesets exist ("New tag ruleset") | https://docs.github.com/en/enterprise-cloud@latest/admin/enforcing-policies/enforcing-policies-for-your-enterprise/enforcing-policies-for-code-governance and https://docs.github.com/en/enterprise-cloud@latest/repositories/configuring-branches-and-merges-in-your-repository/managing-rulesets/available-rules-for-rulesets | E-G2 (fallback: no enterprise push ruleset with path rules, so the lane does not ship) |
| GitHub runs Dependabot jobs on Actions runners, "bypassing both Actions policy checks and disablement at the repository or organization level"; Copilot coding agent sessions "default to an GitHub Actions-based environment" | https://docs.github.com/en/code-security/dependabot/working-with-dependabot/about-dependabot-on-github-actions-runners and https://docs.github.com/en/copilot/concepts/agents/coding-agent/about-coding-agent | E-G2 (0 Dependabot PRs and 0 Copilot sessions after 20 deliveries) |
| The audit log REST and GraphQL API is available only on Enterprise Cloud | https://docs.github.com/en/organizations/keeping-your-organization-secure/managing-security-settings-for-your-organization/reviewing-the-audit-log-for-your-organization | E-G6 |
| Org Codespaces access has `PUT /orgs/{org}/codespaces/access` and no GET; `GET /orgs/{org}/codespaces` lists the org's codespaces | https://docs.github.com/en/rest/codespaces/organizations | E-G2 |

Gate-pinned, with no source cited. Each has a fallback if its gate fails.

| Claim | Gate | Fallback |
|---|---|---|
| A draft PR on a Free-plan private repo returns 422 | E-G5 | None needed: enrollment refuses every org outside Enterprise Cloud anyway |
| `require_last_push_approval` and dismiss-stale stop an App push from riding a human approval | E-G3 | The lane does not ship |
| An App's review does not satisfy a CODEOWNERS-required review | E-G3 | The lane does not ship |
| An org owner who is not an enterprise owner can't edit or bypass an enterprise ruleset | E-G3 | The lane does not ship |
| Pull requests write covers the issues-labels endpoint on a PR | E-G5 | PRs go unlabelled; labels are advisory and the header carries the same text |
| GitHub proxies rendered external images through Camo | E-G4 | None needed: renderable files with external references are refused whatever the proxy |
| linguist-generated, vendored and binary files collapse in the default diff | E-G4 | Refuse the no-ask lane for any file with a linguist attribute or a vendored or generated path pattern |
| The 300-file / 20,000-line / 1 MiB render window | E-G4 | None needed: it sets only a header flag |
| The audit-log action names `repo.fork`, `codespaces.*` and `org.disable_oauth_app_restrictions` | E-G6 | The verifier polls `forks_count`, the codespaces list and the installation list every 60 s instead |
| `GET /orgs/{org}/installations` reports the write App's `repository_selection`, and the audit log emits `integration_installation.repositories_added` | E-G2, E-G6 | The spread bound does not depend on them: the org holds only lane repos and its full repo list is bound, so the verifier polls that list every 60 s |
| On an enrolled mirror GitHub refuses, from the write App token, a ref outside `refs/heads` and `refs/tags` (by git push or `POST /git/refs`) and a draft release with an asset | E-G3 | The lane does not ship. The ref-set and release snapshot fields still catch either one off-box and revoke |

### 7.4 Spec changes (ready to paste)
Shared spots use the merged blocks in section 8.4 where the lane only extends them (M10 §9, M11 §10, M12 §11, M15 App. D, M16 App. E); the items below are the lane's own additions.
1. **§5.3 `repo.deliver` floor and §6.3 binder (control-path refusal, extended; R62 revised).** Keep the no-ask refusal for the §4.2 agent-steering set and the auto-run-on-open and merge-gate paths, and ADD `.gitattributes` (any location), `.github/dependabot.yml` and `.github/copilot-*` to the refused set. On an enrolled mirror the refusal is `HUMAN_ONLY` with the remedy "apply it by hand on the CI repo", because the push ruleset blocks these paths for every actor, the owner included (R76). Build/manifest/test-autoload files (`package.json` and its scripts, pnpm/yarn, `pyproject`/`setup.py`/`setup.cfg`, Cargo `build.rs`, `Gemfile`, `composer.json`, `go:generate`, `Makefile`/`Taskfile`/`justfile`, `Dockerfile`, `conftest.py`, `.mocharc`, jest/vitest config, fixtures) are NOT refused: they are flagged in the header instead (refusing them would gut dependency and feature deliveries and break the ask model). Reason: auto-run-on-open paths fire on a passive gesture and must be refused and blocked off-box; `.gitattributes` can hide a diff; build/test hooks fire only on the owner's active checkout+run, which the owner-duty review-via-web-source-view reduces to §10 residual 3 (closes attack 12's control half; narrows attack 13).
2. **§6.3 binder (render-visible set and auto-collapse refusal; new R68).** rootd computes the RENDER-VISIBLE changed-file set by applying the head tree's gitattributes plus GitHub's linguist generated/vendored/binary heuristics, flags in the header every changed file GitHub will collapse by default, and REFUSES the no-ask lane (fall back to a signed `ask.batch` reviewed in the unlimited §5.4 pager) if any changed source file would be auto-collapsed. Reason: a file marked `linguist-generated` is hidden from GitHub's default diff, so an owner reviewing Files-changed never sees it (closes attack 12).
3. **§6.3 binder (renderable-file beacon refusal; new R69).** Refuse the no-ask lane for any delivery that adds or changes a renderable file (`md`, `ipynb`, `svg`, `html`, `rst`) containing ANY external resource reference; such deliveries go as a signed `ask.batch` where the §5.4 renderer strips or escapes the reference. The scanner must detect references across Markdown (inline, reference-style, and raw HTML `img`/`image`/`srcset`/`background`), SVG (`href`, `xlink:href`, `url()`), and notebook JSON cells, and mark each such file "may beacon on render". Reason: a Camo beacon in a committed renderable file fires on the owner's rendered or image/notebook diff view; the round-1 "flag only" was an advisory line a PR-body lure talks past (closes attacks 3 and 16 for the no-ask lane; residual is a novel render surface the scanner misses, stated).
4. **§6.3 Caps (human-attention source-diff caps; R47/R48 revised again).** Set the no-ask-lane diff caps at a human-attention threshold on the REVIEWABLE SOURCE diff, with recognized lockfiles and vendored trees (`go.sum`, `package-lock.json`, `Cargo.lock`, `uv.lock`, `vendor/`, `node_modules/`, `.venv/`) excluded and covered by the depcheck summary instead: at most 25 changed source files, 600 source diff lines, 256 KiB source bytes, and no single source file over 300 lines or 64 KiB. Over any cap falls back to a signed `ask.batch` (the §5.4 pager has no size limit). GitHub's 300-file / 20,000-line / 1 MiB render window stays ONLY as the header truncation flag, never as the no-ask ceiling. Reason: capping at the render window only prevents hiding past the window; a 290-file mechanical refactor fully renders but no human reads 19,500 lines, so one malicious hunk hides in plain sight (closes attack 15). Excluding lockfiles keeps dependency updates on the no-ask lane, so the ask model holds.
5. **§6.3 PR template (header as entity-escaped HTML, Markdown-neutralized; R64 revised).** Emit the whole rootd header as HTML with every agent-derived string (changed paths, dependency names, resolved URLs, registry/auth overrides, integrity hashes, diffstat filenames, commit subjects) HTML-entity-escaped and never placed inside `<img>`/`<a>`/`<image>` or as a bare URL; run a Markdown-neutralization pass that strips or entity-encodes `! [ ] ( ) < > ` backtick and bare URLs for everything rootd renders to GitHub. The §5.4 terminal escaper is insufficient on a Markdown surface (it passes P and S categories including backtick and brackets). Add a gate that feeds a crafted lockfile `resolved` and `.npmrc` value and asserts the rendered PR-body HTML contains no `camo.githubusercontent.com` URL and no `<img>`. Reason: the round-1 header printed agent-controlled depcheck values into the Markdown body, so an attacker-crafted resolved URL rendered as image Markdown and beaconed on the owner's Conversation-tab view (closes attack 2).
6. **§6.3 PR template (depcheck anomaly ranking; R64 revised).** Rank depcheck high-risk ONLY on genuinely anomalous signals: a first-seen package name, a new or changed install script, a new maintainer or registry/scope, a downgrade, a hashless pin, or a resolved host that differs from the package's prior host. A routine version bump of an existing pinned dependency (changed resolved URL or integrity hash with the same name and host) is shown as a separate low-risk COUNT, not a high-risk line. Reason: flagging every changed resolved URL or integrity hash as high-risk floods a routine npm/pip update with 30 to 50 lines, the owner habituates, and a poisoned bump hides as one indistinguishable line (closes attack 14).
7. **§6.3 PR template and §6.3 push (fixed commit identity and trailer caveat; new R70).** Force the commit author and committer identity of every delivered commit to a fixed agentbox lane identity (a noreply address), so no real-person avatar renders on GitHub, and add a fixed header line stating that all commit subjects and `Co-authored-by` / `Signed-off-by` / `Reviewed-by` / `Acked-by` / `Tested-by` trailers are agent-supplied and unverified by agentbox. Reason: printable-ASCII trailers pass the §5.4 escaper and GitHub renders them as co-author avatars and apparent prior review, lending false authority (closes attack 18).
8. **§6.3 settings and §5.3 preconditions (GitHub-side push ruleset and Codespaces outcomes; new R71, R86, closes the critical attack).** Bind in `settingsHash` and re-read each delivery: (a) a restricted-file-paths PUSH ruleset blocking `.github/workflows/**`, `.devcontainer/**`, `.devcontainer.json`, `.vscode/**`, `.github/codespaces/**`, `.github/dependabot.yml`, `.github/copilot-*`, `.gitattributes` and `CODEOWNERS` for every actor, the owner included (zero bypass actors; an enterprise push ruleset, cited in section 7.3, and if none is available the lane does not ship); (b) the readable Codespaces outcomes of section 7.3: no `.devcontainer/`, `.devcontainer.json`, `.vscode/` or `.github/codespaces/` path in the default-branch tree, 0 codespaces for the repo in `GET /orgs/{org}/codespaces` and no `codespaces.*` audit-log event (both read off-box), and the owner's typed Codespaces-disabled attestation. GitHub has no per-repository Codespaces switch and no GET for the org policy, so "Codespaces off" itself is not bound. The push ruleset is GitHub-enforced against the App's own token, so a compromised `agentbox-git` that bypasses `repo.deliver`'s on-box binder still cannot push an auto-run payload, and no ref carries a dev container config, so a Codespace opened on any branch runs no repo lifecycle command. Pair with off-box alerting on any push touching these paths. Reason: the on-box binder lives inside `repo.deliver` and is never reached by a direct GitHub API call from a compromised broker; GitHub gates only `.github/workflows` by the Workflows permission, not `.devcontainer` or `.vscode`, so the round-1 claim that attack 1 is closed was false (closes attack 19, the critical review-time Codespaces RCE).
9. **§6.3 `lane-settings/v1` (PR-required rule, enterprise enforcement, resolved CODEOWNERS; R46 revised).** Bind and re-check: a require-pull-request-before-merge rule that blocks direct pushes to the default branch for non-bypass actors (so the App's `contents:write` cannot bypass the PR); every lane ruleset an enterprise ruleset with zero bypass actors, read off-box (R84), with enforcement explicitly bound as `active` (treat `evaluate` or `disabled` as gate-gone and hard, non-auto-resumable revocation); the merge-gate state in every 60 s signed snapshot; and the CODEOWNERS RESOLVED membership (team expanded to current human logins via the read-only path, re-enumerated every delivery and sweep) or named-user CODEOWNERS entries, with a catch-all `* @<human-team>` (or named human users) as the first effective rule, no later pattern with an empty owner list, and every owner in the bound human set. Reason: a CODEOWNERS-required review covers only files that match a pattern, so without the catch-all an unowned path merges on any single write-access approval; `contents:write` can push straight to the default branch when the ruleset is disabled/evaluate, and `require_last_push_approval`/CODEOWNERS apply only to PR merges, not direct pushes (attack 8); and the CODEOWNERS blob hash is constant no matter who is added to the team (attack 7). E-G3 must prove on a live org that the App cannot push to default, that disabling/evaluating the ruleset is caught and revokes, that adding any login to the CODEOWNERS team suspends the next delivery, and that a PR touching a path no specific pattern matches still needs a human CODEOWNERS approval.
10. **§6.3 `lane-settings/v1` step 1 (fork outcome; R60 revised).** Add to the hashed reads of `GET /repos/{o}/{r}`: `forks_count` [0]; keep `fork=false`, parent/source absent, `allow_forking=false`, `has_pages=false`, and the org members-can-fork-private-repos setting [off]. Do not bind the fork network list: it is not reliably readable. Add an off-box audit-log watch for `repo.fork`; push rulesets already cover the whole fork network (section 7.3 cited facts). Any observed fork, any `forks_count` increment, or `allow_forking=true` observed even once is permanent, non-auto-resumable revocation requiring re-enrollment, NOT a resume-when-the-flag-reverts suspension. Reason: a brief forking-enabled window lets a member fork the repo cross-org; the fork persists and keeps the full history after the flag reverts and after access is removed, and binding only the config lets the lane resume while the fork still leaks every delivered SHA (closes attack 9).
11. **§6.3 `lane-settings/v1` step 3 (write-App spread bounded by the org; R61 revised).** The mirror org holds only lane repos. The verifier binds the org's full repo list (`GET /orgs/{org}/repos`) and requires every repo in it to be an enrolled lane remote; binds the write App's `repository_selection=selected` from `GET /orgs/{org}/installations`; binds the verifier App's own installation repo list equal to the org repo list; and watches the audit log for `integration_installation.repositories_added`. The on-box enumeration with the write App's own non-narrowed token is liveness only, because that token is broker-controlled. No `/user/installations` read is relied on. Reason: an installation token lists only its own installation's repositories, `GET /user/installations/{id}/repositories` lists only what the authenticated user can reach, and the on-box token can be forged by a compromised broker; bounding the org to lane repos makes the write App's spread harmless whatever its selected set (closes attacks 6, 10).
12. **§6.3 execution (minted-token scope verification; new R72).** At member 0's re-check, after minting the write token narrowed to the one bound repo via `repository_ids`, verify the returned token's repository scope is exactly that one repo before any push; document that token narrowing is advisory and the real bound is the installation's bound selected set. Reason: GitHub caps a token only at the installation's repos; narrowing is caller-chosen, so a compromised broker can mint an un-narrowed token valid for every registered remote, bounded only by the bound installation set (closes attack 11).
13. **§5.3 preconditions and §6.3 (effective reader set; new R73).** Bind the EFFECTIVE reader set, not only direct collaborators and deploy keys: read and bind the org base permission and refuse enrollment, or force `none`, if the repo is readable by all org members; count org owners; surface repo watchers in the type-confirmed audience count; recommend owner-only. Re-check base permission in the drift sweep and suspend on any widening. Reason: an org base permission of Read grants every org member read on the private repo and notifiable @mention/watch access, so the round-1 collaborator-only audience count was misleadingly small (closes attack 5).
14. **§6.3 suspend/revoke and alerts (scrub already-delivered content; new R74).** On lane suspend, revoke, or any bound-audience or visibility drift, do not only block future deliveries: emit an alert listing every open lane PR and every pushed `agent/*` branch, and auto-close the open lane PRs through the write App (pull requests write). The App can't delete the pushed branches, and neither can the owner, because the agent-branch ruleset restricts deletion for every actor with zero bypass actors (R82); the alert tells the owner to delete the mirror repo, or to have an enterprise owner lift that ruleset after revocation and delete the branches, since they carry un-reviewed tainted content a new reader must not inherit. Routine cleanup on a healthy lane (deleting merged `agent/**` branches, or editing CODEOWNERS, which the push ruleset also blocks) needs the same enterprise-owner lift. The lift changes `settingsHash`, so it suspends the lane, and the owner re-enrolls after restoring the ruleset; section 2.9 counts the minutes under "Human work the count leaves out". Reason: suspension only refuses the next delivery; it does not scrub historic branches and draft PRs, so a later collaborator add or base-permission widening exposes pre-positioned agent branches (closes attack 6).
15. **§4.3 / §14 and §10 (TPM scope and IP allow list; R55 revised) and §6.3 drift detection and §10 (off-box primary, on-box liveness; R55).** State plainly that holding the App key in the TPM addresses at-rest key theft only and does not bound a live broker compromise (which asks the TPM to sign a JWT, exchanges it for an installation token, and exfiltrates the token). Bind four facts in the signed snapshot (R87): the org IP allow list is enabled, which already restricts installation, user-to-server and personal access tokens, owners included; "IP allow list configuration for installed GitHub Apps" is OFF, because turning it on adds each installed App's declared IPs as uneditable "Managed by the NAME GitHub App" entries kept in sync with the App creator's list, which widens the list to IPs an App owner picks; no entry is "Managed by" an App; and the entries are exactly the box's single stable egress IP, the verifier's stable egress IP and the owner's named review IPs, each typed at enrollment, because the org list also covers the owner's web UI and Git access and the verifier's API access (https://docs.github.com/en/organizations/keeping-your-organization-secure/managing-security-settings-for-your-organization/managing-allowed-ip-addresses-for-your-organization). Both Apps must be installed on the org, because the list does not restrict an App installed on a user account. rootd checks that its own configured egress IP is one of the bound entries. The verifier reads them through GraphQL `ipAllowListEntries` with its owner read credential, since rootd and the App can't. A token used from any other IP is rejected; rate-limit and inline-log every TPM JWT signing to the off-box sink in near-real-time (closes attacks 21, 2-8, 2-9; residual: an attacker proxying through the compromised broker's own egress or using an owner review IP or the verifier IP). Relabel the on-box 60 s tripwire and the sweep as best-effort LIVENESS only; make an OFF-BOX verifier with its own credentials the primary detection authority and the only source the floor trusts (it reads repo settings and the audit-log stream independently, signs each `lane-settings/v1` snapshot, and triggers `lane.revoke` / the rootd-floor `forbidden` state on any drift, fork, restricted-path push or off-IP token use, latency bound = the stream/poll interval). Correct E-G6 to prove that a compromised write path cannot forge a clean verdict because the authoritative verdict comes from an independent off-box principal, not from a divergence between two paths the same compromised broker serves. Reason: rootd has no network, so both the write and read-only credentials are exercised by the one networked tier (`agentbox-git`), so the round-1 divergence check and the 70 s suspend guarantee were overclaims, and an unsigned rootd re-read is no check at all (closes attacks 20 and 2-6 by re-grounding; R85).
16. **§6.3 Caps and the digest (flood; R65 revised, R80).** Lower the defaults to single digits: `lane.per_remote_day = 3`, `lane.open_per_box = 5`, `lane.tainted_agent_24h = 3` (smaller than `lane.agent_24h`), and `lane.open_per_agent = 3` open lane PRs per authoring agent, equal to `lane.tainted_agent_24h` so it never binds below the other caps and the section 2.9 model is unchanged. `lane.per_remote_day` is a hard cap; no acknowledgement unlocks a further delivery that UTC day (R80). Keep the periodic digest listing open tainted deliveries distinctly; reviewing it before merging is an owner duty, not an unlock. Reason: generous caps let a compromised agentboxd bury one malicious draft among 19 plausible ones in a batch-review the owner rubber-stamps; hard single-digit caps make volume cost attention (bounds attacks 17, 22).
17. **§5.4 enrollment render and owner duties (review surface and cross-project consent; R66 revised).** The enrollment render shows and type-confirms: the audience can receive any file the agent can read across all projects in its single workspace (recommend one project per lane-enrolled agent, and apply a cheap project-subtree refusal in rootd that rejects a delivered tree with paths outside the enrolled project's subtree); and the standing owner duties that reviewing ONLY via GitHub's web Files-changed source view is safe, while opening, cloning, checking out, opening in an editor, launching a Codespace, or running install/build/test on an agent branch executes its code with the owner's credentials, and that opening the rich/rendered or notebook/image diff view can fire a beacon. Reason: the one-workspace-per-agent model (R27) precludes a content-level fix for cross-project copy-in, so this is a stated narrowing; and the owner's review surface is the premise the whole lane rests on (narrows attacks 4, 13, 22).
18. **Appendix E state (lanes and settings), Appendix D (Cedar), and the Decisions (section 9).** `lanes` gains `codeowners_members_hash`, `ip_allowlist_ref`, `review_ips`, `devcontainer_absent`, `codespaces_attest`, `oauth_restrict_attest`, `verifier_key`, `snapshot_max_age_s` (300), `bypass_actors_hash`, `installations_hash`, `verifier_ip`, `org_repos_hash`, `ref_rulesets_hash`, `push_ruleset_hash`, `ruleset_enforcement`, `forks_count`, `base_permission`, the off-box verifier reference, the round-1 state value `forbidden`, `codeowners_hash`, `read_cred_ref` and `project`. `lane-settings/v1` JSON gains a verifier signature, a timestamp and the repo `node_id`, `forks_count[0]`, the restricted-file-paths push ruleset, the non-agent branch, tag and agent-branch rulesets with `bypass_actors=[]`, an empty tag list, no release (drafts included), every ref the default branch or under `refs/heads/agent/`, the `workflows`-rule absence, Dependabot security updates and alerts off, 0 Dependabot secrets, code scanning default setup not configured, the CODEOWNERS catch-all check, the org installation list, the default-tree dev container check and the codespaces count, the Enterprise Cloud membership, the PR-required rule, ruleset enforcement level and status, the resolved CODEOWNERS membership, the effective reader set (base permission, owners, watchers), the org repo list, the write App's `repository_selection` and the verifier App's installation repo list, and the IP allow list settings (enabled, installed-App inheritance off, no "Managed by" entry) and entries (box, verifier and review IPs). `control/budgets.toml`: `lane.per_remote_day` default 3, `lane.open_per_box` default 5, `lane.tainted_agent_24h` default 3, `lane.open_per_agent` default 3. Appendix D: add a `deliver-lane` permit for `repo.deliver` and amend `tainted-ask` to exempt `repo.deliver` (the taint exemption set, pinned to exactly `{repo.deliver}`); `risk-ask`, `budget-ask` and human forbids are unchanged. Decisions: R46, R47/R48, R55, R60, R61, R62 revised; R65, R66 revised; R68–R74 new; R67 kept; R82–R89 new for the second red-team (section 9).
19. **§6.3 `lane-settings/v1` (ref and tag rulesets; new R82).** Bind enterprise rulesets with zero bypass actors and `enforcement=active`: a branch ruleset on `~ALL` excluding `refs/heads/agent/**` and `~DEFAULT_BRANCH` that restricts creation, update and deletion; restrict deletion and block force pushes on `~DEFAULT_BRANCH`, beside the PR-required rule; a tag ruleset on `~ALL` that restricts creation, update and deletion; and block force pushes and restrict deletion on `refs/heads/agent/**`. Bind the tag list as empty, and add two snapshot fields read off-box with the enterprise credential: every ref in `GET /repos/{o}/{r}/git/refs` is the default branch or under `refs/heads/agent/`, and `GET /repos/{o}/{r}/releases` lists no release, draft included. Any foreign ref or release seen on an enrolled lane is permanent revocation. E-G3 proves the App token can't create a non-agent branch or tag, create a release or a draft release with an asset, create `refs/notes/x` or `refs/foo/bar`, or force-push or delete an agent branch; if GitHub accepts any non-heads/tags ref or a draft release from the App, the lane does not ship. Reason: `contents:write` reaches every ref no ruleset covers, rulesets target only `refs/heads` and `refs/tags`, a draft release makes no tag, and the on-box `agent/<executor>/*` check is skipped by a compromised broker (closes attack 2-1; executor separation inside `agent/**` stays an on-box residual).
20. **§5.3 preconditions and §6.3 (nothing else reacts; new R83, R84).** Refuse enrollment, and suspend on drift, if the org has any GitHub App installation other than the write App and the verifier App (`GET /orgs/{org}/installations`), or if either is installed on a user account rather than the org; bind Dependabot security updates off (`GET /repos/{o}/{r}/automated-security-fixes`), vulnerability alerts off, 0 Dependabot secrets at repo and org level, code scanning default setup `not-configured`, and Copilot coding agent disabled for the repo (an owner attestation checked by outcome), because Dependabot runs on Actions runners even with Actions disabled and gets Dependabot secrets; require OAuth app access restrictions on, as an owner attestation checked off-box in the audit log; refuse any applicable ruleset with a `workflows` rule; require zero `bypass_actors` on every lane ruleset, read off-box with the enterprise-owner read credential, and `current_user_can_bypass=never` (not `exempt`) for the App. Give the verifier App exactly organization administration read, members read, organization webhooks read and repository webhooks read, plus repository metadata, contents and administration read. Reason: a CI or deploy App builds pushed branches with its own secrets whatever the Actions setting, and the rulesets API hides `bypass_actors` from the App (closes attacks 2-2, 2-4, 2-5 and the Dependabot and Copilot runner paths; narrows 2-3).
21. **§5.3 `repo.deliver` floor and §6.3 (verifier-signed snapshots, Enterprise Cloud only, no `issues:write`; new R85, R88, R89).** The off-box verifier signs each `lane-settings/v1` snapshot with its own key, enrolled in `lane.set`, including a timestamp and the repo `node_id`. rootd's floor admits a delivery only on a valid verifier signature, a snapshot under 5 minutes old and a matching `settingsHash`; otherwise `lane_unavailable`. Before the hash compare rootd parses the snapshot and evaluates the pinned predicate list of section 7.3 itself, including that its own configured egress IP is a bound allow-list entry. rootd checks signatures, freshness and predicates, not GitHub. Enrollment refuses any org outside a GitHub Enterprise Cloud enterprise. The delivery App drops `issues:write` and sets labels through the issues-labels endpoint under pull requests write. Reason: rootd has no network, so its re-reads were broker-supplied; Team orgs lack the IP allow list, the audit log API and enterprise rulesets; `issues:write` is a no-gate publish channel (closes attacks 2-6, 2-10 by scope, 2-12).

### 7.5 Threats and mitigations
Every working round-1 attack, most severe first. "Closed" means a mechanism the lane adds blocks it; "narrowed" and "re-grounded" are stated honestly and carried to §10.

| # | Red-team attack (severity) | How round 2 closes it |
|---|---|---|
| 19 | A compromised `agentbox-git` pushes a `.devcontainer` branch and PR directly via the GitHub API, bypassing the on-box binder; the owner opens a Codespace to review and the lifecycle command exfiltrates Codespaces secrets with the owner's token (critical) | CLOSED off-box: the restricted-file-paths push ruleset blocks these paths for the App (no bypass), no ref carries a dev container config (default tree checked, the push ruleset blocks additions on every ref, R86), Actions is off, and off-box alerting fires on any such push. GitHub-enforced against the App's own token, so it holds under total broker compromise (R71). |
| 1 | An UNCHANGED trusted default-branch devcontainer auto-runs a malicious `postinstall` added to `package.json` (not a control path) when the owner opens a Codespace to review (high) | CLOSED BY OUTCOME CHECKS, POLICY ATTESTED: the default-branch tree has no dev container config, so an unchanged default-branch devcontainer can't exist on an enrolled repo (R86), plus 0 codespaces checked off-box, the owner's Codespaces-disabled attestation, and the owner duty to review only via the web source view. |
| 2 | A Camo beacon injected into the rootd header via agent-controlled depcheck values (resolved URL, `.npmrc` override) rendered as image Markdown in the PR body, firing on the owner's Conversation-tab view (high) | CLOSED by emitting the header as HTML with every agent value entity-escaped and never inside `img`/`a`, plus a Markdown-neutralization pass stripping `! [ ] ( ) < > ` backtick and bare URLs; a gate asserts the rendered body HTML has no camo URL and no `img` (R64). |
| 3 | A Camo beacon in a committed renderable file (README, notebook, SVG, HTML) with the reference hidden from the round-1 scanner (reference-style, raw HTML `img`/`srcset`/`background`, SVG `href`/`xlink`/`url()`, notebook JSON) (high) | CLOSED for the no-ask lane by refusing any renderable file carrying an external resource reference (routed to `ask.batch` where §5.4 strips it) with a complete cross-surface scanner (R69); residual is a novel render surface the scanner's grammar misses, bounded by owner-only audience and off-box audit, stated. |
| 16 | The same Camo beacon lured by a PR body saying "view the rendered README" (medium) | CLOSED with attack 3: renderable-with-external-ref is refused to the no-ask lane, the header flag is a hard "review as raw source only", and the body is entity-escaped. |
| 12 | A payload in a normal-sized source file plus a committed `.gitattributes` marking it `linguist-generated`, so GitHub collapses it from the default diff (high) | CLOSED by adding `.gitattributes` to the refused control paths (on-box) and the push ruleset (off-box) and by computing the render-visible set (head-tree gitattributes plus linguist heuristics) and refusing the no-ask lane if any changed source file would be auto-collapsed (R62, R68). |
| 15 | A malicious hunk inside an under-cap but still huge mechanical refactor that fully renders but no human reads (high) | CLOSED by setting the no-ask caps at a human-attention threshold on the reviewable source diff (25 files, 600 lines, 256 KiB; lockfiles and vendored trees excluded and summarized by depcheck); over any cap falls back to a signed `ask.batch`. The render-window figures stay only as the truncation flag (R47/R48). |
| 14 | A poisoned dependency bump hidden among dozens of routine bumps that depcheck over-flags, so the owner habituates (high) | CLOSED by ranking depcheck high-risk only on anomaly signals (first-seen package, new/changed install script, new maintainer/registry/scope, downgrade, hashless pin, resolved-host change) and showing routine version bumps as a separate low-risk count (R64). |
| 7 | CODEOWNERS team-roster drift: an attacker login added to the human team leaves the blob hash unchanged, so the sweep sees no drift and the attacker approves the merge (high) | CLOSED by binding the RESOLVED CODEOWNERS member logins (team expanded via the read-only path), re-enumerated every delivery and sweep, or requiring named-user entries; E-G3 proves that adding any login suspends the next delivery (R46). |
| 8 | The default-branch ruleset set to disabled/evaluate plus the standing `contents:write` token lets a compromised broker push attacker commits straight to the default branch, with no PR and no review, inside the sweep window (high) | CLOSED by a require-PR-before-merge rule blocking direct pushes to default for non-bypass actors, both rulesets enterprise-level with zero bypass actors, Enterprise Cloud only (R84, R88), with enforcement bound as active (evaluate/disabled is gate-gone and hard revocation), and the merge gate in every 60 s signed snapshot; the trust assumption that the enterprise-ruleset editor is in the trusted base is stated (R46). |
| 9 | A brief forking-enabled window creates a persistent cross-org fork that keeps the full history and leaks every delivered SHA after the flag reverts (high) | CLOSED by binding `forks_count==0` and `allow_forking=false`, an off-box audit-log watch for `repo.fork`, and treating any observed fork, `forks_count` increment, or `allow_forking=true` as permanent, non-auto-resumable revocation requiring re-enrollment (R60). |
| 6 | The write App silently added to another repo is invisible when the read-only verifier is a separate app whose installation lists only its own repos (medium) | CLOSED by bounding the spread with the org: the mirror org holds only lane repos, the verifier binds the org's full repo list, the write App's `repository_selection=selected` and the verifier App's repo list, and watches `integration_installation.repositories_added`; the on-box enumeration is liveness only (R61). |
| 10 | The design cannot buy both drift-read independence and spread detection from a separate read-only app (medium) | CLOSED by not needing either app to see the other's installation: the org holds only lane repos and its full repo list is bound off-box, so any spread stays inside lane repos and any new repo suspends (R61). |
| 11 | The one-repo write-token scope is broker-enforced, so a compromised broker mints an un-narrowed token valid for every registered remote (low) | CLOSED (bounded) by verifying the minted token's repository scope is exactly the one bound repo before any push and documenting that the real bound is the installation's bound selected set (R72). |
| 5 | Audience under-count because org base permission (often Read) grants every org member read, and the round-1 count bound only direct collaborators and deploy keys (medium) | CLOSED by binding the effective reader set: read and bind org base permission, refuse enrollment or force `none` if all org members can read, count org owners, surface watchers, and re-check in the sweep (R73). |
| 6b | Suspension blocks the next delivery but does not scrub already-pushed branches and draft PRs, so a later collaborator add exposes pre-positioned agent branches (medium) | CLOSED by alerting on suspend/revoke/audience-drift with every open lane PR and pushed `agent/*` branch, auto-closing the PRs through the write App and listing the branches; deleting them needs an enterprise owner to lift the zero-bypass ruleset, which suspends the lane until re-enrollment (R74, R82). |
| 18 | Fabricated commit authorship and `Co-authored-by`/`Signed-off-by`/`Reviewed-by` trailers that pass the escaper and render as co-author avatars and apparent prior review (low) | CLOSED by forcing commit author/committer identity to a fixed agentbox lane noreply identity and adding a fixed header line that all commit subjects and trailers are agent-supplied and unverified (R70). |
| 20 | The on-box tripwire and sweep are fully blindable by the compromised `agentbox-git` they are meant to detect, because rootd has no network and the one networked tier serves both the write and the read-only path (high) | RE-GROUNDED: on-box tripwire/sweep are best-effort liveness; an off-box verifier with its own credential reading the audit-log stream and settings independently is the primary detection and the authority for `lane.revoke`, with a stated latency bound; the real guarantees are the GitHub-side App-unbypassable controls that hold regardless of broker state. E-G6 is corrected accordingly (R55). |
| 21 | The TPM protects the key at rest but a live broker compromise mints and exfiltrates an installation token usable off-box with the full App permission set, bypassing every on-box control (high) | CLOSED (bounded) by the org IP allow list enabled with installed-App inheritance off and no "Managed by" entry, holding exactly the box egress IP, the verifier egress IP and the owner's typed review IPs, read off-box, with both Apps installed on the org (R87), so an off-IP token is rejected, plus rate-limited, off-box-streamed TPM JWT signing; TPM is documented as at-rest only. Residual: an attacker proxying through the compromised broker's own egress or using an owner review IP or the verifier IP, the stated live-compromise residual (R55). |
| 13 | The owner's natural way to review code is to check it out and run install/tests, which executes a committed `conftest.py`, `postinstall`, or `Makefile` target before line-by-line review (high) | NARROWED, not fully closed: the header flags every build/manifest/test-autoload file with a fixed "checking out or running this branch executes this code with your credentials" line, and the standing owner duty is to review only via the web Files-changed source view. Refusing all build/manifest changes would gut dependency and feature deliveries and break the ask model, so this is reduced to §10 residual 3 and stated (R62, R66). |
| 4 | Cross-project exfiltration: the one-workspace-per-agent model lets a delivery to remote A carry project B content copied into A's tree, which a path check cannot catch once the content is a file inside A's subtree (high) | NARROWED, not closed: a type-confirmed enrollment attestation, a one-project-per-lane-agent recommendation, a cheap project-subtree refusal (multi-project-repo case), owner-only minimal audience, the human merge gate seeing the full file list, and the renderable-file beacon refusal bound it. A content-level fix needs per-project workspaces (R27, absent); stated as a residual (R66, R73). |
| 22 | A compromised agentboxd drives a live clean task, attributes the taint-exempt no-ask `repo.deliver`, and publishes attacker-chosen agent-readable content with no human gate until merge (medium) | NARROWED, not closed: the rootd-floor `forbidden` state and `lane.revoke` (Cedar- and agentboxd-independent), lowered single-digit hard caps (R80), and the owner duty to review the on-box tainted digest and confirm the head against `lane show` before merge bound it. A compromised agentboxd still cannot merge (human CODEOWNERS gate), cannot beacon (renderable refusal), and cannot reach a non-owner (minimal audience). Stated §10 residual (R65, R67). |

### 7.5b Round-2 red-team attacks and decisions
A second red-team of the round-2 lane found 12 working attacks. Each has a verdict tied to a mechanism, and the mechanisms are in sections 7.2 to 7.4. "Closed" means a bound GitHub-side or floor control blocks it. "Narrowed" names the residual, carried to section 7.8. "Refused" means the lane's scope was cut so the attack does not apply.

| # | Attack (severity) | Verdict | Mechanism |
|---|---|---|---|
| 2-1 | With `contents:write`, a compromised `agentbox-git` creates or moves refs no ruleset covers (`release/*`, `main2`, tags, `refs/notes/*` or other non-heads/tags refs, a release or a draft release with assets), or rewrites or deletes another agent's `agent/*` branch. The `agent/<executor>/*` namespace is enforced only on the box, so it is skipped (high) | Closed | Enterprise rulesets with zero bypass actors: non-agent branches restrict creation, update and deletion; tags restrict creation, update and deletion, which blocks published releases; `agent/**` blocks force pushes and deletion; empty tag list bound; every ref the default branch or under `refs/heads/agent/` and no release, draft included, bound off-box, with any foreign ref permanent revocation. E-G3 proves each refusal, and the lane does not ship if GitHub accepts a non-heads/tags ref or a draft release from the App. Executor separation inside `agent/**` stays on-box only (residual 4) (R82) |
| 2-2 | A CI or deploy App installed on the org (CircleCI, Vercel and the like) builds every pushed `agent/*` branch with its own secrets. Actions-off doesn't stop it, and "listed and counted" doesn't refuse it (critical) | Closed | Enrollment refuses, and the verifier suspends on, any org installation other than the write App and the verifier App (`GET /orgs/{org}/installations`) (R83) |
| 2-3 | An OAuth app a member authorized reads the repo or reacts to pushes with its own credentials (high) | Narrowed (outcome checks, policy attested) | OAuth app access restrictions on, as an owner attestation the verifier checks in the audit log; disabling them or approving an app suspends the lane. Residual: an app approved and used inside one verifier poll interval (R83) |
| 2-4 | A ruleset `workflows` rule makes a required workflow run on the agent branch, with secrets, even though repo Actions settings look off (medium) | Closed | Enrollment refuses, and the snapshot suspends on, any applicable ruleset with a `workflows` rule; Actions stays off (R83) |
| 2-5 | A deploy key, another App or a role sits in a ruleset's `bypass_actors`. The App and rootd see only their own `current_user_can_bypass`, so that actor pushes control paths or straight to the default branch unseen (high) | Closed | Zero bypass actors on every lane ruleset, read off-box with an enterprise-owner read credential and bound as `bypass_actors=[]` in the signed snapshot; the App must read `never`, not `exempt` (R84) |
| 2-6 | rootd has no network, so every "re-read" comes through `agentbox-git`, which forges a clean snapshot; the floor admits deliveries on a drifted repo (high) | Closed (bounded) | The verifier signs each `lane-settings/v1` snapshot with its key enrolled in `lane.set`, with a timestamp and the repo `node_id`; the floor needs a valid signature, age under 5 minutes and a matching `settingsHash`. Residual: replay of a genuine snapshot inside its 5 minutes (R85) |
| 2-7 | "Codespaces off" can't be read (no per-repo switch, no GET for the org policy), so the org policy flips on unseen and an unchanged default-branch devcontainer runs a malicious `postinstall` (attack 1 reopened) (high) | Closed by outcome checks, policy attested | Readable outcomes: no dev container paths in the default-branch tree (bound) and none addable on any ref (push ruleset); 0 codespaces for the repo and no `codespaces.*` audit event (off-box); the owner's typed policy attestation, labelled as one (R86) |
| 2-8 | The App's own IP allow list doesn't restrict its tokens unless the org turns on installed-App inheritance, so an exfiltrated token works from anywhere (high) | Closed (bounded) | The premise is corrected: the org list already restricts installation tokens, and the installed-Apps setting only adds uneditable "Managed by" App entries. Bind: org allow list enabled, inheritance OFF, no "Managed by" entry, entries exactly the box egress IP, the verifier egress IP and the typed owner review IPs; both Apps installed on the org, not a user account; read off-box through GraphQL `ipAllowListEntries`; rootd checks its own egress IP is an entry. Residual: use from a review IP, the verifier IP or through the box egress (R87) |
| 2-9 | An org allow list pinned to the box IP alone locks the owner out of the web review, so the owner turns it off to review and the token bound is gone (medium) | Closed | The owner's review IPs and the verifier's egress IP are typed at enrollment and bound as named entries, so neither the review nor the verifier is locked out; turning the list off, turning inheritance on, or any "Managed by" entry appearing fails the snapshot and suspends the lane (R87) |
| 2-10 | On a Team org, org owners can always edit org rulesets, and there is no audit log API and no IP allow list, so "org-owner-unbypassable" and off-box detection can't hold (high) | Refused (scope cut) | The lane is Enterprise Cloud only: enrollment refuses any org outside an Enterprise Cloud enterprise, and the q sensitivity counts every Team org repo as unable to enroll (R88) |
| 2-11 | The fork network list isn't reliably readable, so a fork made in a brief window keeps the repo's history unseen (medium) | Narrowed | Bind `forks_count==0` and `allow_forking=false` only; off-box audit watch on `repo.fork`; push rulesets cover the whole fork network; any fork is permanent revocation. Residual: content in the fork before the watch fires (R60) |
| 2-12 | `issues:write` lets a compromised broker post issues and comments on every bound repo: a publish channel with no gate (medium) | Closed | `issues:write` dropped; labels go through the issues-labels endpoint under pull requests write; E-G5 proves labels work and an issue create is refused (R89) |

Totals: 9 closed (2 of them bounded, 1 by outcome checks with the policy attested), 2 narrowed (1 with the policy attested), 1 refused by cutting scope.

### 7.6 Gates
- **E-G1 (week 4, registry).** `repo.deliver` present and `laneOnly`, `reversible=false`, never an `ask.batch` member, never phone-eligible; the taint exemption set is exactly `{repo.deliver}`; `draft` and `createOnly` in the schemas; `lane_unavailable` and `ref_exists` decode; code count unchanged.
- **E-G2 (weeks 7–8, inert, non-fork mirror on a real Enterprise Cloud test org).** Enrollment passes only when every value is met, and each of these refuses enrollment and names the check: an org outside an Enterprise Cloud enterprise (a Team org and a Free org as test cases); `fork=true` or parent/source present; `forks_count>0`; `allow_forking=true` or org private-forking on; Pages on; Actions on; a `workflows` ruleset rule; a tag, a release or a draft release present; a ref outside the default branch and `refs/heads/agent/`; a `.devcontainer/`, `.devcontainer.json`, `.vscode/`, `.github/codespaces/`, `.github/dependabot.yml` or `.github/copilot-*` path in the default-branch tree; a codespace listed for the repo; Dependabot security updates or vulnerability alerts on, or a Dependabot secret at repo or org level; code scanning default setup configured; a CODEOWNERS file without a catch-all `*` first rule, or with an empty-owner pattern; no enterprise push ruleset with file-path rules available (then the lane does not ship); the restricted-file-paths push ruleset absent or not covering any of `.github/workflows`, `.devcontainer`, `.devcontainer.json`, `.vscode`, `.github/codespaces`, `.github/dependabot.yml`, `.github/copilot-*`, `.gitattributes`, `CODEOWNERS`; any lane ruleset missing (push, default-branch, non-agent branch, tag, agent-branch), not an enterprise ruleset, with any bypass actor, or with enforcement evaluate/disabled; a repo or org content webhook; a new collaborator or deploy key; the org base permission readable by all members; any GitHub App installation in the org other than the write App and the verifier App, or either App installed on a user account; a repo in the org that is not an enrolled lane remote; the write App not at `repository_selection=selected`; the org IP allow list off, installed-App inheritance ON, a "Managed by" entry, or an entry other than the box egress IP, the verifier egress IP and the typed review IPs; the repo made internal or public. The verifier, from its own allow-listed egress IP, reads every snapshot field while the list is enabled. Bonus check, not relied on: whether `GET /user/installations/{installation_id}/repositories` with the enterprise-owner credential lists the write App's repos. On an enrolled lane: each such change refuses the next delivery with `lane_unavailable` before any push, plus one alert; a fork of the enrolled repo, or `allow_forking` observed true even once, is PERMANENT revocation requiring re-enrollment; creating a new repo in the org, or an `integration_installation.repositories_added` event, suspends the next delivery (org repo list bound); a codespace created for the repo or a restricted path pushed is caught off-box and revokes. After 20 deliveries: 0 workflow runs, 0 suites/statuses/deployments from other apps, 0 Codespaces created, 0 Dependabot PRs and 0 Copilot coding agent sessions.
- **E-G3 (weeks 7–8, the merge stop proven on a live org).** With the write App token, each is REFUSED by GitHub: marking its own draft ready then self-approving then merging; approving its own PR to satisfy the required review; merging without a human CODEOWNERS approval of the final head; and PUSHING DIRECTLY TO THE DEFAULT BRANCH (the PR-required rule blocks `contents:write`). After a human CODEOWNERS approval, a new App push dismisses it. A review by the App or a non-CODEOWNERS identity does not satisfy the rule. Adding any login to the CODEOWNERS team (resolved-membership drift) suspends the next delivery. A PR touching a path that no specific CODEOWNERS pattern matches still needs a human CODEOWNERS approval (the catch-all rule). Setting the ruleset to evaluate or disabled is caught and is hard, non-auto-resumable revocation. With the write App token GitHub also refuses: creating a non-agent branch (`release/x`, `main2`); creating, moving or deleting a tag; creating a release; creating a draft release with an asset; creating `refs/notes/x` or `refs/foo/bar` by git push and by `POST /repos/{o}/{r}/git/refs`; force-pushing or deleting an `agent/**` branch. If GitHub accepts any non-heads/tags ref or a draft release from the App, the lane does not ship; on the enrolled test lane, a foreign ref or release planted with another credential is permanent revocation. An org owner who is not an enterprise owner can't edit or bypass any lane ruleset.
- **E-G4 (weeks 7–8, delivery shape, refusals, caps, header).** Each delivery makes exactly one draft PR on the default branch with the prefix, the entity-escaped rootd header (diffstat, true file count with truncation flag, grouped control/dependency/build paths, render-visible auto-collapse flags, renderable-beacon flags, the unverified-trailer caveat, the depcheck anomaly-ranked high-risk summary), head equal to the on-box record, and a fixed commit identity. Refused to the no-ask lane with `lane_unavailable` and no push: a range touching `.github/workflows`, `.devcontainer`, `.devcontainer.json`, `.vscode`, `.github/codespaces`, `CODEOWNERS`, `.gitattributes` or the §4.2 steering set; any changed source file GitHub would auto-collapse; any renderable file (`md`, `ipynb`, `svg`, `html`, `rst`) with an external resource reference across inline/reference-style/raw-HTML/srcset/background, SVG `href`/`xlink`/`url()` or notebook JSON; a source diff over 25 files, 600 lines, 256 KiB, or any single source file over 300 lines / 64 KiB (lockfiles and vendored trees excluded); any commit message with a bidi or control character. A crafted lockfile `resolved` URL and `.npmrc` value renders in the PR-body HTML with no `camo.githubusercontent.com` URL and no `<img>`. A dependency bump with a poisoned first-seen or install-scripted package shows the depcheck high-risk line; a routine 40-package bump shows one low-risk count, not 40 high-risk lines.
