```markdown
- **Deputy, poisoning, laundering.** Append: "; the Go cache answers only after two taint records; dependency code that would run untainted after a clear (`vendor/`, `node_modules/`, `.venv/`) is high-risk in `taint review`, shown with content diffs, and needs a typed count bound into H (§7); workspace-bound facts are own-scope, readable only while the reader holds W at the bound epoch, indexed apart from other facts, quarantined at any epoch move including a signed restore, listed by hash even when forgotten, and capped per epoch with a staleness bound; a clean reader gets tainted memory only with `raise: true`, and that filter doesn't cover a fact a compromised broker laundered to `taint=0` (the residual below)".

Add rows:
| Attach session exfiltrates a subscription credential through memory | Attach sessions never get the workspace-bound exemption, so their tainted own-scope writes are quarantined and shown to a human; the post-attach token scan also covers the facts the session wrote (§4.2, §8) |
| Denials as a leak, oracle, steering or suppression channel (secrets, allowlists, policy shape, other agents' resources and spend, taint sources, LAN facts, the approval path) | One constructor and a closed ASCII schema, checked again in the rootd-mounted in-task binary. Only the caller's grammar-checked fields, release constants and own-subject facts. Owner-scoped lookups, per-owner app names, ULID shared ids and one Cedar path for `NOT_FOUND_OR_NOT_OWNED`, with 200 ms and 2 s reply floors. One taint shape, `{tainted, workspaceBound}`. One reason, `signed`, for every non-taint ask. One `BUSY`. Per-agent day shares. Static remedies. Never in P, H or ntfy. DENY records hidden from the query call. Floor codes only from rootd; a rootd-read witness flags forged and unseen denials (§5.6) |
| Ask quotes and status as a policy, allowlist or cross-task oracle | A quote is the submit's floor function with fewer inputs, each missing input at its worst case; no decide, network, file or repo reads; closed enums; no agent-wide value; `already_allowed` reveals no more than a CONNECT probe; counted per task (32), per trace (64) and per agent, bound into every P in the trace, and alerted on recon and enumeration; replies at receipt + 2 s (§5.2, §5.4) |
| Hidden, swapped or rider members in a batch | Two shapes only (`push+pr`, `push+reply`); one remote; replies only on a private remote, to one PR whose number the human types; issue comments and egress never batch; 16 KiB of agent text per member and 64 KiB per batch; the member H list is in the batch H; one page per member, a typed member count, a separate SSHSIG namespace; members can't be approved alone; every nonce burned in one transaction (§5.2, §5.4) |

Residuals:
- Replace "CDN fronting, and write-capable allowlisted hosts." with: "CDN fronting, write-capable allowlisted hosts, and deputy hosts when allowlisted: an agent with `proxy.golang.org` or `sum.golang.org` can send data out in module paths, tainted or not. v2: a GET-only package broker that still taints and takes registry and GitHub hosts off CONNECT lists; a private module proxy as the Go cache upstream."
- Replace the agentboxd residual with: "A compromised agentboxd acts as any untainted agent for unsigned floor ops, and can skip READ-INTENT on bus and memory reads. It can write workspace-bound facts for any live tainted task (it already controls memory writes). It can alter `ask.status` replies and denials within the §5.6 grammar (codes, ids, hex, decimals). It can drop requests or send wrong Cedar denials; the witness records them, but rootd can't re-check Cedar (§5.2). v2: an `agentbox-gate` service (own uid) serves the per-task gate sockets and relays raw requests to rootd. rootd then answers `ask.status` directly, re-evaluates Cedar denials, issues per-task gate MAC keys, binds the prompt hash of human-started tasks, and applies bus and memory READ-INTENT itself."
- Append to "A compromised proxy, keys or git can omit taint for content it delivers. v2: …": "For Go cache files, taskrun's filter already records that second taint (§6.2)."
- Add:
  - Go module cache: tainted tasks of an agent can see which public versions its store holds, until the next clear. A task that turns go's checks off (`GOSUMDB=off`, `GONOSUMDB`) trusts a compromised proxy's `.mod` and `.zip` bytes. This is accepted as bounded. It needs a compromised proxy plus task code that turns the checks off, which taskrun never does. Through the filter, the gate record taints the task before the first forged byte; skipping the filter takes hand-built requests, so the task already runs injected code. Either way the forged code gets nothing that code lacks: every non-read, non-Safe op still needs a signature. The override lasts one task. v2: a clean path for lockfile-pinned files once workspace taint is narrower.
  - Dependency review: npm and Python installed code can't be checked against an upstream hash, so every changed `node_modules/` or `.venv/` package is high-risk at a clear, and so is vendored code of a changed Go pin. v2: offline checks of stored checksum-database proofs for Go.
  - Workspace-bound facts: inside one tainted epoch, an injected bound note can steer the same agent's later tasks, which are all tainted already. Bounded by the per-epoch cap, the staleness bound, the digest and `memory review --bound`; one stricter Cedar forbid restores per-fact review.
  - Denials: run, ask and refuse stay observable as in v5.2, and codes add only the coarse reason. `BUSY` still shows one box-wide bit (the OS lock, the box's 3 CAS-binding slots, the daemon-ask pool). Task code can forge its own in-task log.
  - Ask budget: one batch signature can cover up to 4 effects, so a human may review up to 40 ops per agent per 24 h (v5.2: 20). Within the quote caps, a small low-volume probe raises no alert. Several tasks together, or one task with the maximum budget of 10 sending 4-member batches, can use up a shared per-agent cap and deny a sibling's asks (availability only, and noisy: every ask is an ntfy push).
```

**M12. §11 Failure modes** (A, B, C, D).
```markdown
Append to rows:
- **auditd down:** "No workspace-bound fact is returned, and memory writes are quarantined. Ops, quotes and brokers answer `AUDIT_UNAVAILABLE`; `ask.status` still answers, with `taint.tainted` = true. Every proxy cache request gets 503, like CONNECT."
- **rootd down:** "Witness lines written during the outage are lost; those witnesses are marked incomplete and raise no alert."
- **proxy, keys or git down:** "The task's forwarder retries for 5 s, then answers 503 `BROKER_UNAVAILABLE`, so clients don't hang."
- **Agent quota full (`1/N`):** "`cache.purge{agent}` frees the cache share."

Add rows:
| Go module cache full, corrupt, or upstream down | Misses and failed live checks get 404, and go uses the CONNECT path as in v5.2. A corrupt blob is deleted on read. A checksum mismatch or checksum-log error alerts (at most once per agent per day) and stops that agent's fills until `cache.purge`. With agentboxd down, the filter serves nothing and go uses CONNECT. |
| Proxy stopped or hung during `taint.clear` | Stopped: rootd re-creates the agent's empty store itself and the clear goes on. Hung: the clear is refused (`CACHE_PURGE_FAILED`) and the workspace stays tainted; retry when the proxy answers. |
| depcheck fails or times out | Every changed dependency file is high-risk, and the report says why. |
| A tool ignores the proxy | Its lookup fails in under 1 s, or `connect()` gets `ENETUNREACH`; `agentbox why --errno` explains. |
| A batch member is refused or fails | Later members become `refused` with `BATCH_ABORTED`; earlier effects stay (for example a pushed branch with no PR); the batch row takes that member's state. The agent may re-ask the rest. |
| An alert can't be delivered (ntfy down) | It is queued like other alerts and retried. |
```

**M13. §13 Tooling** (A, C, D).
```markdown
- Task image, mise line: "**mise, pinned, 3-day cooldown:** `claude codex opencode uv ast-grep go`. `gh` is omitted: GitHub goes through the `gh.*` ops." Add: "The image Go is the only Go a task runs by default (`GOTOOLCHAIN=local`, §4.2). mise keeps it current; apt `golang-1.26` would trip `GOTOOLCHAIN=local` on more repos."
- MCP servers: change "`agentbox mcp` (registry tools, including `gh.*` and the bus)" to "`agentbox mcp` (registry tools, including `gh.*`, the bus, `ask.quote`, `ask.status` and `ask.batch`)".
- New bullet: "**Generated tool descriptions.** Each starts with the op's ask-cost line (§5.1). The `ask.*` descriptions tell the agent to: quote its whole plan once before acting; send a push and its PR (or, on a private remote, a push and its replies to one PR) as one `ask.batch`; wait with `ask.status` or `agentbox ask wait <id>`, or end the task normally; and stop at the local branch when an ask is denied or the budget is spent. They also say that quote reasons are coarse (`signed` covers every signed cause), that reply batches need a private remote, that the PR number for a reply batch comes from the prompt or `gh.pr.read` (a tainting read), never from `ask.status`, and that no agent-wide cap is reported. A CLI that can't hold a 25 s tool call is told to use `agentbox ask wait` from the shell (D-G10)."
- New bullet: "**In-task CLI** (part of the read-only agentbox binary; not MCP tools): `agentbox why` and `agentbox ask wait` (§4.2)."
```

**M14. Appendix C** (A, C). In the podman argv: after `--log-driver=none` add `--dns=none`; after the line that mounts the git socket dir add `-v /run/agentbox-witness/t/T42:/run/agentbox/witness:ro \`; and after `-e HOME=/home/agent -e CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_NONESSENTIAL_TRAFFIC=1 -e CODEX_HOME=/home/agent/.codex \` add:
```sh
  -e HTTPS_PROXY=http://127.0.0.1:8080 -e https_proxy=http://127.0.0.1:8080 \
  -e NO_PROXY=127.0.0.1,localhost,::1 -e no_proxy=127.0.0.1,localhost,::1 \
  -e GOPROXY=http://127.0.0.1:8083,https://proxy.golang.org,direct -e GOSUMDB=sum.golang.org -e GOTOOLCHAIN=local \
  -e npm_config_audit=false -e npm_config_fund=false -e npm_config_update_notifier=false \
  -e NO_UPDATE_NOTIFIER=1 -e PIP_DISABLE_PIP_VERSION_CHECK=1 \
```
Add under the block:
```markdown
- **Go env.** This argv is for an agent whose allowlist holds `proxy.golang.org:443`. For any other agent, rootd passes `-e GOPROXY=off` instead of the `GOPROXY` and `GOSUMDB` pair. If the admin set `go.private`, rootd adds `-e GOPRIVATE=<patterns>` from `control/egress/<agent>.goprivate`, already validated. taskrun unsets `GOFLAGS`, `GONOSUMDB`, `GONOPROXY` and `GOINSECURE` before it starts the CLI, in case the image sets them, and starts the Go module filter on `127.0.0.1:8083` inside the container. No new mount and no published port: the filter uses the existing gate and proxy sockets.
- **Proxy env.** `HTTPS_PROXY` is the existing egress path, now explicit. `HTTP_PROXY` and `ALL_PROXY` are never set.
- **DNS.** The image's `/etc/resolv.conf` is `nameserver 127.0.0.1` with `options timeout:1 attempts:1`, and nothing listens on port 53. If the pinned podman doesn't leave the image's file in place under `--dns=none` (C-G2), rootd bind-mounts a root-owned read-only copy of it instead (R26).
- **Witness.** Writing a FIFO on a read-only mount is permitted, like connecting to a socket: the kernel skips the mount write check for special files. So the task can write the witness but can create nothing there. C-G2 checks this under rootless podman with `keep-id`.
- **Ids.** `T42` stands for a `task_<ULID>` id (§5.1); the example keeps the short form for readability.
```

**M15. Appendix D: Cedar** (B, C, D). Replace the `Ctx` line with:
```
  type Ctx = { approved: Bool, tainted: Bool, workspaceBound: Bool, riskEscalated: Bool, hop: Long, budgetLeftCents: Long, executor: Agent };
  // executor: from rootd task_registry, never from the caller
  // workspaceBound: agentboxd sets it from the registry flag, own scope, auditd's row for W and the task-executor holder kind; never for an attach session; never from the caller
```
Replace the Host read action line with:
```
  action "system.info", "disk.usage", "os.generations", "pkg.search", "bus.agents.list", "gh.issue.read", "gh.pr.read", "ask.quote", "ask.status"
    in [Read] appliesTo { principal: [Agent, Human], resource: [Host], context: Ctx };
  action "ask.batch" in [SideEffect] appliesTo { principal: [Agent, Human], resource: [Host], context: Ctx };
```
Replace `tainted-ask` and add one forbid:
```cedar
@id("tainted-ask") forbid(principal, action in Agentbox::Action::"SideEffect", resource)
  when { context.tainted && !context.approved && !context.workspaceBound };
@id("workspace-bound-memory-only") forbid(principal, action in Agentbox::Action::"SideEffect", resource)
  when { context.workspaceBound && !context.approved }
  unless { action in [Agentbox::Action::"memory.write", Agentbox::Action::"memory.forget"] };
```
Add after the policies:
```markdown
- **`ask.batch`.** No policy permits it with `approved=false`, so `approved-side-effects` makes it Ask. Each member is also authorized as its own action, per chain member (§5.2). `ask.quote` and `ask.batch` admission skip the decide step. `memory-own`, `memory-procedural-never`, `risk-ask` and `budget-ask` are unchanged, so Risk ≥ 4 still sends a bound write to quarantine.
- **Agent-visible names (§5.6).** An agent sees an `@id` only for `hop-limit` and `memory-procedural-never`. An ancestor's block is `cedar.chain`; any other forbid is `cedar.custom`. Every ask reads reason `tainted` or `signed`. Diagnostics go only to audit.
- **Missing bus task id.** Evaluated as a synthetic Task with no optional attributes, so `task-get`, `task-executor`, `task-claim-board` and `task-cancel-originator` all fail on it.
- **Golden tests add:** a context missing `workspaceBound` gives Deny; `workspaceBound=true` on any non-memory action gives the same outcome as `false`; a session-origin write never gets `workspaceBound=true`; `ask.batch` is never Run; a human forbid on `ask.batch` or `ask.quote` gives Deny.
```

**M16. Appendix E: state** (A, B, C, D). rootd.db: replace `task_registry`, `delegations` and `pending`, and add `trace_quota` and `task_denials`:
```sql
CREATE TABLE task_registry(task TEXT PRIMARY KEY, parent TEXT, agent TEXT NOT NULL, uid INTEGER NOT NULL, chain TEXT NOT NULL CHECK(json_valid(chain)),
                           origin TEXT NOT NULL, trace TEXT NOT NULL, deadline INTEGER NOT NULL, started_at INTEGER, ended_at INTEGER,
                           end_reason TEXT CHECK(end_reason IN ('completed','failed','canceled','timeout','deadline','parent_stopped')),
                           asks_budget INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK(asks_budget BETWEEN 0 AND 10),  -- §4.2 ask budget
                           asks_used INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0, asks_granted INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
                           asks_created INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,  -- every ask row, free re-pends included; drives the recon alerts
                           quote_items INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0 CHECK(quote_items >= 0), quote_deny INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
                           CHECK(asks_used + asks_granted <= asks_budget)) STRICT;
CREATE INDEX task_registry_parent ON task_registry(parent, ended_at);
CREATE TABLE trace_quota(trace TEXT PRIMARY KEY, quote_items INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0 CHECK(quote_items >= 0),
                         quote_deny INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0, asks_created INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0) STRICT;  -- per-trace quote rollup (§5.2)
CREATE TABLE delegations(task TEXT PRIMARY KEY, parent TEXT NOT NULL, executor TEXT NOT NULL, allowed_transitions TEXT NOT NULL CHECK(json_valid(allowed_transitions)),
                         expires_at INTEGER NOT NULL, version INTEGER NOT NULL,
                         asks INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0) STRICT;  -- written at bus.task.send (to:) or at a skills: claim; asks = the parent's grant
-- pending.op: plaintext until a final state, then audit classes applied (§5.2)
CREATE TABLE pending(id TEXT PRIMARY KEY, task TEXT NOT NULL, agent TEXT NOT NULL, op TEXT NOT NULL CHECK(json_valid(op)), bound TEXT NOT NULL CHECK(json_valid(bound)),
                     h TEXT UNIQUE NOT NULL, nonce TEXT UNIQUE NOT NULL,
                     state TEXT NOT NULL CHECK(state IN ('pending','approved','executing','executed','refused','failed','indeterminate','denied','expired')),
                     signer TEXT, reason TEXT,  -- a §5.6 code, optionally ':<what>'; 'batch_denied' for an unstruck batch member
                     result TEXT CHECK(result IS NULL OR json_valid(result)),  -- outputs; ask.status returns only the agentbox-generated ones (§5.3)
                     match_key TEXT NOT NULL,   -- SHA-256 of JCS{op, structural args, bound inputs}; body hashes taken before the op marker (§5.4)
                     batch TEXT, batch_idx INTEGER,  -- member of an ask.batch row; NULL otherwise
                     expires_at INTEGER NOT NULL, approved_at INTEGER, created_at INTEGER NOT NULL,
                     CHECK(expires_at > created_at), CHECK((batch IS NULL) = (batch_idx IS NULL))) STRICT;
CREATE INDEX pending_task ON pending(task, state);
CREATE INDEX pending_match ON pending(task, match_key);
CREATE INDEX pending_batch ON pending(batch, batch_idx) WHERE batch IS NOT NULL;
CREATE TABLE task_denials(task TEXT NOT NULL, id TEXT NOT NULL, code TEXT NOT NULL, by TEXT NOT NULL, created_at INTEGER NOT NULL,
                          PRIMARY KEY(task, id)) STRICT;  -- floor denials rootd minted and Cedar denials agentboxd sent; deleted after the task's witness check
```
On `ids`, add the comment `-- apps: name = '<agent>/<app>' (per-owner names, §4.1)`. The daemon-ask pool is counted from `pending` joined to `task_registry` on `origin='daemon'` over the last 24 h; no new column. taint.db: the `taint` comment becomes `-- epoch moves only on clean→tainted, on clear, and on a signed workspace.restore (§5.2); rows are never deleted, so epochs never repeat`. memory.db: replace `facts` and the FTS line with:
```sql
CREATE TABLE facts(id TEXT PRIMARY KEY, scope TEXT NOT NULL, state TEXT NOT NULL CHECK(state IN ('quarantined','active','forgotten')),
                   taint INTEGER NOT NULL, author TEXT NOT NULL, approved_by TEXT, expires_at INTEGER, title TEXT, body TEXT NOT NULL,
                   meta TEXT CHECK(json_valid(meta)), created_at INTEGER NOT NULL,
                   ws_subject TEXT, ws_epoch INTEGER,  -- set by a workspace-bound write (§8); kept while quarantined, and while forgotten in the same epoch; NULL after release
                   CHECK((ws_subject IS NULL) = (ws_epoch IS NULL)),
                   CHECK(ws_epoch IS NULL OR (taint = 1 AND scope LIKE 'agent/%' AND ws_subject LIKE 'workspace:%'))) STRICT;
CREATE INDEX facts_ws ON facts(ws_subject, ws_epoch) WHERE ws_epoch IS NOT NULL;
CREATE VIRTUAL TABLE memory_fts USING fts5(id UNINDEXED, title, body);     -- active facts without a binding, and released facts (§8)
CREATE VIRTUAL TABLE memory_fts_ws USING fts5(id UNINDEXED, title, body);  -- active workspace-bound facts only; matched, never bm25-ranked
```
New store, `data/cache/<agent>/index.db` (agentbox-proxy, `synchronous=FULL`):
```sql
-- sha256, size and h1 never change after insert
CREATE TABLE entries(key TEXT PRIMARY KEY,  -- 'go:<module>@<version>.<mod|zip>', unescaped, canonical version
  sha256 TEXT NOT NULL, size INTEGER NOT NULL, h1 TEXT NOT NULL,  -- the checksum-database hash the fill matched
  upstream TEXT NOT NULL, filled_by_task TEXT NOT NULL, filled_at INTEGER NOT NULL,
  used_at INTEGER NOT NULL) STRICT;  -- updated at most once an hour
CREATE TABLE fill_days(day_start INTEGER PRIMARY KEY, bytes INTEGER NOT NULL) STRICT;  -- Unix ms of 00:00 UTC; daily fill budget
-- no availability column: the task's filter checks availability live (§6.2)
```
`control/budgets.toml` keys added in v5.3:

| Key | Default | Used by |
|---|---|---|
| `cache.agent_max_bytes` | 8 GiB | A: per-agent store limit (kernel qgroup) |
| `cache.task_fill_max_bytes`, `cache.task_fill_max_files` | 2 GiB, 20000 | A: fills per task |
| `cache.agent_fill_day_bytes` | 4 GiB | A: fills per agent per UTC day |
| `cache.max_idle_days` | 30 | A: nightly eviction |
| `memory.bound_max_per_epoch` | 50 | B: bound facts per (W, epoch) |
| `memory.bound_stale_ms` | 604800000 (7 days) | B: epoch age limit for binding |
| `memory.digest_interval_ms` | 21600000 (6 h) | B: ntfy digest |
| `keys.agent_day_share.<agent>` | the box day cap ÷ the number of agents | C: per-agent spend share |
| `ask.per_task`, `ask.per_daemon_task`, `ask.daemon_per_day` | 3, 1, 5 | D: ask budgets |
| `ask.agent_24h`, `ask.agent_ops_24h` | 20, 40 | D: per-agent caps (the 20 was a v5.2 constant) |
| `ask.quote_items_task`, `ask.quote_items_trace` | 32, 64 | D: quote caps |
| `ask.recon_items`, `ask.recon_deny`, `ask.enum_deny_trace` | 24, 4, 16 | D: task and trace alerts |
| `ask.agent_recon_items_24h`, `ask.agent_recon_deny_24h` | 128, 32 | D: per-agent alert |

## 9. Decisions on every open question

| Id | Question | Decision | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| R1 | The ≤5 asks per 100 tasks gate fails (model: 108.7) | Replace it with the section 2.7 gate | The floor alone gives 91 per 100 on delivery work with no taint; a gate the floor fails by design can't ship |
| R2 | Which tasks does the benchmark grade? | 30 tasks drawn from the baseline mix (27 deliver something, 3 read-only) | A benchmark that never delivers passes any ask gate |
| R3 | Keep the egress (`net.allow_domain`) batch family? | Drop it from v1 | It saves 0 asks (hosts arrive one at a time), lets a deputy host ride with wanted hosts, and makes a "no" cost the good hosts (I1, I2) |
| R4 | Who may add a deputy host (`proxy.golang.org`, `sum.golang.org`)? | Humans only: `agentbox egress allow --agent <a> <host>:<port>`, still signed with the typed host:port and the DEPUTY HOST line. An agent request gets `HUMAN_ONLY{deputy_host}`. Never in `common.list`. | Whether an agent may use a data-out deputy is a per-agent setup choice, not a per-task need; this removes injection steering and costs 0 asks |
| R5 | What can a batch hold? | Two shapes: `push+pr` (any remote) and `push+reply` (private remote, 1–3 replies on one PR whose number the human types). `gh.issue.comment` never batches. | Covers all 74 batched deliveries in the model and closes rider comments (D attack 7) and the combined attack (section 8.2) |
| R6 | One deny and dedupe key | `matchKey` = op + structural args + bound inputs. Free text (`reason`, `title`, `body`, `content`, commit messages) is out; body hashes, taken before rootd appends the op marker, are in. One key for dedupe, deny-final and executed results. | Rewording can't re-open a no; a new body is a real new question; a title-only change isn't |
| R7 | How does a human deny part of a batch? | A strike list. The CLI needs `--strike <idx>,…` or `--all`. Unstruck members get `batch_denied`, which is not final. | Makes "no" cheap and precise, and removes the approve-everything-or-restart pressure |
| R8 | How fine are ask reasons? | Agents see `tainted` or `signed` (plus `unchecked` in quotes). Fine reasons appear only in `agentbox task show`. | Fine reasons map custom policies and push-policy coverage (D attack 8) and break quote = submit |
| R9 | Two things called "budget" | The ask budget is `asks` and `ASK_CAP{task_budget}`; spend is `KEYS_BUDGET`; Cedar's `budget-ask` shows as `signed`; `budgetLeftCents` keeps its name | One word per thing |
| R10 | The agent-facing taint shape | `taint = {tainted, workspaceBound}` everywhere | These are the two facts an agent can act on; kinds and sources leak sibling and attach activity |
| R11 | Three status ops (`op.get`, `taint.status`, `ask.status`) | One op: `ask.status{id?, waitMs?}` | One taint shape by construction, and two fewer ops |
| R12 | Return GitHub PR and comment numbers to tasks? | Not through `ask.status`. Humans see them in `agentbox task show`. This limits `ask.status` only: a task that needs a PR number for `push+reply` takes it from its prompt or reads it with `gh.pr.read`, a tainting read as in v5.2. The reply members bind the number in their H; the human's typed number only confirms it (section 6.3, §5.4 step 7). | Keeps the §5.3 read-taint rule exact (agentbox generates every byte) and closes C attack 3's channel fully |
| R13 | Do asks die when their task ends? | They survive a `completed` end until they expire; any other end denies them | Fixes the v5.2 contradiction between §5.4 and §4.2 step 6 / §5.2, and lets an agent submit and finish |
| R14 | Does a re-pend use budget? | No: the re-pend after `STALE{taint_moved}` and recovery re-offers are free | The agent didn't ask again; the box did |
| R15 | The approve command for a batch | `agentbox approve <batch id>`; members can't be approved alone | One signature over the member H list |
| R16 | Reply timing for quotes and admissions | One resolver for submits, quotes and admissions. `NOT_FOUND_OR_NOT_OWNED` at receipt + 200 ms; `NEEDS_SIGNATURE`, every `ask.quote` reply and every `ask.batch` reply at receipt + 2 s | One floor hides both existence and Cedar depth; quotes are planning calls, so 2 s is cheap |
| R17 | What does auditd's query call hide? | DENY, WITNESS, GATE_MISMATCH, PAD_OVERRUN, QUOTE, ALERT and cache records | A task must not learn whether its probes raised alerts, or read a sibling's plan (I4) |
| R18 | Recon alert thresholds | Task or trace that ends with 0 asks and ≥4 deny items or ≥24 items; trace with ≥16 deny; agent with ≥128 items or ≥32 deny in 24 h. Alerts only, no cap. | An honest plan quote (one call of up to 8 items, few denies) never trips them; recon needs many denies or many calls |
| R19 | Defaults for bound memory | 50 bound facts per epoch; 7-day staleness bound; digest every 6 h | At 0.3 notes per task and 10–20 tasks a day, a week fills 20–40 notes, so a weekly clear keeps notes working while a neglected box stops accumulating after a week |
| R20 | Can attach sessions write bound notes? | No; their tainted own-scope writes always quarantine | session-home holds subscription tokens (B attack 1) |
| R21 | Do bound notes survive a signed restore? | No. A signed restore moves the epoch, and rootd runs snapshot, restore and clear one at a time per workspace. | Aligns §5.2 with §5.3 and closes B attack 4 |
| R22 | What does a task see when the box-wide daemon pool is full? | `BUSY` (the true reason is audited) | Leak rule 6: a box-wide limit shows one bit only |
| R23 | How many denial codes? | 36. D's codes become `what` values of existing codes (`ASK_CAP`, `CAP_REACHED`, `ADMISSION_REJECTED`, `ASK_DENIED`, `NOT_FOUND_OR_NOT_OWNED`, `DENIED_POLICY`, `BUSY`); two new codes, `HUMAN_ONLY` and `BATCH_ABORTED` | An unknown code fails the in-task check; fewer codes are easier for agents to learn |
| R24 | Default per-agent spend shares | The box day cap split equally, recomputed at `agent.add` and `agent.remove`, unless `budget.set` gives explicit shares (it refuses a sum above the cap) | `KEYS_BUDGET` must depend only on the caller's own share (C attack 2) |
| R25 | Unchecked upstream facts behind the Go cache | A-G3 checks them. If proxy.golang.org redirects `.zip` requests, or a taken-down version still answers 200 on `.info`, the cache ships off (no filter entry; v5.2 Go behavior). If HTTP/2 over CONNECT fails, the filter uses HTTP/1.1. | The cache's safety rests on the first two facts; failing closed costs only speed |
| R26 | What if podman ignores `--dns=none` for the image's resolver file? | rootd bind-mounts a root-owned read-only copy of it | Either way the task's resolver is 127.0.0.1 with a 1 s timeout; C-G2 checks which path applies |
| R27 | One workspace per agent | A precondition for both §0.6 readings | The cache invariant and the bound-fact read predicate are per agent; per-project workspaces would need per-workspace caches and exact-subject binding |
| R28 | Does v5.3 fit the 16 weeks? | No. It adds about 39 engineer-days, so the plan becomes about 20 weeks with 2 engineers (about 34 with 1). Row labels stay as phase names. The kill test stays at the end of the weeks 5–6 phase (about 4 working days later). | Honest capacity; the order of rows doesn't change |
| R29 | Keep the per-agent caps of 20 asks and 40 reviewed ops per 24 h? | Yes, as defaults; `budget.set` raises them; `agentbox status` warns when an agent is within 5 of a cap | They bound fatigue and abuse; at v5.3 rates they bind only after about 18 tasks a day |
| R30 | Bound memory wants regular clears, and each clear makes the next Go task cold | Keep the purge on every clear; recommend a weekly clear | The purge is the cache's safety invariant; the cost is one cold Go task per clear |
| R31 | How much taint do the quiet npm and pip defaults remove? | 0 asks in every task class | The baseline never counted those checks, and the sticky workspace keeps every task tainted anyway |
| R32 | Which non-CONNECT requests may the proxy serve? | Only origin-form `GET` under `/_cache/` on a task's own proxy socket (bare 404, 405 or 503 on misses). Everything else: `EGRESS_WRONG_ROUTE{plain_http}`. | go needs bare 404s to fall back; any other HTTP to the proxy is a misroute |
| R33 | Set `GOFLAGS=-mod=readonly`? | No | An explicit `-mod` makes go ignore `vendor/`; build commands already don't resolve unpinned versions; the filter closes `go get` steering |
| R34 | The longest `ask.status` wait | 25 s; `agentbox ask wait` loops; a CLI that can't hold 25 s is told to use `agentbox ask wait` from the shell | Stays under common tool-call timeouts; D-G10 checks each pinned CLI |
| R35 | How many fixed lines in the generated `CLAUDE.md` and `AGENTS.md`? | Eight (M9) | The shortest set that tells an agent how to act on every new surface |
| R36 | App naming | Per owner: name `<agent>/<app>`, user `app-<M>`, Tailscale Service `svc:<agent>-<app>` | `app.deploy` of another agent's name must create the caller's own app, not reveal a collision (C attack 1) |
| R37 | The write-capable allowlisted-host residual | Stays a §10 residual with the v2 GET-only package broker path. No v5.3 design widens it. | No design in this round fixes or red-teams it; a volume cap alone doesn't stop low-volume secret exfiltration |
| R38 | How does a bound write show up in quotes and replies? | It quotes as `run` and returns `{bound: true}`; an over-cap or stale write returns `MEMORY_QUARANTINED` | Binding is a success, not a refusal |
| R39 | Does `pending.deny` need an approver signature? | No | It only removes capability, like `task.stop` |
| R40 | Does `cache.purge` need an approver signature? | No (admin only) | It deletes only cached public files |
| R41 | What bar does "every red-team attack is closed" mean? | v5.3 closes every attack that its own new mechanisms create, except a bounded case that §10 states with its argument (only A-11). A pre-existing v5.2 residual may stay open only if this file shows that v5.3 doesn't widen it. The nine attacks stated but not closed are listed in section 10.5, each with its v5.2 basis and the reason. | One bar for all four proposals. The pre-existing residuals need work outside these designs (the v2 GET-only package broker, gate-side taint checks, `agentbox-gate`; R37), so "not widened" is the honest test for them |
| R46 | How does GitHub stop a no-ask delivery from merging itself or bypassing review? | A require-PR-before-merge rule blocks direct pushes to the default branch for non-bypass actors; every lane ruleset is an enterprise ruleset with zero bypass actors (R84) and enforcement bound `active` (evaluate/disabled is gate-gone and hard revocation); the merge-gate state is in every 60 s signed snapshot; and the resolved CODEOWNERS membership (team expanded, or named-user entries) is bound in each snapshot, with a catch-all `* @<human-team>` as the first effective rule and no later empty-owner pattern | `contents:write` can push to the default branch when the ruleset is disabled/evaluate, and `require_last_push_approval`/CODEOWNERS apply only to PR merges (attack 8); the CODEOWNERS blob hash is constant no matter who joins the team (attack 7); a CODEOWNERS-required review covers only matched paths, so an unowned path would merge on any write-access approval |
| R47/R48 | What caps a no-ask delivery? | A human-attention threshold on the reviewable SOURCE diff: 25 files, 600 lines, 256 KiB, no single file over 300 lines or 64 KiB; recognized lockfiles and vendored trees excluded and summarized by depcheck; over any cap falls back to a signed `ask.batch`. The 300-file / 20,000-line / 1 MiB render window stays only as the header truncation flag | A 290-file mechanical refactor fully renders but no human reads 19,500 lines, so one hunk hides in plain sight (attack 15); excluding lockfiles keeps dependency updates on the no-ask lane |
| R55 | TPM scope, token exfiltration, and detection under broker compromise | The TPM covers at-rest key theft only; bind the org IP allow list with installed-App inheritance off, holding the box egress IP, the verifier egress IP and the owner's review IPs (R87), plus rate-limited, off-box-streamed TPM JWT signing; make an off-box verifier with its own credential the primary detection authority, and relabel the on-box tripwire/sweep as liveness only | A live broker compromise mints and exfiltrates a token usable off-box for about an hour (attack 21); rootd has no network, so both credentials run through the one networked tier and the on-box divergence check is blindable (attack 20) |
| R60 | How is the fork channel bound? | Bind `forks_count==0` and `allow_forking=false`, and watch the audit log for `repo.fork` off-box; the fork network list is not bound, because it is not reliably readable, and push rulesets cover the whole fork network (attack 2-11); any observed fork, `forks_count` increment, or `allow_forking=true` seen even once is permanent, non-auto-resumable revocation requiring re-enrollment, not a resume-when-the-flag-reverts suspension | A brief forking-enabled window makes a persistent cross-org fork that keeps the full history and leaks every delivered SHA after the flag reverts (attack 9) |
| R61 | How is machine-account spread detected? | Bound by the org, not by enumeration: the mirror org holds only lane repos; the verifier binds the org's full repo list (every repo an enrolled lane remote), the write App's `repository_selection=selected` from `GET /orgs/{org}/installations` and the verifier App's repo list, and watches `integration_installation.repositories_added`. The on-box enumeration with the write App's token is liveness only. `/user/installations/{id}/repositories` is at most an E-G2 bonus check | An installation token lists only its own installation's repos; `/user/installations/{id}/repositories` lists only what the authenticated user can reach, so a classic PAT can't be assumed to list another App's installation; the on-box token is broker-controlled (attacks 6, 10) |
| R62 | Which paths does the no-ask binder refuse, and which does it only flag? | Refuse the §4.2 steering set, the auto-run-on-open and merge-gate paths, and `.gitattributes`; flag (not refuse) build/manifest/test-autoload files | Auto-run-on-open paths fire on a passive gesture and must be refused; `.gitattributes` can hide a diff; refusing build/test files would gut dependency and feature deliveries and break the ask model (attack 12 control half; attack 13 narrowed) |
| R64 | How does the header avoid being a beacon or a flood? | Emit it as entity-escaped HTML, Markdown-neutralized, never inside `img`/`a`; rank depcheck high-risk only on anomaly signals and show routine bumps as a separate low-risk count | Round 1 printed agent-controlled depcheck values into the Markdown body, so a crafted resolved URL rendered as a Camo image beacon (attack 2); over-flagging every bump habituates the owner (attack 14) |
| R65 | How is delivery volume bounded? | Single-digit caps: `lane.per_remote_day=3`, `lane.open_per_box=5`, `lane.tainted_agent_24h=3`, `lane.open_per_agent=3` (open lane PRs per authoring agent, equal to the tainted per-agent cap so it never binds below it and the model is unchanged); all are hard caps, and no acknowledgement unlocks more (R80) | Generous caps let a compromised agentboxd bury one malicious draft among plausible ones in a batch-review the owner rubber-stamps (attacks 17, 22) |
| R66 | How is the cross-project audience and the review surface handled? | The enrollment render shows and type-confirms the cross-project audience and the owner review-surface duties (web Files-changed source view only); recommend one project per lane-enrolled agent and apply a project-subtree refusal | One-workspace-per-agent (R27) precludes a content-level cross-project fix, so this is a stated narrowing; the owner's review surface is the premise the whole lane rests on (attacks 4, 13, 22 narrowed) |
| R67 | How is a revoked lane kept revoked under compromise? | Keep the admin-set `forbidden` lane state, read at the rootd floor, Cedar- and agentboxd-independent | A compromised agentboxd or a Cedar edit must not revive a revoked lane |
| R68 | How is a diff-hiding `linguist-generated` file caught? | Compute the render-visible changed-file set (head-tree gitattributes plus linguist heuristics) and refuse the no-ask lane if any changed source file would be auto-collapsed | A file marked `linguist-generated` is hidden from GitHub's default Files-changed diff, so an owner never sees it (attack 12) |
| R69 | How is a beacon in a committed renderable file caught? | Refuse the no-ask lane for any renderable file (`md`, `ipynb`, `svg`, `html`, `rst`) carrying any external resource reference (route to a signed `ask.batch` where §5.4 strips it); scan Markdown, raw-HTML, SVG and notebook surfaces | A Camo beacon in a committed renderable file fires on the owner's rendered, image or notebook diff view; a round-1 flag is an advisory line a PR-body lure talks past (attacks 3, 16) |
| R70 | How is fabricated authorship handled? | Force the commit author and committer to a fixed agentbox lane noreply identity, and add a fixed header line that all commit subjects and trailers are agent-supplied and unverified | Printable-ASCII trailers pass the §5.4 escaper and GitHub renders them as co-author avatars and apparent prior review (attack 18) |
| R71 | How is an auto-run-on-open payload blocked off-box? | Bind a restricted-file-paths push ruleset (blocking `.github/workflows`, `.devcontainer`, `.devcontainer.json`, `.vscode`, `.github/codespaces`, `.github/dependabot.yml`, `.github/copilot-*`, `.gitattributes`, `CODEOWNERS` for every actor, the owner included, zero bypass actors, as an enterprise push ruleset; if none is available the lane does not ship) and the readable Codespaces outcomes (R86); bound in each signed snapshot; off-box-alert on any such push | The on-box binder is never reached by a direct GitHub API call from a compromised broker, and GitHub gates only `.github/workflows` by the Workflows permission, not `.devcontainer` or `.vscode` (attack 19, critical) |
| R72 | Is the one-repo write-token scope trustworthy? | Verify the minted write token's repository scope is exactly the one bound repo before any push; the authoritative bound is the installation's bound selected set | GitHub caps a token only at the installation's repos; narrowing is caller-chosen, so a compromised broker can mint an un-narrowed token (attack 11) |
| R73 | How is the audience bounded? | Bind the effective reader set (org base permission, owners, watchers); refuse enrollment or force `none` if all org members can read; recommend owner-only; re-check in the sweep | An org base permission of Read grants every org member read on the private repo and @mention/watch access, so the round-1 collaborator-only count was misleadingly small (attack 5) |
| R74 | Does suspension scrub already-delivered content? | On suspend, revoke, or any audience/visibility drift, alert with every open lane PR and pushed `agent/*` branch, auto-close the PRs through the write App, and list the branches. The agent-branch ruleset blocks every actor, the App and the owner included, from deleting them (R82), so deletion, routine cleanup of merged `agent/**` branches, and CODEOWNERS edits need an enterprise owner to lift the ruleset; the lift changes `settingsHash`, suspends the lane and needs re-enrollment (about 15 min per cleanup, section 2.9) | Suspension only refuses the next delivery; it does not scrub historic branches and draft PRs, so a later reader inherits pre-positioned tainted content (attack 6 persistence) |
| R75 | Does the lane keep asks at the claimed ~39.7? | No: 78.7 at the headline 10 tasks per agent per day. Keep the round-2 single-digit caps; ~39.7 holds only at about 4 tasks per day or fewer with zero content refusals; E-G9's ≤45 target is graded at the model's throughput, not the ideal | `lane.tainted_agent_24h=3` binds above about 4 tasks per day, and the cap was lowered for attacks 17 and 22 without re-walking the ~39.7 claim (section 2.9); the 78.7 itself is corrected to 92.0 by R78 |
| R76 | What is the remedy when the binder refuses a control path? | On an enrolled mirror, control-path changes are refused outright: `ask.batch` admission returns `HUMAN_ONLY`, remedy: apply the change by hand on the CI repo, never on the mirror. Each counts as 1 human-gate action, a hand change on the CI repo | The enterprise push ruleset has zero bypass actors and blocks those paths for every actor, the App and the owner included, so a signed `push+pr` batch and the owner's own push to the mirror would both be rejected (section 2.9) |
| R77 | Is Proposal E part of v5.3? | Superseded: the owner rejected E on 2026-11-20 (section 7). Original decision: yes, as an opt-in lane, off by default, for inert private mirror repos in Enterprise Cloud orgs only. Each of the second red-team's 12 attacks has a decision in section 7.5b: 9 closed (R82–R87, R89), 2 narrowed (2-3, 2-11), 1 refused by cutting Team orgs from scope (R88). Apply section 7.4. E-G2, E-G3, E-G5 and E-G6 are ship gates: if one fails on the live Enterprise Cloud test org, the lane does not ship and v5.3 is A–D. Plan on 108.7 for CI repos | Holding the lane with its attacks unrecorded left the question open. Every attack now has a written mechanism a critic can check, each GitHub fact it rests on is cited or gate-pinned with a fallback (section 7.3), and the caps are no looser than round 2. No §10 bound outside the lane changes |
| R78 | What does the lane save on the real box? | 92.0 human-gate actions per 100 tasks (91.0 asks plus 1.0 hand changes on the CI repo) at 3 agents, 10 tasks a day each, one draft review a day, only with every remote an inert Enterprise Cloud mirror; 108.7 for CI repos. The mirror-to-CI copies (16.7 per 100, 5 a day) are counted in a separate human-gate row. E-G9 becomes at most 96 on that setup and at most 81 with one agent | `lane.open_per_box = 5` is box-wide, so the box admits 5 lane deliveries a day, not 9; the round-2 model counted one agent. Section 1 names three agents, and a once-daily review is the realistic default on a home box. An ordinary CI repo can't meet the preconditions (q about 100%) (section 2.9) |
| R79 | Do per-project workspaces change the lane's figure? | No: 92.0 either way. Taint per (agent, project) saves about 0 asks; `lane.tainted_agent_24h` stays per agent and does not follow the project. They count only for closing attack 4. This replaces the earlier 58.7 | 81 of 100 tasks taint themselves, so a project workspace is tainted again within 1 to 2 tasks. The per-agent cap bounds attacks 17 and 22 by volume, and scaling it by project count would weaken that bound. `lane.open_per_box` binds either way |
| R80 | How are `HUMAN_ONLY` control-path deliveries, the daily cap and the lane digest counted? | `HUMAN_ONLY` deliveries (about 1.0 per 100 tasks) are hand changes on the CI repo, never pushes to the mirror: 0 asks, but counted as 1 human-gate action each in the 92.0 headline and shown in the human-gate row; not a saving. `lane.per_remote_day = 3` is a hard cap. The lane-digest acknowledgement is only a pre-merge review duty: not an ask, and it never unlocks a further delivery | A hand change on the CI repo is more human work than the signed ask it replaces, and the gate exists to measure human attention. The round-2 text called 3 both a cap and a floor that an acknowledgement unlocks; the ask model and the attack 17 and 22 bound both need a cap, so the cap wins |
| R81 | Should any cap be raised to recover the 78.7? | No. Keep `lane.open_per_box = 5`, `lane.per_remote_day = 3` and `lane.tainted_agent_24h = 3`. No §10 bound outside the lane changes. The owner can review drafts twice a day (78.7) | The caps bound attacks 17 and 22; raising them trades a fixed safety bound for about 13 gate actions per 100 tasks |
| R82 | Which refs can the delivery App write? | Only `agent/**` branches, by create and fast-forward. Enterprise rulesets with zero bypass actors restrict creation, update and deletion of every other branch (the default branch keeps only the PR-merge path), restrict creation, update and deletion of every tag (which blocks releases), and block force pushes and deletion on `agent/**`. The empty tag list is bound, and two off-box snapshot fields cover what rulesets can't: every ref in `GET /git/refs` is the default branch or under `refs/heads/agent/`, and no release exists, draft included. Any foreign ref or release on an enrolled lane is permanent revocation; if GitHub accepts a non-heads/tags ref or a draft release from the App in E-G3, the lane does not ship. Executor separation inside `agent/**` stays on-box only, a stated residual. The zero-bypass rules also stop the owner deleting `agent/**` branches (R74) | `contents:write` reaches every ref no ruleset covers; rulesets target only `refs/heads` and `refs/tags`, `POST /git/refs` takes any name under `refs` with two slashes, and a draft release makes no tag; the `agent/<executor>/*` check lives in the broker a compromise skips (attack 2-1) |
| R83 | What else may react to a push? | Nothing. Refuse enrollment, and suspend on drift, if the org has any GitHub App installation other than the write App and the verifier App; require OAuth app access restrictions on (owner attestation, checked off-box in the audit log); refuse any applicable ruleset with a `workflows` rule; both Apps installed on the org, not a user account; Dependabot security updates and alerts off, 0 Dependabot secrets, no `.github/dependabot.yml`, code scanning default setup not configured, and Copilot coding agent disabled for the repo (attested, outcome-checked). The verifier App has exactly organization administration read, members read, organization webhooks read and repository webhooks read, plus repository metadata, contents and administration read | A CI or deploy App builds pushed branches with its own secrets whatever the Actions setting; other installations' repo selections can't be read, so the refusal is org-wide; Dependabot runs on Actions runners even with Actions disabled and gets Dependabot secrets (attacks 2-2, 2-3, 2-4) |
| R84 | How are ruleset bypass actors checked? | Every lane ruleset is an enterprise ruleset with zero bypass actors. The verifier reads `bypass_actors` with an enterprise-owner read credential and binds `bypass_actors=[]` in the signed snapshot. The App must read `current_user_can_bypass=never`, not `exempt` | The rulesets REST API returns `bypass_actors` only to callers with write access to the ruleset, so the App and rootd can't see a deploy key, another App or a role listed there (attack 2-5) |
| R85 | What does rootd's floor trust? | Only a `lane-settings/v1` snapshot signed by the off-box verifier's key (enrolled in `lane.set`), carrying a timestamp and the repo `node_id`, under 5 minutes old, with a `settingsHash` equal to the grant's, and passing rootd's own evaluation of the pinned predicate list (section 7.3; golden-tested in E-G7), including that rootd's configured egress IP is a bound allow-list entry. Otherwise `lane_unavailable`. rootd checks signatures, freshness and predicates, not GitHub. The org Codespaces policy, OAuth restrictions and Copilot coding agent setting can't be read by anyone, so the attacks they cover are "closed by outcome checks, policy attested" | rootd has no network, and every on-box re-read runs through `agentbox-git`, which can forge a clean snapshot (attacks 20, 2-6). A hash compare alone passes a verifier bug or a stale grant signed over a bad snapshot. 5 minutes bounds replay while letting a 60 s signing loop miss a few rounds |
| R86 | How is the Codespaces precondition checked? | By readable outcomes: no `.devcontainer/`, `.devcontainer.json`, `.vscode/` or `.github/codespaces/` path in the default-branch tree (bound) and none addable (push ruleset); 0 codespaces for the repo in `GET /orgs/{org}/codespaces` and no `codespaces.*` audit-log event (off-box); and the owner's typed "disabled" policy confirmation, labelled an attestation | GitHub has no per-repository Codespaces switch, and the org policy has a PUT but no GET, so "Codespaces off" can't be re-read (attacks 1, 2-7) |
| R87 | How is the IP allow list bound? | The org IP allow list is enabled; installed-App inheritance is OFF; no entry is "Managed by" an App; the entries are exactly the box egress IP, the verifier egress IP and the owner's review IPs, each typed at enrollment, bound and shown in the render; both Apps are installed on the org, not a user account; rootd's configured egress IP is one of the entries. The verifier reads them through GraphQL `ipAllowListEntries` with its owner read credential, from its own entry | Checked on the live docs 2026-11-20: the org list already restricts installation, user-to-server and personal access tokens, owners included. The installed-Apps setting only copies each App's declared IPs in as uneditable "Managed by the NAME GitHub App" entries synced to the App creator's list, which widens the list and breaks an exact entry set. The list does not restrict an App installed on a user account. A list without the verifier's IP locks the verifier out, and no snapshot gets signed; a review IP for the verifier would merge two trust roles (attacks 2-8, 2-9) |
| R88 | Which orgs can enroll? | Only orgs inside a GitHub Enterprise Cloud enterprise. Enrollment refuses every other org, Team orgs included, and the q sensitivity counts every Team org repo as unable to meet the preconditions | IP allow lists, the audit log API and enterprise rulesets exist only on Enterprise Cloud; on Team, org owners can always edit org rulesets (attack 2-10) |
| R89 | Does the delivery App need `issues:write`? | No. Drop it. Labels go through the issues-labels endpoint under pull requests write; E-G5 proves it on the test org | `issues:write` lets a compromised broker post issues and comments on every bound repo, a publish channel with no gate (attack 2-12) |

*R46–R89 are Proposal E's decisions (R77–R81 adopt the lane as opt-in and correct its model; R82–R89 answer the second red-team); R42–R45 were the round-1 lane draft that round 2 supersedes.*

## 10. How to apply

### 10.1 Order
Applied 2026-11-20 without Proposal E: steps 1–4 for A–D; section 7.4 and the E parts of sections 10.2–10.5 were not applied.

1. Apply each proposal's numbered spec changes (sections 3.3, 4.3, 5.3 and 6.3) in the order A, B, C, D, then section 7.4 for Proposal E (opt-in, R77).
2. Where a change names a merged block (M1–M16), paste that block from section 8.4 once, and skip any per-proposal wording for the same spot.
3. Then update §15 and PLAN.md (sections 10.3 and 10.4 below), and add the Round 6 entry to §17 (section 10.5).
4. Set the header to v5.3, dated 2026-11-19, and add a Round 6 row to the reviews table that points at this file.
5. If E-G2, E-G3, E-G5 or E-G6 fails on the live Enterprise Cloud test org, revert section 7.4 and the E parts of sections 10.2–10.4, and ship A–D only (R77).

| 3 | auditd never deletes taint rows; `taint.get` returns the holder kind (B) | A taint row survives `taint.clear` with its epoch incremented; `taint.get` names `task` or `session` for each writer | 0.25 |
| 4 | denial codes table and codegen, `ops.Deny`, `gen/denial.v1.json`, the in-task check, ULID ids, the DENY sink check (C); batch and quote registry fields, the three schemas, Cedar actions, golden lists (D); the `memory.write` title as hash class (B) | C-G1, D-G1, D-G2 | 4.25 |
| 5–6 | Go task env, quiet npm and pip, `go.private.set`, Go in the image (A); HTTP-aware forwarders, `agentbox why`, the proxy env, `--dns=none`, the fixed lines, the witness FIFO (C) | A-G1, A-G2, C-G2 | 2.75 |
| 7–8 | the proxy's Go module cache, the taskrun filter, `data/cache/<agent>`, the service btrfs filter (A); proxy, keys and git denial formats, dial budgets, per-agent day shares, the git 404 floor (C) | A-G3–A-G14, C-G3, C-G4 | 8.5 |
| 9–10 | DEPUTY HOST line, `HUMAN_ONLY`, `agentbox egress allow` (A); `workspaceBindable`, `workspaceBound`, the two policies (B); Cedar and floor mapping, owner-scoped resolution, reply floors, `pending.deny`, the witness check, `agentbox task show` (C); the ask budget, `ask.quote`, `ask.status`, `ask.batch`, the match key, the strike list, recon alerts, the daemon pool (D) | A-G15, B-G1, B-G2, C-G5–C-G7, D-G3–D-G10, X-G1, X-G2, X-G6 | 11.0 |
| 11–12 | snapshot taint at RESULT, restore moves the epoch, per-workspace serialization (B); per-owner app names (C) | B-G3 | 1.0 |
| 13–14 | depcheck and the dependency report, the purge in `taint.clear`, the attach dependency gate (A); workspace-bound memory, decoupled release, cap, staleness bound, digest, attach exclusion and scan (B); bus and memory codes, bus ULIDs (C); ask grants and the trace rollup (D) | A-G16–A-G18, B-G4–B-G12, C-G7 (rerun), C-G8, D-G11, X-G3–X-G5 | 9.75 |
| 15–16 | the benchmark on the baseline mix; behavior metrics | Replace "≤5 asks per 100 tasks" with the gate in section 2.7 of PROPOSALS-v5.3.md (D-G12). Add A-G19, B-G13, C-G9. | 1.5 |

**Proposal E adds about 15 engineer-days on top** (section 7.7; opt-in, R77): row 4 +0.5 (registry, Cedar and schema for `repo.deliver` and `lane.*`); rows 7–8 +7.5 (the delivery App with the TPM key and TPM-signed JWT behind the org IP allow list, `lane-settings/v1`, the ref and tag rulesets, the installation and `workflows`-rule refusals, the binder refusals, the entity-escaped header, the draft-PR template, recovery); rows 9–10 +6.0 (rootd `lane.set`/`lane.revoke`, the floor with its signature-and-freshness check and the lowered caps, the off-box verifier with its signed snapshots, scrub-on-suspend, `agentbox lane`); rows 15–16 +1.0 (the lane benchmark and the injection set). Gates: E-G1 (row 4), E-G2–E-G5 (rows 7–8), E-G6–E-G8 (rows 9–10), E-G9 (rows 15–16). **Total about 54 engineer-days with E; about 39 if a ship gate fails and E is dropped.**

### 10.4 Other PLAN.md changes
- **Defaults, "Team and time":** "2 engineers, about 20 weeks (1 engineer: about 34 weeks). v5.3 adds about 39 engineer-days for proposals A–D (PROPOSALS-v5.3.md, section 10.3). The delivery lane E is opt-in and adds about 15 days, for about 24 weeks (about 39 with 1); if a lane ship gate fails, the lane work stops and the plan returns to about 20 weeks. Row labels name phases, not calendar weeks." (R28, R77)
- **External prerequisites for the delivery lane** (R77; before rows 7–8 start, section 7.7): a test org inside a GitHub Enterprise Cloud enterprise with an inert non-fork mirror repo (forking, Pages and Actions off, no dev container paths, org Codespaces policy disabled, OAuth app restrictions on, no other app installation); the zero-bypass enterprise ruleset set (push, default-branch with the PR-required and CODEOWNERS-human rules, non-agent branch, tag, agent-branch); a CODEOWNERS human team; a Team org and a Free org for the refusal tests; a TPM path for the App key; the org IP allow list with installed-App inheritance, a stable box egress IP and the owner's review IPs; an off-box audit-log stream sink, the verifier App and an enterprise-owner read credential held off-box.
- **Week 2, the THREAT-MODEL.md bullet:** append "and the v5.3 residuals (ARCHITECTURE §10, Round 6)".
- **After "Round-5 findings by build week":** add a table "v5.3 proposals by build week" with the Row and Build columns of section 10.3 above.
- **Kill test:** change "By the end of week 6" to "By the end of the weeks 5–6 phase".

### 10.5 §17: the Round 6 entry (paste)
```markdown
- **Round 6 (v5.2 → v5.3, agent ergonomics).** Five designs: the four ergonomics designs plus the delivery lane (round 2), each red-teamed and scored by three judges, then merged in `PROPOSALS-v5.3.md`. The goal was a box agents can work in, with no §10 control weakened outside the lane. The delivery lane is opt-in, for inert mirror repos in Enterprise Cloud orgs only: a second red-team of round 2 found 12 working attacks, and the revision records each with a decision (9 closed, 2 narrowed, 1 refused by cutting Team orgs from scope). It ships only if its live-org gates pass.

| Proposal | Red-team attacks | Closed in the design | Stated, not closed | Weeks |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| A. Read-only Go module cache | 13 | 11, plus the deputy-host exposure regression | the deputy channel itself (pre-existing); checks turned off with a compromised proxy (new; accepted as bounded) | 5–6, 7–8, 9–10, 13–14 |
| B. Workspace-bound memory notes | 11 | 7 | 4, all pre-existing: two write-capable-host paths, the deputy GET path, broker taint omission | 3, 4, 9–10, 11–12, 13–14 |
| C. Structured denials | 4 | 3, plus the `KEYS_BUDGET` cross-agent bit | `BUSY`'s one box-wide bit (pre-existing) | 4 to 15–16 |
| D. Ask budget, quotes, batches | 9 | 7 | 2, both pre-existing: write-capable hosts; the shared per-agent cap | 4, 9–10, 13–14, 15–16 |
| E. Delivery lane (opt-in, Enterprise Cloud mirrors only) | 22 + 12 from the second red-team | 17 + 9, including the critical review-time Codespaces RCE (off-box push ruleset plus no dev container config on any ref), plus attacks 20 and 21 re-grounded to off-box-primary detection and an IP allow list | 5 narrowed or residual: cross-project content (4), owner-runs-code (13), a render surface the scanner misses (3 residual), a live `agentbox-git` compromise within the App permissions (19/20/21 residual), compromised agentboxd within the caps (22); plus 2 narrowed from the second red-team (OAuth app approval 2-3, fork content before the audit watch 2-11) and 1 refused by scope (Team orgs, 2-10) | 4, 7–8, 9–10, 15–16 |

**The bar for "closed".** An attack on a mechanism v5.3 adds is closed, or accepted as a bounded residual that §10 states with its argument. A v5.2 residual may stay open only if v5.3 doesn't widen it. These are the nine attacks for proposals A–D stated but not closed; the delivery lane's residuals are in section 7.8, each bounded by a zero-bypass enterprise rule, the IP allow list, off-box detection and the human CODEOWNERS merge gate. Attack numbers are rows of the threat tables in `PROPOSALS-v5.3.md` (sections 3.4, 4.4, 5.4, 6.4 and 7.5).

| Attack | Kind | v5.2 basis | Why v5.3 doesn't widen it, or why it is bounded |
|---|---|---|---|
| A-1, B-3: data out through `proxy.golang.org` module paths | v5.2 | §10 "Exfiltration over allowed channels", residual "write-capable allowlisted hosts" (a deputy host is one; §0.5 limit) | The cache adds no destination. A fill or a live check sends `proxy.golang.org` only a checked path and canonical version that the task could send over CONNECT, and lists, `@latest` and checksum lookups still use CONNECT. Exposure narrows: deputy hosts are human-only, never in `common.list` and flagged at approval, and agents without them get `GOPROXY=off`. |
| B-2, D-1: data out through a write-capable allowlisted host | v5.2 | the same row and residual | No design adds an egress destination or an allowlist path. A bound note moves no byte off the box. `net.allow_domain` never batches, so each new host keeps its own signature with the typed host:port. |
| B-10: a bound note carries a token to a later task, which sends it to such a host | v5.2 | the same row, plus §0.6: the sticky-tainted workspace already outlives tasks | The note's only readers hold the same tainted epoch, and could read the same token from a file in that workspace. The way out is still a write-capable host, and v5.3 adds none. |
| B-9: a compromised proxy omits READ-INTENT, so a clean task commits a trusted fact | v5.2 | §10 "Proxy, keys or git compromise", residual "it can omit taint for content it delivers" | B's exemption covers only writes by tainted tasks. A laundered task is clean, so its write takes the v5.2 trusted path unchanged. For Go cache files A narrows the residual: the filter records a second taint. |
| C-2, its `BUSY` part: one box-wide bit between colluding agents | v5.2 | no §10 row. Each box-wide limit already returned a typed error: the box's 3 CAS-binding asks and the disk reserve (§5.2 Binding admission), the OS lock (§3.3) and the keys day budget (§6.3) | `BUSY` merges them into one bit with no sub-reason, and per-agent day shares remove the keys bit. Its one new cause, the daemon-ask pool, takes human-visible asks to fill, like the CAS-binding cap. §10 now states it. |
| D-3: one task uses up the shared per-agent cap and starves a sibling | v5.2 | no §10 row. §5.4 Ask caps: 20 asks per agent per 24 h, shared by its tasks | In v5.2 one task could make all 20 asks. Now one task makes at most its budget (default 3, at most 10). Only a task with the maximum budget, sending ten 4-member batches, can reach the new 40-ops cap alone. Availability only, and every ask is an ntfy push. §10 now states it. |
| A-11: an agent turns go's checks off, and a compromised proxy feeds it forged `.mod` or `.zip` files | new | none: the Go cache is new | Accepted as bounded (§10). It needs a compromised proxy plus task code that sets `GOSUMDB=off` or `GONOSUMDB`, which taskrun never does. Through the filter, the gate record taints the task before the first forged byte. Skipping the filter takes hand-built `/_cache/` requests, so the task already runs injected code, which is tainted unless a broker omitted that taint (row B-9). The forged code gains nothing that code lacks: every non-read, non-Safe op still needs a signature, and the only unsigned ways out are the hosts in the first two rows. The override lasts one task, and forged code left in the workspace is a dependency change at the next clear (§7). |

The merge also fixed four cross-design interactions (deputy-host erosion, conflicting deny rules, conflicting reason enums, alert feedback through the query call) and four v5.2 text faults: the §5.2/§5.3 restore-epoch contradiction, the snapshot-taint race, the §5.4/§4.2 open-ask contradiction, and the missing `HTTPS_PROXY` in App. C. The ≤5-asks gate was replaced, because the floor alone gives 91 asks per 100 delivery tasks (§15). The delivery lane (round 2) re-grounded three round-1 overclaims — on-box drift detection, TPM-only key protection, and binding only `allow_forking` — to off-box-primary detection with a bound GitHub App IP allow list and a bound fork outcome, and closed the critical review-time Codespaces RCE off-box with a restricted-file-paths push ruleset and no dev container config on any ref. Its revision answered the second red-team's 12 attacks and cut the lane to Enterprise Cloud mirrors. Its real guarantees are GitHub-side rules GitHub enforces against the delivery App's own token, and a floor that trusts only verifier-signed snapshots. The effect (108.7 → 92.0 human-gate actions with three agents at 10 tasks a day each, a once-daily review and every remote an inert Enterprise Cloud mirror, not 78.7 or the lane's claimed ~39.7) is modelled in section 2.9. For CI repos the figure stays 108.7, the planning figure.
```

- The agentbox code: weeks 1–2 (install, generations, release) are untouched. The first v5.3 code lands in week 3 (B's taint-row rule).
- `ARCHITECTURE-v5.1.md` and `PLAN-v5.1.md`: history.
